  5. Owner items that the AI does not decide: the import account credentials and their server setup, the import schedule per server, and who acts as the operator of stage and production. (Amended 2026-10-16, D-074: the owner stated that Infomaniak operates stage and production; the import account credentials are an operator step of the initial setup, explained in `docs/OPERATIONS.md`, section 5, and are no longer an owner item; the import schedule per server stays open.) *(Amended again 2026-10-16, D-075: there is no import account at all; the sentence about the credentials in this decision is void.)*
  6. The application account does not receive the `REFERENCES` privilege (`docs/INSTALLATION.md`, "Database privileges"). Reason: the MariaDB documentation for GRANT marks `REFERENCES` as "Unused" in the column-privilege table (the only table row fetched). Verified on 2026-10-14 (tester, local MariaDB 12.3, application account): `SHOW GRANTS` lists no `REFERENCES`; `manage.py test --noinput` with this account ran 791 tests, result OK. Not verified: the same on MariaDB 10.11 (CI minimum job runs the same suite; check in the next CI run).
* Sources: docs/DECISIONS.md D-033; docs/PROJECT.md "User Functions" and "Supported Devices"; docs/SECURITY.md sections 3 and 7.
* Open points:
  * Step A results (2026-10-14):
  * Terms of use (verified via the project owner's statement, source permitted under AGENTS.md section 3.3; the page https://www.swisstopo.admin.ch/de/nutzungsbedingungen-kostenlose-geodaten-und-geodienste could not be fetched by the tools): free geodata and geodata services may be used, distributed, made accessible, enriched, edited and used commercially. Attribution is mandatory for digital and analogue presentations and publications and for any passing on; one of these texts must be shown: "Bundesamt für Landestopografie swisstopo", "Office fédéral de topographie swisstopo", "Ufficio federale di topografia swisstopo", "Uffizi federal da topografia swisstopo", "Federal Office of Topography swisstopo", "©swisstopo".
  * Licence (decided 2026-10-14 by the owner, who copied the terms text): the STAC collection https://data.geo.admin.ch/api/stac/v0.9/collections/ch.swisstopo.amtliches-gebaeudeadressverzeichnis states "license":"proprietary" and links to https://www.swisstopo.admin.ch/ogd-conditions as "Terms of use". Decision: free use is allowed and the source must be named, with the text "Bundesamt für Landestopografie swisstopo" (owner's copy of the swisstopo terms, 2026-10-14). This matches the general opendata.swiss terms symbol "Open use. Must provide the source." (https://opendata.swiss/en/terms-of-use, fetched 2026-10-14). The STAC label "proprietary" stays a metadata difference and is not used as the legal basis.
  * Dataset facts (verified 2026-10-14 by download into a temporary folder, not stored in the repository): the Swiss CSV asset amtliches-gebaeudeadressverzeichnis_ch_2056.csv.zip is 143,324,478 bytes; its SHA-256 equals the catalogue multihash 1220D85F85AC36115E492F77E9C7FB2D567E1AA0D5EAFF57E2EFEFFBA302DC6F164F (prefix 1220 = SHA-256 multihash); it contains one CSV file of 468,445,908 bytes, UTF-8 with BOM, delimiter ";", 3,304,242 data lines.
  * CSV header (verified): ADR_EGAID;STR_ESID;BDG_EGID;ADR_EDID;STN_LABEL;ADR_NUMBER;BDG_CATEGORY;BDG_NAME;ZIP_LABEL;COM_FOSNR;COM_NAME;COM_CANTON;ADR_STATUS;ADR_OFFICIAL;ADR_MODIFIED;ADR_EASTING;ADR_NORTHING. There are no separate postal code or locality columns: ZIP_LABEL holds "<postal code> <locality>" (example "4052 Basel"); COM_NAME holds the municipality.
  * Unverified: update interval (STAC shows updated 2026-10-14; the schedule is not stated), the meaning of ADR_STATUS values, and whether BDG_NAME or STN_LABEL can contain person names (must be checked before storage in the second database).
  * Consequence for step B: the import reads ZIP_LABEL and COM_NAME and must check the SHA-256 multihash before use, as decided. Storage of the full CSV is not decided; only the columns needed for validation and autocomplete are to be stored (data minimisation, FADP art. 6, to be documented in step B).
  * Verification attempts on 2026-10-14: the terms pages https://www.swisstopo.admin.ch/ogd-conditions and https://www.swisstopo.admin.ch/de/nutzungsbedingungen-kostenlose-geodaten-und-geodienste could not be read by the fetch tool or the browser (connection reset). The STAC metadata still states "license":"proprietary"; the geocat.ch record (uuid 150c38c5-2650-4018-b5c7-939e2cdd56ce) shows distributor swisstopo, update frequency "As needed" and no licence field. The owner confirmed free use with source attribution on 2026-10-14 (see the licence bullet below).
  * Columns chosen for the local copy (confirmed by the owner 2026-10-14, data minimisation): STN_LABEL (street), ADR_NUMBER (house number), ZIP_LABEL (postal code and locality, split at the first space). Not stored: ADR_EGAID, STR_ESID, BDG_EGID, ADR_EDID, BDG_CATEGORY, BDG_NAME (may contain names; not needed), COM_FOSNR, COM_NAME, COM_CANTON (municipality is not used for the postal locality), ADR_STATUS, ADR_OFFICIAL, ADR_MODIFIED, ADR_EASTING, ADR_NORTHING. The locality is the postal locality from ZIP_LABEL, not the municipality (COM_NAME); the reference project https://github.com/schopf16/leg-abrechnung (README, section 7) applies the same rule, which is a hint only and not a legal source.
  * Owner items under Decision 5 remain open (see the amendment of Decision 5, D-074).
  * Update 2026-10-15 (D-066): the terms page could be fetched (version 1.3.2021) and confirms the six source texts and that a source reference is mandatory for digital representations; the owner decided that the software shows no attribution in the user interface and that it belongs to the privacy notice and the imprint (D-022). Whether that place satisfies the terms is an open owner question in D-066 (closed 2026-10-16: the owner settled it, D-074). The earlier statement that the attribution is shown wherever register-based checks are shown is superseded.

See D-050 for the update rule and the open owner question.

## D-050: Update Rule for the Local Address Register

* Date: 2026-10-14
* Status: Decided by the owner on 2026-10-14: option C. Implementation pending (step B2); the security conditions below apply and the security reviewer must review them before use.
* Context: The owner asked for an update once per quarter, or manually in the admin area. swisstopo's geocat.ch record states the update frequency "As needed". The reference project https://github.com/schopf16/leg-abrechnung (README section 7; app/importers/address_register.py) uses an admin button "Jetzt aktualisieren", downloads the file from within the application, shows the data date from swisstopo and marks the register as stale after 92 days.
* Options:
  * A. Admin button in the application starts the download and build (as the reference project). Needs outbound HTTPS from the web server to data.geo.admin.ch and a 140 MB write during a web request or job.
  * B. The operator runs a separate import command once per quarter (own account, see D-049 step B). The admin area shows the data date and a warning after 92 days. The web process makes no external call.
  * C. Option B as the mechanism, plus an admin button that only starts the same command as a background job. Only users with a dedicated permission (D-012) can use it. Outbound HTTPS is needed on the server.
* AI recommendation: B, because it keeps the web process free of external calls (D-033) and needs no new outbound connection. C meets the owner's "manually in the admin area" wish, but only if the owner accepts the outbound connection and the controls below.
* Security implications for A and C: host allowlist (data.geo.admin.ch), SHA-256 check against the catalogue before use, size limit, CSRF protection, permission check, rate limit, no secrets in logs, and no change to the live register until the new one is complete. Not yet reviewed by security-reviewer.
* Verified facts: STAC item updated 2026-10-14; geocat.ch update frequency "As needed"; the reference project uses 92 days as the stale limit (a hint, not a legal source).
* Owner decision (2026-10-14): option C. The quarterly rule is kept as a warning after 92 days (owner requirement "einmal im Quartal oder manuell"). The import runs as a background job started from the admin area by a user with a dedicated permission; the web request itself does not download the file. The operator's own import command (option B) stays available. Outbound HTTPS from the server to data.geo.admin.ch is required.
* Sources: docs/DECISIONS.md D-033, D-049; https://data.geo.admin.ch/api/stac/v0.9/collections/ch.swisstopo.amtliches-gebaeudeadressverzeichnis; https://www.geocat.ch/geonetwork/srv/eng/catalog.search (uuid 150c38c5-2650-4018-b5c7-939e2cdd56ce); https://github.com/schopf16/leg-abrechnung.

## D-051: Phase 1 Coordination and Instance Presentation

* Date: 2026-10-14
* Status: Implementation in progress under the owner's directive to complete phase 1.
* Decision: the main session coordinates bounded development packages, resolves technical questions from verified sources and the existing decisions, and records evidence. This does not remove security reviews, human review of critical changes, operator credentials or deployment approval.
* The existing `normalized_email` column is retained: its nullable value releases a deactivated account's address while its unique index protects all other account states (D-016, D-034, D-035). Removing it would require a replacement for this invariant and a data migration, neither of which is needed to complete phase 1.
* Instance branding is read from environment variables, following the existing configuration mechanism (D-027). Colours and static logo paths are validated at startup. Accent text, links on both surfaces, and optional highlight text must reach 4.5:1 contrast.
* Colour mode is a public presentation preference stored in a host-only, HttpOnly, SameSite=Lax cookie, Secure with the `__Host-` prefix outside local development. A CSRF-protected POST accepts only `auto`, `light` or `dark`; the server renders the selected value. No JavaScript or URL parameter can override branding. `auto` follows the device colour scheme through CSS.
* Validated branding is served as a same-origin stylesheet, not inline CSS; the existing CSP remains unchanged. Logos remain self-hosted static images, never inline SVG or uploads *(uploads are allowed since D-073, under the rules there; the images are still never inline)*. Operator checks of SVG content and accent meaning remain required.
* Sources: owner directive (2026-10-14); D-024, D-025, D-027; docs/DESIGN.md sections 3, 6, 7 and 10; WCAG 2.2 relative luminance and contrast definitions (https://www.w3.org/TR/WCAG22/); Django `HttpResponse.set_cookie` (https://docs.djangoproject.com/en/6.0/ref/request-response/#django.http.HttpResponse.set_cookie); existing instance configuration and installed Tabler CSS colour-scheme/light-dark tokens.
* Verification: targeted branding, input validation, cookie, method, CSRF and redirect tests are added in `leg_platform/tests/test_presentation.py`. Results are recorded only after execution.
* Open: integrated verification and security review; real operator infrastructure, Graph delivery and minimum-version CI are not implied by local tests.
* Owner approval (2026-10-14, explicit follow-up): after successful tests and security review, the temporary local-only administration gate may be replaced by the mandatory second-factor and permission checks. HTTPS, secure cookies and all other controls remain required. The gate is not removed merely because implementation exists.
* Graph logging hardening: the verified MSAL signing-error logger `msal.oauth2cli.assertion` is restricted to CRITICAL without propagation, matching the existing token-response logger (D-048). This closes the previously accepted ERROR-level diagnostic disclosure risk; application failures still surface through the generic Graph error path.
* Dependency metadata check (2026-10-14): the official PyPI version JSON endpoints report an empty `vulnerabilities` array for all 13 pins in `requirements/minimum-constraints.txt` and Django 6.1.2. The Windows `Invoke-RestMethod` requests succeeded; the initial Python HTTPS requests failed with `URLError` and were not treated as evidence. This is a snapshot of PyPI's reported advisories, not a complete vulnerability audit or proof of ongoing support. Transitive dependencies and the complete minimum-version test matrix remain unverified in this work unit.
* Graph test-send limit (implementation assigned): reuse the existing transactional per-address outgoing-mail budget (D-038, 12 per hour and 24 per 24 hours) for the requesting administrator's canonical email address. No new configurable limit or counter is introduced. A refused send gives explicit feedback and must not contact Microsoft. This is a reuse of the existing mail-abuse control, not evidence of delivery or a completed review.
* Failed in-flight baseline: while implementation was changing, the tester's full suite found 791 tests and ended with 26 failures and 187 errors (559.420 seconds). Circular imports and incomplete presentation-route integration were observed. System and migration checks failed during application setup. These results are recorded as failures, not as a stable baseline or acceptance; final verification must run against a stable worktree.
* Local minimum-matrix infrastructure check: the Windows launcher lists Python 3.14 and 3.13, not 3.12. `docker info --format '{{.ServerVersion}}'` failed because the local Docker engine pipe was unavailable. Therefore Python 3.12/MariaDB 10.11 validation cannot be claimed from this machine. No global runtime or container service was installed or started, and no code was published to trigger CI.

## D-052: Address Directory Integration and Attempt Schedule

* Date: 2026-10-14
* Status: Implementation in progress; schedule explicitly decided by the owner.
* *Amended 2026-10-16 (D-075): the three bullets below about the `addresses` SELECT-only connection, the separate writer account and `leg_platform.import_settings`, and about the isolated SQLite test aliases, are void. The directory is an own SQLite file (alias `addresses`) that the web process reads and the worker writes with the same settings; `ImportRequest`, the schedule, the lookup limits and the worker are unchanged; the tests use a temporary SQLite file for the directory. The retention of previous generations is replaced by "current and previous only" (D-075).*
* The owner requires one automatic attempt every 90 days. On failure, log the failure and do not retry automatically before another 90 days. Manual attempts may be repeated after completion or failure; they do not change the independent automatic schedule. Concurrent queued/running requests are coalesced. This replaces the one-hour manual cooldown and the previous open operator scheduling point in D-049/D-050.
* The attempt timestamp is persisted before the automatic download starts, including failed attempts. The long-running isolated worker checks whether an automatic attempt is due. The initial automatic attempt is due when no earlier automatic attempt exists. The data-date warning remains at 92 days (D-050); an import failure can therefore leave a stale but usable directory.
* The web process has only the optional `addresses` SELECT-only connection. `ImportRequest` is in the application database; directory rows and metadata are routed only to the separate public directory. Writer credentials are refused in normal settings. `leg_platform.import_settings` is restricted to import, worker and migration commands and adds a separate writer account for the same address database. No foreign keys connect profile data to it.
* Tests use `leg_platform.test_settings`: the application database remains MariaDB; the address aliases are isolated synthetic SQLite databases, with the reader mirroring the writer. This does not prove MariaDB directory behaviour or real SELECT-only grants. CI is updated to use the same test entry point; actual MariaDB address provisioning remains an operator gate.
* Lookup is a CSRF-protected local POST with bounded fields, a 4 KiB body limit and at most 10 results. It uses the existing transactional request-limit helper and trusted-proxy handling. The baseline is 60 requests per minute per IP and 600 per minute per instance, equivalent average rates to the official Nginx search-endpoint example of 1 request/second per IP and 10 per server. The application uses sliding windows, not Nginx's leaky bucket; these values are not a capacity guarantee. Manual-import requests additionally have short protective limits (5/minute per IP, 20/minute per instance), not an hourly cooldown.
* Live official STAC verification (2026-10-14): item ID `amtliches-gebaeudeadressverzeichnis_ch`, data date 2026-10-14, expected fixed HTTPS asset URL and SHA-256 multihash `1220D85F85AC36115E492F77E9C7FB2D567E1AA0D5EAFF57E2EFEFFBA302DC6F164F` matched the implementation. No real dataset was stored in the repository or imported during this work unit.
* Sources: owner's explicit answers on 2026-10-14; D-033, D-049, D-050; official STAC item https://data.geo.admin.ch/api/stac/v0.9/collections/ch.swisstopo.amtliches-gebaeudeadressverzeichnis/items/amtliches-gebaeudeadressverzeichnis_ch; Django multiple-database documentation https://docs.djangoproject.com/en/6.1/topics/db/multi-db/; Nginx rate-limit examples https://nginx.org/en/docs/http/ngx_http_limit_req_module.html.
* Security review of 2026-10-14 (no critical or high findings; the review ran no tests and did not verify unchanged control files, which the main session checked with `git diff` on `.claude/**`, `CLAUDE.md`, `AGENTS.md` and `CONDUCT.md`: no change):
  * Medium, fixed: the 4 KiB limit of `POST /addresses/lookup/` ran in the view, after the CSRF middleware had parsed the body. `RequestBodyLimitMiddleware` (`leg_platform/middleware.py`) now refuses a larger `Content-Length` with 413 and an invalid or negative one with 400 before sessions, CSRF and authentication; the view check stays as defence in depth. Under WSGI, Django's request reads only as many bytes as `Content-Length` states (`WSGIRequest.__init__`, `LimitedStream`), so the header decides what can be read. Under ASGI Django's handler can receive the whole body before middleware runs (D-048, Django's `DATA_UPLOAD_MAX_MEMORY_SIZE` documentation), so there the middleware limits parsing work, not reception (re-check A-1, Low). A body-size limit of the web server or reverse proxy is therefore mandatory before any stage or production deployment (`docs/INSTALLATION.md`). Other anonymous POST endpoints (login, registration, password reset, `/appearance/`) have no early limit of their own and rely on Django's `DATA_UPLOAD_MAX_MEMORY_SIZE` and the same proxy limit (re-check A-4, informational). Tests: `leg_platform/tests/test_request_body_limit.py` (limits, edge cases of `Content-Length`, methods, order in `MIDDLEWARE`, exact path).
  * Low, fixed: `cleanup_mail_requests` did not delete expired email change links, so their proposed address (`target_email`) stayed in the database. `delete_expired_reset_tokens()` now includes them; tests in `accounts/tests/test_password_reset.py`.
  * Admin gate: the reviewer judged the supporting controls (`SecondFactorMiddleware`, `PortalAdminSite.has_permission()`, per-model permissions) to be present but not sufficient evidence to remove `LocalAdminOnlyMiddleware` before the full suite passes on a stable worktree. The full suite then passed (899 tests, Python 3.14.8, Django 6.1.2, MariaDB 12.3, deprecation warnings as errors, `--settings=leg_platform.test_settings`). On the owner's conditional approval of 2026-10-14, `LocalAdminOnlyMiddleware` was removed. Its former 404 test is replaced by `test_admin_is_protected_but_not_hidden_outside_local_development` (stage and production: anonymous visitors are redirected to the portal login, a staff user without a completed second factor does not get the administration, with the second factor it opens, and group management still needs the permission). The earlier statements in D-004, D-040 and D-041 that the administration is restricted to `APP_ENV=local` are superseded by this entry. Controls that remain: portal login with the abuse protection, the central second-factor check (`SecondFactorMiddleware` and `PortalAdminSite.has_permission()`), forced password change, per-model permissions, CSRF, secure cookies, HTTPS and HSTS outside local development. The administration URL `/admin/` stays predictable; the controls above, not its secrecy, protect it. The removal was re-checked by the `security-reviewer` on 2026-10-14 (read-only; the review ran no tests): no critical, high or medium findings; recommendation "yes" on three conditions: the full suite passes, an operator confirms HTTPS, secure cookies, HSTS and a proxy body limit before any stage or production deployment (A-1), and the tests below. Low findings handled: C-1/C-2, `leg_platform/accounts/tests/test_admin_exposure.py` walks every route of the administration (anonymous, authenticated non-staff, staff without a completed second factor, verified staff without permissions) and checks that no GET changes an account; the environment-name test in `test_admin.py` is rewritten accordingly; C-3, the old wording in D-004/D-017/D-040/D-041 and the installation guide is marked as superseded; C-4, `instance_stylesheet` and `colour_mode` are allowed during a forced password change (`PASSWORD_CHANGE_VIEWS`), so the page is rendered with its branding. Still open: any new admin URL must be wrapped with `admin_site.admin_view()`; the walk test fails otherwise. Not reviewed: the settings per `APP_ENV` in `leg_platform/environment.py` beyond what the tests show, `django_otp` internals, the ASGI handler source, a real deployment.
* Open: integrated tests, security review, real import, directory MariaDB/InnoDB behaviour. (Amended 2026-10-16, D-075: only the current and the previous generation are kept; the SELECT-only grants no longer exist.) A hard-killed worker can leave a running job and requires operator investigation before resetting it; no automatic recovery is claimed.
* Publication: the owner explicitly refused committing/pushing for CI in this work unit. All changes remain local; current minimum/latest CI verification stays open.

## D-053: Startup and Deployment Documents, Local Synthetic Address Directory

* Date: 2026-10-14
* Status: Proposed. The local parts are implemented and covered by database-free tests; the deployment runbook is a proposal, has not been run on a server and needs the owner decisions listed below. A review by the `security-reviewer` and a human review (D-019) are still open.
* *Amended 2026-10-16 (D-075): `leg_platform/local_directory_settings.py` and the two SQLite aliases for a throw-away file are removed; the directory is an own SQLite file in every environment (the alias `addresses`, D-075); `manage.py seed_local_address_directory` now writes the synthetic rows into that file, only with `APP_ENV=local`, and still refuses to replace a directory that is not synthetic. The importer service of the runbook uses `app.env` like the mail worker; there is no `import.env` and no import account. The sentences below about the separate settings, SQLite aliases and importer credentials are void.*
* Context: the main session ran an out-of-repository settings module and a seed script for the design review, and the designer and tester agents needed one document to start, check, use and stop the local environment. The owner also asked for a runbook that lets another AI assistant configure and start the instance on the Infomaniak "VPS Lite" (D-008) safely and repeatably.
* Decision (local):
  * `docs/STARTUP.md` is the single document for the local environment (Windows, PowerShell, `.venv`, local MariaDB 12.3). It states the rules for agents (no printing of the environment, no `.env`, no deletion of the seeded accounts, stop only by process ID), the start of the development server and of the mail worker with logs outside the repository, health checks, test accounts and the console mail, the one-test-run-at-a-time rule (D-046), the test and check commands and a troubleshooting table.
  * The out-of-repository address directory approach is replaced by two small repository parts: `leg_platform/local_directory_settings.py` (refuses to be imported unless `APP_ENV=local`, refuses a file inside the repository or a relative path, adds two SQLite aliases `addresses` and `address_import` for one file, changes no security setting and does not modify the dictionary of the normal settings) and `manage.py seed_local_address_directory` (refuses unless `APP_ENV=local`, refuses unless both aliases are SQLite, refuses to replace a directory whose checksum is not the synthetic marker `synthetic-local-directory`, loads eight invented addresses). The importer, the router, the views and the security settings are unchanged. Tests: `leg_platform/addresses/tests/test_local_directory.py` (15 `SimpleTestCase` tests, no database). The three commands that create and fill the SQLite file were not executed end to end by the author (constraint of that work unit); the first user reports the result.
* Proposal (deployment, `docs/DEPLOYMENT.md`): direct deployment without containers (virtual environment, Gunicorn, nginx, MariaDB 12.3 from the MariaDB Foundation repository, Certbot, systemd units and a timer), because D-015 prefers simple, established, maintained components, the instance needs a terminal for management commands (D-005, D-008) and the supervision is plain systemd. `docs/ARCHITECTURE.md` keeps "container-based or direct server deployment" open until the owner decides.
* Verified facts (official sources, read on 2026-10-14):
  * Infomaniak: the VPS Lite is offered with Linux or Windows images; only official images are installed, without security software such as fail2ban; the firewall is configurable in the Manager; the VPS Lite has no guaranteed SLA and, according to one guide, no snapshots (another guide offers snapshots for the Cloud VPS; contradiction); Infomaniak does not back up a VPS; outgoing port 25 cannot be opened on a VPS Lite; the default user is `ubuntu` or `debian` with key authentication (FAQ 1933, 1973, 2007, 2812, 2822, 1495, 1660 on infomaniak.com). The versions of the Linux images are not stated; which plan is called "VPS Lite 1" is not stated on the product page (smallest plan listed: 1 vCPU, 2 GB RAM, 20 GB disk).
  * MariaDB 12.3 is a long-term-support series (end of life 2029-06-12; release 12.3.3 of 2026-08-22, `downloads.mariadb.org` REST API). The Foundation repository `mirror.mariadb.org/repo/12.3` has suites for Ubuntu jammy, noble, plucky, questing and resolute and for Debian bullseye, bookworm, sid and trixie. The signing key fingerprint is `177F 4010 FE56 CA33 3630 0305 F165 6F24 C74C D1D8` (MariaDB documentation "GPG"); the key file is an armored block. On Linux packages the `root` account uses Unix socket authentication by default (MariaDB documentation "mariadb-secure-installation").
  * Ubuntu 26.04 LTS: standard support until May 2031, `python3` 3.14.3; Ubuntu 24.04 LTS: until June 2029, `python3` 3.12.3; Debian 13: full support until 2028-08-09, LTS until 2030-06-30 (Ubuntu release list, packages.ubuntu.com, debian.org/releases).
  * nginx documentation: `client_max_body_size` (default 1m, error 413), `proxy_request_buffering on` buffers the whole request body, `ssl_reject_handshake` since 1.19.4, `limit_req_status`, `map` with named captures, `add_header` inheritance. Gunicorn documentation: sync workers need a buffering proxy, systemd unit with `Type=notify`, `--forwarded-allow-ips` default `127.0.0.1,::1`, workers "generally 2-4 x cores". systemd manuals: `EnvironmentFile=` is read by the service manager before file system changes, `RuntimeDirectory=` is excluded from `ProtectSystem=strict`, `Persistent=` and `RandomizedDelaySec=` of timers, `systemd-run` options. Mozilla server-side TLS guidelines v6 ("intermediate"). Let's Encrypt: HTTP-01 on port 80, OCSP responders switched off on 2025-08-06. Django 6.1 deployment checklist.
  * Offline check against the installed code (Django 6.1.2, `django-ipware` 8.0.0, `python-ipware` 4.1.1, `axes_client_ip`): behind one reverse proxy on the same host, `AXES_IPWARE_PROXY_COUNT=0` resolves every visitor to `127.0.0.1`, count 1 with a header that holds only the client address resolves to no address (login refused), and count 1 works only with a header of exactly two entries, `<client>, <proxy>`. The runbook therefore has nginx overwrite `X-Forwarded-For` with `"$remote_addr, 127.0.0.1"` (proposal, finding F-1).
  * The importer keeps previous directory generations and inserts all rows in one transaction (`leg_platform/addresses/importer.py`); the first start of the importer service makes the first automatic attempt (`jobs.py`); a hard-killed running job is never taken over (`jobs.py`).
* Not verified: everything that needs a real server (all commands of the runbook, image versions, firewall defaults, the Certbot timer name, `EnvironmentFile` in transient units, systemd hardening with the installed Python, the importer's duration and disk use, chunked request bodies behind nginx). The runbook marks each of these as UNVERIFIED.
* Owner decisions needed (the complete list with options and sources is section 1 of `docs/DEPLOYMENT.md`, O-1 to O-23): release of the deployment gates and approval of each production deployment; stage or production role of the VPS; host name and DNS; operator and SSH keys; operating system image; container or direct deployment; TLS approach; source of the code and the exact commit; Python source; instance name, privacy notice and branding; Microsoft Graph and mail DNS records; address directory (now or later, sharing, importer account, disk); backups (target, retention, encryption, restore tests); incident contact and monitoring; log retention; the client-IP and proxy-count approach; proxy rate limits; the first administrator and the secure channel; update policy; HSTS; IPv6; resources; database transport on loopback. (2026-10-16, D-074: the owner stated that Infomaniak operates stage and production; this list is otherwise unchanged. The importer account is an operator step, not an owner decision.)
* Findings for the main session: F-1 (single reverse proxy and the client IP; a native single-proxy mode would need a security-reviewed change), F-2 (chunked bodies and the 4 KiB limit), F-3 (no hash-pinned installation), F-4 (directory growth and one large transaction on a 20 GB disk), F-5 (nginx error log can keep token URLs), F-6 (a killed importer leaves its job running), F-7 (database transport on loopback), F-8 (backup and snapshot statements of Infomaniak contradict each other).
* Security implications: the runbook never prints or stores secrets outside two root-owned 0600 environment files; the web service never receives the importer credentials; the importer is a separate service with its own settings; the first administrator's password is typed by a human. The local settings module cannot be used outside `APP_ENV=local`. The runbook's hardening and proxy settings are a proposal until reviewed.
* Sources: `docs/INSTALLATION.md`, `docs/OPERATIONS.md`, `docs/SECURITY.md` sections 3, 6, 7, 8 and 9, `docs/ARCHITECTURE.md`, D-002, D-008, D-013, D-015, D-020, D-025, D-027, D-028, D-030, D-037, D-038, D-041, D-046, D-049 to D-052; the vendor documents named above (infomaniak.com FAQ, mariadb.org and mariadb.com documentation, nginx.org, gunicorn.org, freedesktop.org and Debian manpages for systemd, ssl-config.mozilla.org, letsencrypt.org, certbot.eff.org, documentation.ubuntu.com, debian.org, docs.djangoproject.com).

## D-054: Usability Batch A of the Designer Review: Referrer Policy of Form Pages, Error Pages, Alerts, Forms and Wording

* Date: 2026-10-14
* Status: Decided by the main session after the designer's usability review of the phase-1 journeys (report of 2026-10-14, findings R-01 to R-27). Implemented in batch A (everything except the address suggestions, which a later work unit redesigns). Not yet reviewed by the security reviewer; the points that need it are listed under "Security implications".
* Context: the review found that no person could create an account in a real browser, and that the member pages were hard to use for an older person without IT knowledge. The designer's report is input data: every finding below was checked in the code, and the blocker and the visual findings in the browser, before it was changed.
* Decision:
  * **R-01, Referrer Policy.** `Referrer-Policy: no-referrer` stays only on the GET views whose URL contains the token (`TokenPageMixin`: registration, password reset, email change and second-factor reset verification). It is removed from the four views that render a form after the redirect (`RegistrationCompleteView`, `PasswordResetSetView`, `EmailChangeConfirmView`, `TwoFactorResetConfirmView`); their URLs contain no token, and they use the project default `same-origin` (`SECURE_REFERRER_POLICY`). Cause: for a form POST a browser takes the Referrer Policy of the page that contains the form; with `no-referrer` the Fetch standard makes it send `Origin: null`, and Django's `CsrfViewMiddleware` refuses an `Origin` that is not the host of the request. Django's test client sends no `Origin`, which is why the tests did not see it. CSRF protection is unchanged (no trusted origin was added, `null` is still refused). No token and no secret is exposed by the new policy: the form pages have no token in the URL, the policy `same-origin` sends no `Referer` to another origin, and every external link of these pages already has `rel="noopener noreferrer"`. The verify pages keep `no-referrer`, so the token URL itself still reveals nothing. Known remainder (fixed afterwards, see the review in D-055): the invalid-link page that a verify view renders directly (under the token URL) has `no-referrer`; the colour-mode form of the footer and the two logout forms of the header are therefore left out of such pages (`request.no_referrer_page`), because a browser would send `Origin: null` and the CSRF check would refuse them.
  * **R-02, error pages.** `404.html`, `403.html`, `403_csrf.html` and `500.html` extend `error_base.html`, a standalone layout of the register "Clear" (no `base.html`, no session, no database, no context processor needed). They are Swiss German, use only the portal's stylesheets (no inline style or script, so the Content Security Policy is kept), show no technical detail (no exception text, request path, reason or debug note; Django's `permission_denied` passes `exception`, `page_not_found` passes `request_path`, `csrf_failure` passes the reason; none is used) and link to the start page or the login page. Django 6.1.2 loads `403_csrf.html` by name in `django.views.csrf.csrf_failure`; a setting `CSRF_FAILURE_TEMPLATE` does not exist (the review named it by mistake), so nothing is configured. `server_error` renders `500.html` without a request, therefore it uses only static files and fixed paths. The 403 page for a permission error is new text ("Access denied").
  * **R-03, QR code.** The code is shown on a white tile with 24 px padding (about nine modules of quiet zone at the shown size) in light and dark mode, with the caption "Scan with the camera inside your authenticator app." The image is still served by `TwoFactorQrView` as its own SVG; the Content Security Policy is unchanged.
  * **R-06, alerts.** Text in the text token of the register on a tint, a 4 px border and an icon in the colour of the meaning (success and warning use the energy colours "used within the LEG" and "production", docs/DESIGN.md, section 3.4; the information alert follows the accent; the danger alert keeps the error colour as text colour). The icons are Tabler Icons v3.49.0 (`circle-check`, `alert-triangle`, `info-circle`, `alert-circle`, MIT, `static/vendor/tabler-icons/`, checksums in `CHECKSUMS.txt`) used as CSS masks from the own origin, so `img-src 'self'` suffices. `role="status"` on the message list and `role="alert"` on form errors are unchanged. Contrast ratios (WCAG 2.2, relative luminance formula; measured in the browser with computed colours, the same values as the script) are in the table below.
  * **R-07, check marks.** Tabler draws the tick of checkboxes and radio buttons with a mask whose image is a `data:` URL, which `img-src 'self'` blocks (checked in Tabler 1.6.1's stylesheet). `static/img/check.svg` and `static/img/radio-dot.svg` replace it; the colour of the mark is the text colour of the accent (`--tblr-form-check-icon-color`), so it follows the instance colours in both modes. `data:` is not added to any directive.
  * **R-14, size.** Members: page titles 28 px, labels, inputs, buttons and select fields 16 px, hints and error texts 15 px, controls at least 48 px high (measured). The administration (Django admin) is not changed.
  * **R-15, logout.** From 768 px the top bar shows "Profile" and a visible "Log out" button (the existing logout form with its CSRF token and POST); the menu is for small screens. A person who must change the password first sees the logout button too. After logout the login page says "You have been logged out."
  * **R-16, input types.** Login: `type="email"`, `inputmode="email"`, `autocapitalize="none"`, `spellcheck="false"`, form still `novalidate`. Post office box: `type="text"`, `inputmode="numeric"`, validated on the server as before. Postal code: `inputmode="numeric"` for Switzerland, `text` for other countries (rendered by the server for the country of the form and followed by `postal-code-input.js`), because foreign postal codes contain letters. Setup code: `inputmode="numeric"` and `autocomplete="one-time-code"` only on the first code of the setup (six digits); the field of the second login step and of the forms that ask for the code again also accept emergency codes and stay unrestricted. No `maxlength="6"`: the check removes spaces and hyphens (`totp.normalize_code`), and a pasted "123 456" would be cut.
  * **R-08, forms.** One shared markup (`_field.html`, `_profile_fields.html`, `_error_summary.html`): optional fields are marked "(optional)" and the sentence "Fields marked optional may stay empty. All other fields are required." replaces the marking of required fields (the house number is marked optional too, because the server does not require it). The HTML `required` attribute is set on the town, the postal code and the country (always required) and on the password fields, not on the names and the street (required only under a condition that the text states: first and last name unless a company name is given; street unless a post office box is given). Fields are grouped with `fieldset` and `legend`; street and house number and postal code and town are side by side from 576 px; the post office box follows the country with the hint "Only if you have no street address."; the include of the address suggestions (`addresses/widget.html`, contract unchanged) sits directly after the street row. An invalid form starts with an error summary (`alert-danger`, `role="alert"`, `tabindex="-1"`, `autofocus`, links to the fields); no script is needed. The advice for the password replaces Django's list of validator rules (the minimum length is read from the configured validator); the rules are still enforced and every violation is reported at the field. A "Show password" button (external script, hidden until the script enables it, so the fields are plain password fields without JavaScript) is available on every password field, including the login, the password change, the reset and the forms that ask for the password again. Empty required fields get specific messages; "This field is required." is translated "Please fill in this field." as fallback.
  * **R-09, Option A only.** *Superseded by D-055 (owner decision of 2026-10-14, 17:48): the person is logged in after the completion and sees a welcome page; this bullet describes the first state.* The success message after the registration mentions the extra protection; the first profile view of an account without a second factor shows a dismissible card ("Protect your account even better?", "Set up now", "Not now"); "Not now" is a POST that sets a session flag, nothing is stored in the database, and the rules and the login flow are unchanged. Option B (log the person in after the completion) was not implemented in this entry; the owner approved it in D-055. The prefill of the login address after the registration is obsolete with it.
  * **R-10, two-factor pages.** Plain wording ("Extra protection when logging in" with the term "two-factor authentication" next to it), a native `details` element "What is an authenticator app?", one ordered list for the steps with the manual key in a `details` element, a step indicator (docs/DESIGN.md, section 6; reduced to two steps by D-055), a wrong-code message that explains the 30-second rotation, the setup explains that it is voluntary or, for staff users and system administrators, that it is mandatory for the role (and has no "Cancel" link then). "Recovery codes" are called "emergency codes" ("Notfallcodes") in templates, messages, emails, the labels of editable email texts and of the security log; stored keys, code identifiers and the log messages stay English (`recovery_code_used` etc.), and the alphabet and length of the codes are not changed. A migration (`0014`) records the changed labels of choices (no SQL). Administrator-edited `EmailText` rows are data and keep their text. The page of the codes shows them larger in an ordered list, has one button "I have saved the codes - continue", and "Print this page" and "Copy codes" buttons that need `recovery-codes.js` (hidden without it; the codes are only read from the page, never stored, logged or sent; the clipboard is used only in a secure context). The overview counts the emergency codes with a text for zero, the "Turn off" button is the outlined error style, and the wording is "turned off" everywhere. After a login with an emergency code the person sees "You used an emergency code. N codes are left. You can create new codes in your profile." (the person has just proved to own the code; nothing about the central check changed).
  * **R-11, pages after a request.** The registration, password reset and email change pages say what to expect in plain words (a message within minutes, look in the spam folder, the link is valid for 24 hours, what to do if nothing arrives). The text stays conditional ("If we can use the address you entered, we have sent a message to it") because for an unknown, blocked or rate-limited address no message is sent; the pages are identical for every address (a test compares them).
  * **R-12, deletion.** *Superseded by D-055: one text and one message for every account, no retention wording, button "Delete account".* First state: the consequences are itemised; the checkbox label is on the right of the box; the box is never shown ticked again after an error; the required message is specific; the button is "Delete account permanently"; the result messages told the two outcomes apart. The right-hand checkbox, the unticked box after an error and the specific required message remain.
  * **R-13, emails.** The three emails with a personal link that had no contact line (registration, password reset, removal of the second factor) end with "Questions or problems: <website>" where `INSTANCE_WEBSITE_URL` is configured (the existing placeholder `{contact_url}`, no change of the validation of D-039). The notification emails already contained the contact line. Greeting by name is not implemented: the senders only receive the address, so it needs a new placeholder and a change of every sender and of the mail queue; it is left to a decision.
  * **R-18, phone number.** Swiss numbers are shown in the national format ("031 123 45 67"), other numbers in the international format, on the profile and in the edit form; storage stays E.164 (`phonenumbers`, `PhoneNumber.as_national` and `as_international`).
  * **R-19, R-20.** The invalid-link pages say "invalid, expired or was already used" and offer two buttons (log in; register again or request a new link). The lockout page names the real duration from `LOGIN_COOLOFF` (not a copy of the value) and links to the password reset.
* Contrast ratios of the alerts (default accent; computed with the WCAG 2.2 formula by script and measured with the computed colours in Chromium, identical):

  | Alert | Mode | Text on tint | Border and icon on tint | Border and icon on surface |
  | ----- | ---- | ------------ | ----------------------- | -------------------------- |
  | Success (`#E8F3EC`, border `#2F8F5B`) | light | 15.38 (`#1C1917`) | 3.55 | 4.04 |
  | Warning (`#FDF3E0`, border `#B7791F`) | light | 15.88 (`#1C1917`) | 3.31 | 3.64 |
  | Information (accent 12 % on white, `#E6EDEA`, border `#2F6B4F`) | light | 14.71 (`#1C1917`) | 5.29 | 6.29 |
  | Information, Ittigen accent (`#E0EBF4`, border `#005BA2`) | light | 14.46 (`#1C1917`) | 5.75 | 6.96 |
  | Danger (`#FBECEA`, border and text `#B42318`) | light | 5.73 | 5.73 | 6.57 |
  | Success (`#1A2B21`, border `#5DBB86`) | dark | 13.64 (`#F5F5F4`) | 6.32 | 7.43 |
  | Warning (`#2E2512`, border `#E8B34A`) | dark | 13.85 (`#F5F5F4`) | 7.90 | 9.14 |
  | Information (accent 14 % on `#1C1917`, `#283129`, border `#74C59A`) | dark | 12.33 (`#F5F5F4`) | 6.53 | 8.49 |
  | Information, Ittigen accent (`#272D33`, border `#6AA9E0`) | dark | 12.75 (`#F5F5F4`) | 5.54 | 6.97 |
  | Danger (`#33201E`, border and text `#F0948A`) | dark | 6.81 | 6.81 | 7.75 |

  Required: 4.5:1 for text (success criterion 1.4.3) and 3:1 for the icon and the border (1.4.11). The test `leg_platform/tests/test_design_assets.py` recomputes these from the stylesheet.
* Security implications (for the security reviewer):
  * The change of the Referrer Policy and its reasoning (R-01): the policy of the four form pages, the unchanged CSRF check, that a POST with `Origin: null` is still refused, and the known remainder (colour-mode form on an invalid-link page under a token URL).
  * Error pages (R-02): no information in the 403, 404 and 500 pages; the 500 page renders without request, database and context processor; the Content Security Policy is on every error response.
  * New external scripts (`password-toggle.js`, `recovery-codes.js`, `postal-code-input.js`): no inline code, no network, no storage; the emergency codes are read from the page for print and copy only. The "Show password" button turns a password field into text while it is shown.
  * The message after a login with an emergency code (R-10) and the dismissal of the tip (R-09, session only, POST with CSRF, login required).
  * Disclosure of the lock duration on the lockout page (R-20): the value is already public in `docs/SECURITY.md`.
  * Emails (R-13): a plain website line from the instance configuration where set; no link that confirms or undoes anything; the notifications still invite no reply; emergency wording in the editable defaults.
  * The result messages of the account deletion and the wording of the retention variant (R-12): replaced by D-055.
* Decisions of the project owner on 2026-10-14: see D-055 (auto-login after the registration, two-factor setup without the password within five minutes, uniform deletion wording, relabelled status). They change the points R-09, R-10 (steps) and R-12 above.
* Verified facts (2026-10-14):
  * Fetch standard (https://fetch.spec.whatwg.org/, `fetch.bs` of branch `main`, "append a request `Origin` header"): for a request whose method is not GET or HEAD and whose mode is not "cors", `no-referrer` sets the serialized origin to `null`; `same-origin` keeps the origin for a same-origin request.
  * Django 6.1.2 `django/middleware/csrf.py` (`_origin_verified`: the `Origin` must be the origin of the request or a trusted origin), `django/views/csrf.py` (`csrf_failure` loads `403_csrf.html`), `django/views/defaults.py` (`page_not_found`, `permission_denied`, `server_error`: contexts and `500.html` rendered without a request).
  * Chromium (integrated browser, 127.0.0.1): a form on a page with the policy `no-referrer` (a `meta` tag added to the profile form for the test) sent `Origin: null` and got the new 403 page; the same form on a normal page sent `Origin: http://127.0.0.1:8001` and was saved. The registration completion form was submitted in the browser and succeeded. No console error or Content Security Policy violation on the member pages; the mask icons and check marks load under `img-src 'self'`. Computed sizes: labels and inputs 16 px, hints 15 px, title 28 px, controls 48 px.
  * Tabler 1.6.1 stylesheet: `.form-check-input::before` uses `mask-image` with a `data:` URL; `.alert` has no icon and mixes its colours; `.btn-link` and the other buttons animate their colour.
  * WCAG 2.2 relative luminance and contrast ratio (https://www.w3.org/TR/WCAG22/).
  * Tabler Icons v3.49.0, `icons/outline/*.svg` (https://github.com/tabler/tabler-icons, MIT).
  * `phonenumbers` and `django-phonenumber-field` 8.5.0: `PhoneNumber.as_national`, `as_international`, `phonenumbers.region_code_for_number`.
* Tests (2026-10-14): `leg_platform/accounts/tests/test_browser_origin.py` (forms accepted with the `Origin` of a browser, `null` refused, policy of the form and token pages), `test_usability.py`, `leg_platform/tests/test_error_pages.py`, `test_design_assets.py`, and the changed tests of `test_account_lifecycle.py`, `test_password_reset.py`, `test_registration.py`, `test_second_factor.py` and `test_admin.py`. The result of the complete suite is in the report of the work unit.
* Open: Option B of R-09 and the prefill of the login address (settled by D-055); the owner's legal check of the retention wording (R-12; settled by D-055, no wording is shown); greeting by name in emails (R-13); the address suggestions and the two address findings (R-04, R-05, R-21) in the next work unit; the alphabet of the emergency codes (unchanged); the proposed changes of `docs/DESIGN.md` (white QR tile, alert colours and contrast table, check marks, 16 px rule for controls and messages, logout in the top bar, step indicator, error summary), which the designer or the owner applies; the choice-only migration `0014` is not applied to the local development database by this work unit.
* Sources: the designer's report (2026-10-14); the documents and installed versions named under "Verified facts"; `docs/SECURITY.md`, sections 3, 6, 10 and 11; D-016, D-024, D-025, D-037, D-038, D-039, D-040, D-052.
* Decisions of the project owner on 2026-10-14 (statements of 17:48 and 17:51, binding): they replace scope items 9 to 12 of this batch where D-055 says so (registration login and welcome page, simplified setup, deletion wording). The designer batch otherwise stands as decided by the main session. Sources: the owner's statements of 2026-10-14; D-055; `docs/SECURITY.md`.

## D-055: Owner Decisions of 2026-10-14: Login After the Registration, Two-Factor Setup Without the Password Within Five Minutes, Uniform Deletion

* Date: 2026-10-14
* Status: Decided by the project owner (statements of 2026-10-14, 17:48 and 17:51). Implemented together with D-054. Not yet reviewed by the security reviewer; the points that need it are listed under "Security implications". Amends D-016 (labels only), the rule on automatic login in D-016 and D-037 (registration), and the re-authentication rule of `docs/SECURITY.md`, section 3 (two-factor setup only).
* Context: two-factor authentication must be as simple as possible so that people actually use it. The designer's review (D-054) showed that registration, the first login and the setup together are long, and that the deletion page tells people about legal retention that they cannot influence.
* Decision:
  * **A1. Login after the completion of the registration.** When the completion form is saved, `RegistrationCompleteView` calls Django's `login()` with `second_factor.LOGIN_BACKEND`, renews the session ID (done by `login()`), and redirects to the new welcome page `/welcome/` ("Your account is ready"). The person has just proved control of the mailbox with the single-use link and has chosen the password. The account has no privileges and no groups; the customer number is issued once as before. `login()` with an explicit backend does not call `authenticate()`, so Axes records no failed attempt and no lockout; the session timeouts are started by the existing `user_logged_in` receiver (`session_timeout.start_session`); the session hash follows the new password. A used, expired or replaced link logs nobody in (the token is consumed in the same transaction, unchanged). The success message "You can now log in" and the retyping of the email on the login page are gone.
  * **A2. Welcome page and setup.** The welcome page needs a login, shows the customer number and one recommended step: the primary button "Set up extra protection now (about 2 minutes)" (a POST form to the setup view; no state-changing GET), the secondary link "Later" (to the profile), two short sentences why and the native `details` "What is an authenticator app?". Accounts that already have a second factor are redirected to the profile. "Later" leads to the profile, where the dismissible card of D-054 reminds the person (dismissal in the session only); the same card is shown on the first profile view of any account without a second factor. The setup has two screens after the button: (1) one page with the QR code on its white tile, the numbered steps, the manual key in a `details` element and the code field (`inputmode="numeric"`, `autocomplete="one-time-code"`); (2) the page of the emergency codes with one button "I have saved the codes - continue", "Print this page" and "Copy codes". The step indicator says "Step 1 of 2" and "Step 2 of 2". All server-side checks stay: the device is confirmed only after a valid code; the emergency codes are issued at the confirmation; notification email, security log entry, ending of the other sessions and keeping the current one (D-040, D-044); the alphabet and length of the codes are unchanged.
  * **A3. Re-authentication for setting up two-factor authentication.** The exact rule: `second_factor.recently_authenticated(request, max_age=timedelta(minutes=5))` is true only if the session holds a server-side timestamp (`authenticated_at`, seconds since the epoch of the server clock) that is a number (not a boolean), not in the future and at most five minutes old. The timestamp is written only by the `user_logged_in` receiver `remember_authentication`, that is by `login()`: after the password (and after the second factor, if the account has one, because `login()` is only called then) and when the registration is completed. It is not written by activity, by the cycling of the session key, by a password change or a password reset, and never from client input. It ends with the session (logout and flush). The setup view asks for the current password only if the session is not recent; the unbound password prompt is shown without an error if the person pressed the button of the welcome page and the session has meanwhile become older. Removing the second factor, creating new emergency codes, changing the email address and deleting the account still always need the current password (and the code where one is set up). Staff users and system administrators, who must set up the second factor, benefit from the window after their login in the same way.
  * **A4.** The change of the wording is in the templates and the catalogue; the recovery-code login message and the other items of D-054 are unchanged.
  * **B1. Deletion.** The deletion page and the result message are identical for every account: "Your account will be deleted. You will no longer be able to log in. This cannot be undone. You can register again later with the same email address."; checkbox "I understand that my account will be deleted."; button "Delete account"; message "Your account has been deleted." The retention branch, its texts and its context are removed from the view, the template and the catalogue. No artificial delay is added.
  * **B2. Behaviour unchanged, display changed.** Without a retention obligation the account and its data are erased; with one the account is kept in the existing deactivated state (the stored key stays `deactivated`, D-016), cannot log in, cannot reset its password, and its email address is released. In the administration the status is displayed as "Deleted (retention obligation)" (model choices, list, detail, filters, security log label "Account deleted (retention obligation)"); the list shows the retention flag as a column and a filter; the action "Reactivate account" is labelled "Restore account" (conditions of D-016 unchanged). Clarification of D-016: "deactivated" is the technical state, the display text for people is "Deleted (retention obligation)"; the history of D-016 is not rewritten. `docs/PROJECT.md`, "Account States", is adapted.
  * **Decision of the project owner:** the application tells the person only that the account is deleted and that logging in is no longer possible; data subject requests are answered by the owner on request. The legal assessment of this wording (DSG Art. 6, 19, 25) was not verified by the AI and is the owner's responsibility.
* Decisions of the project owner on 2026-10-14: A (auto-login, welcome page, simplest setup flow, re-authentication window of five minutes for the setup only) and B (uniform deletion, relabelled status) as stated above; at 17:51 the owner added that no retention or privacy wording is wanted in the application.
* Verified facts (2026-10-14):
  * Django 6.1.2 `django.contrib.auth.login()` (source read): the session key is cycled (or the session flushed if another user was logged in), the backend is stored without `authenticate()`, `rotate_token` renews the CSRF token, `user_logged_in` is sent.
  * Code of this repository: `login()` creates no Axes failure; `session_timeout.start_session` writes the session times on `user_logged_in`; the login view calls `login()` only after the second factor is complete (the test `test_with_a_second_factor_the_window_starts_after_the_code_not_after_the_password`).
  * OWASP Authentication Cheat Sheet (https://cheatsheetseries.owasp.org/cheatsheets/Authentication_Cheat_Sheet.html, fetched 2026-10-14): "Require Re-authentication for Sensitive Features" asks for the current credentials before updating sensitive account information such as the password or the email address, and "Re-authentication After Risk Events" lists "Minimize User Friction" and context-aware decisions. It names no time window and does not name the setup of an additional second factor as one of the sensitive operations. A recency window of five minutes is therefore the owner's decision, not an OWASP rule. The OWASP Session Management Cheat Sheet was not fetched: not verified.
* Rationale: a person who has just logged in with the password (or just completed the registration) has proved the knowledge of the password minutes ago; asking again at the moment when we want them to protect their account lowers the use of the protection. Auto-login after the single-use link removes a step where people give up.
* Security implications (for the security reviewer):
  * Risk of A3: a hijacked fresh session could add its own authenticator within five minutes. Mitigations: the window of five minutes (server clock, not client-controlled, not extended); the setup still needs a valid code of an app that the attacker would have to control (D-040); the notification email to the account's own address; the security log entry; other sessions are ended at the confirmation; removal, new codes, email change and deletion still need the password;   a session that is not recent is asked for the password.
  * Risk of A1: the login after the completion makes a stolen verification link a login (it already gave the right to set the first password and was single-use; the link is consumed in the same transaction; nothing changes for a replaced or expired link). No privileges are granted; the session starts as an ordinary member session with the usual timeouts.
  * The welcome page and the setup start need a login; the setup start is a POST with a CSRF token; opening any page changes nothing.
  * B1: the page and the message are identical for every account, so the response does not tell whether a retention obligation exists. The code path still differs (a retained account is kept; no artificial delay is added).
* Alternatives: keep the password prompt for every setup (rejected by the owner: too much friction); a longer window (not chosen: the owner stated five minutes); a different message for retained accounts (rejected by the owner).
* Sources: the owner's statements of 2026-10-14; `docs/SECURITY.md`, sections 3 and 11 (updated); `docs/PROJECT.md` (updated); D-016, D-037, D-040, D-044, D-054; Django 6.1.2 source of `django.contrib.auth`; the OWASP page named above.
* Tests (2026-10-14): `leg_platform/accounts/tests/test_recent_authentication.py` (window rule, fresh and aged sessions, no extension by activity or password change, not client-controlled, ends with logout, removal/new codes/email change/deletion still need the password, staff, CSRF and login, registration completion logs in, no privileges, used and replaced link, no failed attempt, welcome page); `test_account_lifecycle.py` (uniform deletion page and message, admin display); `test_usability.py`, `test_browser_origin.py`, `test_portal_views.py` (changed texts and flows).
* Consequences: `docs/DESIGN.md` should state the two-step indicator (proposal in the report of the work unit).
* Security review of 2026-10-14 (read-only; ran no tests; no Critical, High or Medium findings; nothing found that weakens CSRF, Origin or Host handling):
  * Finding 1 (Low), fixed: an invalid-link page under a token URL has `no-referrer`; the colour-mode form was already left out, now the two logout forms of the header are as well (`request.no_referrer_page`, `templates/base.html`). A redirect of invalid tokens to a fixed non-token URL would remove the whole class; not done (**done in D-067**, `request.no_referrer_page` removed). Tests: `accounts/tests/test_token_page_forms.py`, `tests/test_colour_mode_form.py`.
  * Finding 2 (Low), accepted and documented: within five minutes after a login a person at an unattended, logged-in browser can start the setup without the password and confirm it with their own app. The real consequence for the owner of the account is a lock-out: a password reset never removes the second factor (`docs/SECURITY.md`, section 3), so only an administrator-started removal (D-004, D-041) or the emergency command helps; the attacker, who has no password, keeps access only as long as that session lives (8 hours absolute for members, 4 hours for staff). The notification email about the setup tells the person to contact the operator (D-038).
  * Finding 3 (Low), accepted: a started setup (pending device) is valid for 15 minutes (`ENROLMENT_LIFETIME`) and the confirm page can be opened again within that time; a session that started it in the window can finish it later. This was the case before; the start is now possible without the password inside the window.
  * Finding 4 (Low), accepted: "Copy codes" puts the emergency codes on the system clipboard, where clipboard history or sync may keep them; the script only reads the page, with no network and no storage. The person can use "Print" instead.
  * Finding 5 (Low), partly fixed: new negative tests (`test_token_page_forms.py`: invalid-link pages have neither logout nor colour-mode form, `no-store` on the welcome and two-factor pages). Open: assert in the concurrency test of the registration completion that exactly one client holds `_auth_user_id`, and a cross-account test of `authenticated_at`.
  * Info: deletion paths differ inherently in timing (no delay added, owner agrees); the lockout page shows the public cool-off of 15 minutes; a clock jump gives a negative age, which fails closed; the message after an emergency-code login shows a number the person can already see after login.
  * Not reviewed: files outside those read, behaviour in real browsers, the `CHECKSUMS.txt` hashes, dependency versions, NIST SP 800-63B on enrolling an additional factor, the legal side of the deletion wording.

## D-056: Design Package of the Project Owner (2026-10-14), Phase 1: Foundation and Login

* Date: 2026-10-14
* Status: Order of the project owner. The design package `LEG-Design-Vorgaben/` (folders `login`, `register`, `navigation`, `profil`, `legal`, `logo`, `app`; images, `VORGABEN.md`, `referenz/*.html`) is the visual and functional target of the homepage and takes precedence over `docs/DESIGN.md` and earlier decisions where they differ (this decision, the changed `docs/DESIGN.md` and `docs/SERVICES.md` record the changes). The decisions of the team lead (`K1` to `K22`) fill the points that the package leaves open. Phase 1 covers the foundation, the corner buttons, the entry shell and the login. Not yet reviewed by the security reviewer; the points for the review are listed below.
* Priority of the sources: images (PNG) over the text of `VORGABEN.md`, over the reference HTML, over older project documents (team lead decision). Exception: K7 (registration, step 4b: the e-mail address as plain text).
* Decision:
  * **Tokens (login/VORGABEN.md, section 6).** `--bg`, `--panel`, `--fg`, `--muted`, `--line`, `--accent`, `--on-accent`, `--yellow`, `--bad`, `--ok`, `--warm`, `--logo-plate` in `portal.css` (light on `:root`, dark in `:root[data-theme="dark"]`), plus the new status token `--warn` for "open" and "not set up" (K16: `#8A5A00` light, `#E8B34A` dark; text on the panel 5.9:1 and 8.9:1). The variables of Tabler (`--tblr-primary`, `--tblr-body-bg`, ...) are mapped onto the tokens. The platform defaults of `branding.py` are now the values of the package: accent `#005BA2` / `#6AA9E0`, text on it `#FFFFFF` / `#0B1520`, highlight `#DDDD22` (which is `--yellow`). **This replaces the rule in `docs/DESIGN.md` 3.3 that the values of the Ittigen instance are not defaults in code (D-013, D-014)**: the owner ordered them as defaults; an instance can still replace every value, and the contrast check now uses the new surfaces (`#FFFFFF`, `#F6F7F8`; `#191C1F`, `#111315`). Spacing 4, 8, 12, 16, 24, 32, 48 px; radius 8 px for buttons and fields, 12 px for cards and tiles, 6 px for the logo plate; no shadows and no gradients except the one soft shadow of the floating language menu (as in the reference of the owner); focus: 2 px accent outline with 2 px offset; a field in focus has a 1.5 px accent border and a 3 px ring of 30 % accent. Allowed font sizes (K6): 11, 12, 13 (micro labels only), 14, 16, 18, 20, 22, 24, 26, 32, 40 px.
  * **Light and dark only by the button (R13 changed, login/VORGABEN.md 5.2 and 12).** No `prefers-color-scheme`, no "Auto", no `color-scheme: light dark`, no `light-dark()`. The server always renders light (`data-theme="light" data-bs-theme="light"`); `static/js/theme-init.js` (in the `<head>`, not deferred) applies the choice of the tab, and the attribute `data-bs-theme` has always the same value, because Tabler styles its components with it. The cookie `colour_mode`, the view `/appearance/`, the template `_colour_mode.html` and the footer select are removed. The theme script reads only the allowlisted values `light` and `dark` and nothing from the URL (unchanged principle of `docs/DESIGN.md`, section 7).
  * **K3: persistence of the choice, the one deviation from the package.** The package says that nothing is stored and every new page load starts light and German. In an application of several pages that would reset the choice at every click. The choice (theme and language) is therefore kept in the `sessionStorage` of the tab: no cookie, nothing is sent to the server, a new tab starts light and German. A reload in the same tab keeps the choice (the acceptance test of the package, step 3, expects light and German after a reload; the deviation is intentional and the owner is informed).
  * **Corner buttons (WL-03).** `_corner_controls.html` and `static/js/corner-controls.js` on every page (also the error pages and the signed-in pages): a round 40 px language button "DE" with a menu (Deutsch, Francais, Italiano, English with flags 20 x 14 px, files `static/img/flags/*.svg` because the Content Security Policy allows no `data:` images), opening on hover, on focus and on a tap, with an invisible bridge, `aria-haspopup`, `aria-expanded` that follows the menu, Escape and the arrow keys; and a round theme button (sun in light, moon in dark; `aria-label` names the mode and the action). Without JavaScript both buttons are hidden and the page stays light and German. The flags of France and Italy are drawn correctly as vertical tricolours (the images of the package show them black: an error of the images). Translations exist for the login page only (a static dictionary `static/js/login-texts.js`, German as the source; French, Italian and English are the texts of the package, plus three short texts for the labels of the buttons that are not in the package); every other page stays German, and there the language button only remembers the choice. The German texts of the dictionary are checked against the Swiss German catalogue by a test.
  * **Logos (WL-07).** The two files of `LEG-Design-Vorgaben/logo/` are used unchanged as images (`<img>`, never inline), outside the repository in the instance static directory (`INSTANCE_STATIC_DIRECTORY`, `INSTANCE_LOGO_LIGHT`, `INSTANCE_LOGO_DARK`; `docs/STARTUP.md`, sections 4 and 6). They were checked for scripts, event handlers and external references (none). Light mode: positive file on a transparent background; dark mode: negative file on a plate (`--logo-plate`, radius 6 px, padding 4 px 8 px); 360 px wide on the desktop, 40 px high in the top bar of the phone. The instance name shown to people is "LEG-Portal" (set in the local instance configuration; the code default stays "LEG Platform" because it is neutral).
  * **Entry shell (WL-08).** `entry_base.html` is the common layout of every page without a session: login, registration (layout only; the three steps follow in a later phase), password reset (four pages), the second step of the login and the lockout page. Two equal columns above 760 px (the warm panel with the logo, the title, the lead and two points; the form with a width of at most 400 px), on the phone a top bar of at least 64 px with the logo and no text. The panel texts per page type: the login texts (also password reset and second factor), and the registration texts "Konto erstellen" with the lead and the two points of the images (K8). The footer row "Datenschutz | Impressum | host of the website" is shown on every entry page (the registration package says it follows the login package).
  * **Login (WL-04, WL-06).** Exactly as in `login/VORGABEN.md` and the images (desktop 1440, phone 390 without scrolling, light and dark; verified with screenshots, see the report). The password field has a small eye icon inside the field (K20, a deviation from the package: the "show password" function is kept for older people; it is hidden without JavaScript).
  * **Stay signed in (WL-05, K21).** The checkbox "Angemeldet bleiben" is off by default. If it is ticked, a user gets a persistent session of `STAY_SIGNED_IN_DURATION` (14 days, `settings.py`): the cookie and the server-side session expire after this time, and the idle and the absolute timeout of `session_timeout` are both this long, so the duration does not slide. It is **ignored, not hidden, for staff users and system administrators**: their sessions keep the timeouts of D-041 (15 minutes idle, 4 hours). The wish of the form is put on the request object by the login view before `login()` (never into the session) and turned into the persistent session by the receiver `session_timeout.start_session`, which checks the account (`requires_second_factor`) and otherwise resets the expiry to the default (`set_expiry(None)`, so that a second login in the same session without the box does not keep the long expiry); with a second factor the wish is stored in the dict of the pending login (`second_factor.begin_pending_login(..., stay=...)`) and counts only after the code, so an abandoned second step leaves nothing behind. Nothing comes from the client after the form. The expiry is set with a `timedelta`: Django then stores an **absolute** end time, while an integer would mean "that many seconds of inactivity" and move the end at every save (verified in `django/contrib/sessions/backends/base.py`, `set_expiry` and `get_expiry_age`, Django 6.1.2); so the 14 days do not slide, the cookie is sent again with the remaining time, and the server check uses the same duration for the idle and the absolute timeout (review findings L1 to L3 of phase 1). The value of 14 days is a proposal of the team lead; **the security review may change it** (OWASP Session Management Cheat Sheet names no value for persistent sessions of low-risk applications; this was not verified further). A user who ticks the box accepts a longer-lived cookie on that device.
  * **Portal settings (WL-22, WL-21, K9).** New singleton model `PortalSettings` (administration "Portal-Einstellungen"; only system administrators, no adding or deleting; changes are in the history of the administration): the address of the privacy notice and the address of the legal notice, only `https://` addresses without credentials, white space or control characters (`validate_https_address`; empty is allowed). The texts themselves are maintained on the website of the owner; the portal has no pages for them. The footer links open in a new tab (`target="_blank" rel="noopener noreferrer"`); an item without an address is plain text. The privacy address falls back to `INSTANCE_PRIVACY_URL`. The lookup (`accounts/portal_settings.py`) reads the database at most once per request and fails safe: on a database error the privacy address comes from the instance configuration and the legal notice has no link, so that the error pages work. The placeholder `{privacy_url}` of the email texts uses the same address. **Audit (review finding E2):** every change of an address is a security event `portal_settings_changed` (kept for the long period, like the Graph configuration), and the log line names only the host names of the old and the new address (public data, no paths or queries); the full addresses are in the history of the administration. An address with an unclosed bracket (`https://[::1`), a control character or DEL is refused by the validation, not by an exception (finding L4). The third item shows only the host of `INSTANCE_WEBSITE_URL` (a link to the website in a new tab).
  * **Texts.** English source texts with the Swiss German translation in `locale/de_CH`; the texts of the images are taken exactly. The old texts that are no longer used (colour mode, device setting, "Welcome to %(instance_name)s", "To the website") are removed from the catalogue. The LEG-specific texts of the package ("Willkommen im Portal der LEG", ...) are in the catalogue and in the dictionary, as the owner prescribes them; a second instance changes them in its own catalogue.
  * **Deviations from the images that are kept on purpose (design acceptance of phase 1):** the heading "Anmelden" (and the heading of every entry page) is the `<h1>` of the page and the title of the warm panel is a `<p>` (the reference uses an `h1` in the panel and an `h2` for the form); the look is the same, and a screen reader finds the main heading first. On a page without translations the language button shows DE and the menu only remembers the choice (the page text stays German, so the button does not claim another language). Tabler's resting shadows and its alternate glyph settings are switched off (rule R7, the typeface of the package).
  * **Proposals that the owner may change** (the package leaves them open; team lead decision): the registration panel texts for the lead on the password step, the labels of the corner buttons in French, Italian and English, the 14 days of "stay signed in".
* Sources: `LEG-Design-Vorgaben/login/VORGABEN.md` (sections 2 to 12), `login/referenz/index.html`, `login/bilder/*.png`, `register/VORGABEN.md`, `legal/VORGABEN.md` (section 8), `logo/`; team lead decisions (`lead-decisions.md`, K1 to K22); Django 6.1 documentation, "How to use sessions" (`set_expiry`, `get_expiry_age`) and "Settings" (`SESSION_COOKIE_AGE`), checked against the installed Django 6.1.2 source (`django/contrib/sessions/backends/base.py`, `django/contrib/auth/__init__.py`); WCAG 2.2 criteria 1.4.3, 1.4.11 (contrast values calculated with `leg_platform.branding.contrast`: `#8A5A00` on `#FFFFFF` 5.93, on `#F6F7F8` 5.53; `#E8B34A` on `#191C1F` 8.95, on `#111315` 9.73; `#005BA2` on `#F6F7F8` 6.49; `#6AA9E0` on `#111315` 7.42).
* Security implications for the review: (1) the persistent session (duration, no sliding, the staff exclusion, the wish through the second step; tests in `test_stay_signed_in.py`); (2) the new admin model (permission for system administrators only, the https-only validation, links with `noopener noreferrer`, the fail-safe lookup); (3) the scripts of the corner buttons (external files, allowlisted values from the `sessionStorage` only, no URL parameters, no inline script; Content Security Policy unchanged); (4) the removal of the `/appearance/` view and the cookie (less state, less attack surface); (5) the instance logo files (public static files, checked; `INSTANCE_STATIC_DIRECTORY` is outside the repository).
* Note 2026-10-16 (owner decision, D-074): the review of the French, Italian and English texts by native speakers is not required and not planned; the requirement was dropped. The texts and the language menu stay as they are.
## D-057: Design Package, Phase 2: the Frame After the Login, the Start Page and the Task Tiles

* Date: 2026-10-14
* Status: Order of the project owner (D-056); implemented in phase 2. Not yet reviewed by the security reviewer.
* Decision:
  * **Frame (WL-11, K1, K4, K5, K10, K13).** `base.html` is the frame of every page after the login: a fixed header of 64 px (the logo 48 px high, the name, the corner buttons of D-056), on a desktop a fixed left menu of 232 px ("Mein Bereich": Start, Profil, for staff users "Verwaltung"; "Demnächst": Standorte, Rechnungen, Energie, Mitteilungen, each with the mark "später"; below a line "Abmelden" and "Datenschutz" and "Impressum"), on a phone (760 px and below, as the entry shell) a fixed bottom bar of 72 px (Start, Energie, Rechnungen, Standorte) and the same menu in a drawer of 290 px that opens from the left with an overlay of 35 % black. The images of `navigation/` win over `app/` (K1). Styles are in `static/css/frame.css`, the drawer in `static/js/frame-menu.js` (Escape, a click on the overlay or the close button; the page behind is `inert` and the focus is held in the drawer; the focus returns to the menu button). Without JavaScript the menu button is a link to the anchor of the drawer, which the style sheet shows with `:target`, so the profile stays reachable on a phone. The "später" entries are no links (K10): no `href`, `aria-disabled="true"`, a visually hidden note "noch nicht verfügbar".
