The active menu entry is marked with `aria-current="page"`; on the profile no entry of the bottom bar is active (K4). "Abmelden" is a real POST form with the CSRF token; on a page that sends no referrer (token pages, D-054) it is left out. "Verwaltung" is shown only for `is_staff` (K13); the administration checks the permissions itself. The addresses of "Datenschutz" and "Impressum" are those of the portal settings (D-056). Controls are at least 44 px high on a desktop and 48 px on a phone (K5). An account that must replace its password (D-005) gets the header and "Abmelden" only; a person without a login who opens a page that is not part of the entry shell yet gets the header with the logo and the legal links only. The old header, `<details>` menu, top navigation, tab bar and the footer of the colour mode form are removed; their rules in `portal.css` stay until the end of the design work and are then deleted.
  * **Start page (WL-12).** `/` is the start page for a person with a login (a visitor is sent to the login page): "Guten Tag, Vorname Nachname" (the company name for an account without a personal name), the customer number and the street with the house number (or the post office box) of the account. `LOGIN_REDIRECT_URL` is `home`; the `next` logic is unchanged. The page is not cached. `/welcome/` was removed in phase 3 (D-058): the registration leads to the start page.
  * **Tasks and tiles (WL-13, K2).** The tasks are computed from the state of the account, never stored (`accounts/tasks.py`): "Adresse ergänzen" while a street (or post office box), a postal code or a town is missing; "Zwei-Faktor-Anmeldung" while no second factor is set up. A tile has the title, the status "Offen" (a dot of `--warn` plus the word), one line, a secondary button and the button "Ausblenden". The 2FA button is a POST to the setup page (a GET changes nothing, D-055); the address button leads to the profile edit page until the profile is rebuilt (phase 4). Two tiles side by side from 761 px (at least 280 px wide), one per row on a phone. The tile replaces the earlier card "Konto vervollständigen" of the registration images and the tip on the profile (the session flag, the view `two_factor_tip_dismiss`, the template block and its texts are removed). **Hiding is final and server-side:** the model `HiddenTask` (account, key, time; unique per account and key; deleted with the account), `POST /tasks/<key>/hide/` with the CSRF check, a login and the account of the request only, an unknown key is a 404, no way back, no list of hidden tasks; a hidden task stays hidden even if something is missing later (the process asks then, as the package says). The empty state (no tile): a round 120 px mark with a check in the accent on the warm colour, "Keine offenen Aufgaben", the text of the package, no button and no card frame. The administration shows the hidden tasks of an account read-only (support: "I do not see the tile").
  * **Changes of the review of phase 1** (`docs/SECURITY.md`, section 3 and 11, for the wording): L1 the wish "stay signed in" lives on the request object or in the pending login, never in the session; L2 a login without the box resets the expiry; L3 the expiry is an absolute time; L4 the address validation catches parsing errors and DEL; E1 SECURITY.md describes the option; E2 changes of the portal settings are a security event.
  * **Acceptance of the designer, phase 2 (the images win; team lead decision):** the desktop menu has 32 px above the first label, 4 px between the entries (44 px high; 48 + 4 = a pitch of 52 px in the phone menu), ends 24 px above the bottom edge, the icon column starts 24 px from the left (the text at 56 px: the 3 px bar is the transparent left border, the padding is 9 px); the group labels have 12 px above and below; the legal row is 28 px high. The desktop header has 16 px at the left; the corner buttons are 24 px from the right edge with 12 px between them (a rule `body:has(.frame) .corner` for a desktop; the phone and the entry pages keep 16 px and 8 px), the name ends 20 px before them. On the start page the subtitle is 8 px below the title and the tiles (or the empty state) start 64 px below the subtitle (y = 231 px in the image: 16 px more than the spacing scale allows, because the image wins). The empty state is a panel as wide as the content (1 px line, 12 px radius, no shadow, at least 360 px high, centred content). In the phone menu the focus goes to the close button when it opens, the close button and the logo plate are 16 px from the edges and the legal row is indented (24 px) with 36 px between the two links. **Deviation, noted on purpose:** the padding of a tile stays 24 px (R3 of the package, the scale 4, 8, 12, 16, 24, 32, 48); the image shows about 20 px. **Second deviation, noted on purpose (3c item 11):** the corner buttons stay 40 px high, as in the images and in the package, although K5 asks for 44 px (desktop) and 48 px (phone) for controls.
  * **Changes of the review of phase 2 (N-1 to N-4, E-1 to E-4):** `POST /tasks/<key>/hide/` without a login leads to the login page without `?next`; the key is checked explicitly (`ValueError` in `tasks.hide`, a 404 in the view) and no `except KeyError` surrounds the write; an account that must replace its password and an account whose mandatory second factor is not complete in this session get the slim frame (header and "Abmelden" only; the context value `frame_restricted`, `leg_platform/context_processors.py`); negative tests for the forced password change, staff without a second factor, `no-store` on every frame page and HEAD, OPTIONS and PATCH on the hide URL (`test_frame.py`, `docs/SECURITY.md`, section 11); the footer and menu links are checked again before output (`portal_settings._safe_link`: https only, no unsafe characters, otherwise no link); `environment._parse_url` and `validate_https_address` share `has_unsafe_url_character` (white space and the Unicode categories Cc, Cf, Zl, Zp); the phone menu has a link to `#` that closes it without a script (the buttons stay); the menu entry "Profil" is active on the profile pages only (`PROFILE_VIEWS`).
* **Deviation kept on purpose (final acceptance, A5):** the control "Menü öffnen" on a phone is an `<a href="#...">` and not a `<button>`: without JavaScript the anchor opens the drawer, and a second anchor closes it (security review phase 2, E-3). With JavaScript it is operated like a button (Enter, Space, Escape closes, focus held and returned). The corner buttons are 40 px as in the images (exception from K5, D-057 above).
* Sources: `LEG-Design-Vorgaben/navigation/VORGABEN.md` (sections 2 to 7, 8a), `navigation/bilder/*.png`, `app/bilder/kacheln-*.png` and `register/bilder/6-hinweis-*.png` (replaced by the tiles, K2), team lead decisions K1, K2, K4, K5, K10, K13 (`lead-decisions.md`); WAI-ARIA Authoring Practices (modal dialog: focus held inside, Escape closes, focus returns to the control); HTML Standard (`inert`); Django 6.1.2 source for the sessions (see D-056).

## D-058: Design Package, Phase 3: the Registration in Three Steps and the Other Entry Pages

* Date: 2026-10-15
* Status: Order of the project owner (D-056); implemented in phase 3. Not yet reviewed by the security reviewer; the points for the review are listed below.
* Decision:
  * **The three steps (WL-09, K7, K8, K14, K15, K20, K22).** Step 1 "E-Mail-Adresse bestätigen" asks for the address only ("Link senden", the link is valid 24 hours). The page after it, "Prüfen Sie Ihre E-Mails", is the same for every address (new, with an account, blocked, locked: D-054, unchanged), names the address that this browser entered (kept in the session of this browser only, `accounts/resend.py`, never in the URL) and offers "E-Mail erneut senden" after a pause of 60 seconds. **The pause is enforced on the server** (a time in the session; a request before it sends nothing); the countdown of `static/js/resend-cooldown.js` only shows it, and the limits per address, per network and per instance (D-038) apply to every resend. The address cannot be chosen in a resend request. "Andere E-Mail-Adresse verwenden" leads back to step 1. A link that is not valid, expired or already used leads (redirect) to "Link nicht mehr gültig", a page of its own with the address field and "Neuen Link senden" (a form on the page of the link itself would be refused by the CSRF check, D-054). Step 2 of the bar, "Passwort festlegen" (4a): the address as text and at the same time a real, read-only `username` field in the same form (so that a password manager saves both; the submitted value is never used), the password twice and the rules as a list. Step 3 of the bar, "Angaben zu Ihrer Person" (4b): the address in a box with the status "Bestätigt", first name, last name, street, house number, postal code, town (pairs side by side, on the phone too) and the checkbox for the privacy notice (a link to the address of the portal settings when there is one; opens in a new tab). The step bar is made of classes (`step-fill-1` to `-3`: 33.333 %, 66.667 %, 100 %) because the Content Security Policy allows no `style` attribute; the screens "check your email" and "link not valid" belong to step 1.
  * **The account is created at "Konto erstellen" (K22).** The row of the account is still created at step 1 (unchanged design, D-037, expires with the link), but the account is **activated only by the last step**: the status, the customer number and the password are written there, in one transaction with the lock of the account and of the link (D-037, unchanged), then the person is logged in as before (D-055) and sent to the start page. The password of 4a is checked there and kept **only as a hash** (Django's `make_password`) in the session, **bound to the digest of the verification link** (a hash held for another link is refused, and opening another link drops it); the page of 4b without it redirects to 4a. The password itself is never stored in the session. **Consequence:** the similarity check of Django (`UserAttributeSimilarityValidator`) can only use the address at 4a, not the name that comes at 4b. The consent to the privacy notice is stored with the time and the address of the notice that was shown (`User.privacy_accepted_at`, `privacy_accepted_url`, not editable, migration 0018). `/welcome/` and its template are removed. The fields salutation, company, address addition, post office box and phone number stay in the model and are entered in the profile (K15, phase 4; the profile is not changed in this phase).
  * **Password rules (K20 and `docs/SECURITY.md`, section 3).** The list under the first password field states the real policy of the project (at least 15 characters, a sentence is easy to remember, all characters allowed and no composition rules, no known or number-only passwords, not similar to the address). **This deviates from the placeholder of the package**, which names upper-case letters, digits and special characters: the policy of the project has no composition rules (`docs/SECURITY.md`, section 3, source OWASP Authentication Cheat Sheet; NIST SP 800-63B-4, Appendix A "Strength of Passwords", section "Complexity": composition rules bring little and people answer them in predictable ways), and the list must not promise a rule that the server does not enforce.
  * **Mails of the registration (K14).** The two mails have the subjects "Ihre Registrierung beim LEG-Portal" and "Ihr Konto beim LEG-Portal" (the name of the portal from the instance configuration), the texts of the package and, instead of a button, the link on a line of its own after "Registrierung fortsetzen:" or "Zur Anmeldung:" (the mail system sends plain text; a HTML part with a button would need its own template and review). The texts can still be edited in the administration ("Mailtexte", D-039); the defaults are the new ones.
  * **Other entry pages (WL-10, WL-18).** All pages of a link of a mail and every page without a session use the entry shell (D-056): password reset (request "Passwort zurücksetzen", the page after it like "check your email" without the bar, the new password like 4a, the invalid link like "Link nicht mehr gültig" with the address field), the second step of the login ("Anmeldung bestätigen", "Code aus der App", the hint about the recovery code), the lockout page ("Anmeldung vorübergehend gesperrt" in the alert style), and the result pages "E-Mail-Adresse geändert" (shown by the confirmation itself, no redirect; the address is named) and "Link nicht mehr gültig" with the panel "Mein Konto". The templates for "Zweite Adresse bestätigt" and "Änderung gestoppt" exist with the texts of the designer (`email_second_confirmed.html`, `email_change_stopped.html`); they are not routed, the second address and the stop link come with the profile (phase 4).
  * **Terms.** The page of the second step of the login says "Wiederherstellungscode" (the package). The other pages and mails of the second factor said "Notfallcode" until phase 4; since phase 4 the whole application says "Wiederherstellungscode" / "recovery code" (D-059).
  * **Dead code.** Removed: `CompleteRegistrationForm`, the view `WelcomeView`, the template `welcome.html`, the old strings of the catalogue. The old rules of the member menu in `portal.css` were removed in phase 4.
  * **Link validity (designer finding 4, team lead decision):** the link of the registration mail stays valid **until step 4b is completed** (or 24 hours pass), not only until the first click. Reasons: mail scanners open links, the back button, and the loss of a session between the steps must not force a new mail. The mail therefore has exactly the text of the package ("... Der Link ist 24 Stunden gültig."); no mail and no page of the registration claims that the link "can be used once" (the other links, for the password reset and the second factor, remain single use and say so). Opening the link rotates the session key (`cycle_key`, security review B1); the data of the session stay.
  * **Bot protection, stage 0 (security review A1, A2, B-items; team lead decision):** (a) a hidden field `contact_fax_number` (hidden by the class `hp-field`, `aria-hidden`, `tabindex="-1"`, `autocomplete="off"`) and a signed form time stamp `form_started` (`accounts/bot_check.py`, `django.core.signing`, minimum age 2 seconds, setting `MAIL_FORM_MINIMUM_SECONDS`, maximum 3 days) in step 1, the invalid-link form and the request of a password reset; a hit leads to the same confirmation page without a job, so a script learns nothing; (b) the read-only precheck of the limits of the client address and of the instance (`mail_queue.requests_refused`) before anything is stored: a refused request creates no `MailJob` and no session of the client (`resend.remember` is not called) and gets the visible answer "Zu viele Anfragen, bitte versuchen Sie es später erneut." with HTTP 429; **only** these two limits are ever shown, because they do not depend on an account (no account oracle); the limit of an address stays silent; (c) a request for the address this browser entered less than the pause ago creates no new job (the page is the same); (d) a second window of 3 requests per 10 minutes per address next to 12 per hour and 24 per day (D-038). **Recommendation, not built:** a proof of work (ALTCHA) as stage 1 against bots that fill the form correctly. It needs the approval of the designer and the study of the manufacturer's documentation (licence, data protection, self-hosted widget under the CSP) before anyone builds it.
  * **Changes of the review of phase 3 and the acceptance of the designer (blocks 3b and 3c):** the field `username` of 4a and of the new password is set again to the verified address when the form is bound (A4); the hint no longer contains an example password (A5); the keys `mail_request_*` and the password hash leave the session after the completion and when the link is invalid (B2); the 4b submit button is disabled while the form is sent (`static/js/submit-once.js`, B3); a person who is logged in and opens a link of the registration goes to the start page (B3); the privacy address shown at 4b is the one stored (B6); the security event `registration_completed` names the internal account number only (B8); the e-mail address at 4b is plain text with the status beside it (K7); a field has one message (the checks of the model that repeat a form check are not run twice, no "Firmenname"/"Postfach" in the registration texts); the error summary uses short field names and is shown and focused on the single-field pages too; hints and rules are linked with `aria-describedby`; invalid fields have a red 2 px border; the hint text is 14 px; the note "Ihre Adresse konnte nicht geprüft werden und wird so gespeichert, wie Sie sie eingegeben haben." appears at 4b only when a directory is configured but not reachable; the street and house number grid is `minmax(0, 1fr) 96px` (checked at 360 and 390 px); the page of an invalid link of the second-factor removal has the heading "Link nicht mehr gültig" and names the website of the instance as the way to contact the operator. **Deliberate deviation:** the heading of the login page is `h1` "Anmelden" and the panel title a `<p>` (D-6 of the designer acceptance of phase 1: better for screen readers).
* Sources: `LEG-Design-Vorgaben/register/VORGABEN.md`, `register/bilder/*.png`, `register/referenz/*.html`, `profil/bilder/email-*.png`, team lead decisions K7, K8, K14, K15, K20, K22 (`lead-decisions.md`); NIST SP 800-63B-4 (Digital Identity Guidelines, Authentication and Authenticator Management), Appendix A "Strength of Passwords" (checked on 2026-10-15 at pages.nist.gov/800-63-4/sp800-63b/passwords/); OWASP Forgot Password Cheat Sheet (the same response for every address, single-use link, rate limits); Django 6.1.2 documentation "Password management" (`make_password`) and "Sessions".
* Security implications for the review: (1) the held password hash in the session and its binding to the link (`REGISTRATION_PASSWORD_SESSION_KEY`, `RegistrationCompleteView`); (2) the server-side pause and the address in the session (`resend.py`, `ResendView`; the address of the session is never taken from the request); (3) the account row created at step 1 is activated only at step 3 (the transaction and the lock order are unchanged); (4) the pages without a session now show the entered address (only to the browser that entered it); (5) the consent fields; (6) the bot check of stage 0 and the 429 policy are described above (blocks 3b); the proof of work is a recommendation.

## D-059: Design Package, Phase 4: the Profile in Cards, the Second Address and the Inline Second Factor

* Date: 2026-10-16
* Status: Order of the project owner (D-056); implemented in phase 4. Not yet reviewed by the security reviewer; the points for the review are listed below.
* Decision:
  * **One page, five cards (WL-14, K11, K12).** `/profile/` is "MEIN KONTO / Profil" in the frame of D-057: the cards "Persönliche Angaben", "E-Mail-Adressen", "Passwort", "Zwei-Faktor-Anmeldung" and "Konto löschen" (the red start card). A card changes in place. The state is rendered by the server and is in the address (`?edit=email`, `?edit=two_factor&step=disable` ...) and in the answer of a form (an invalid form answers with the same page and the bound form), so the page works without JavaScript, a reload or the back button keeps the step, and only one card is in the edit state at a time (the others show their view). A success redirects to `/profile/#card-...` with a message. The paths of the earlier pages stay as the places where a card posts (`/profile/edit/`, `/profile/email/`, `/profile/password/`, `/profile/two-factor/...`, `/profile/delete/`); a GET on them shows the card in the matching state (the setup pages render the profile themselves, because the middleware of the mandatory second factor sends a restricted account there and a redirect would loop). The pages `email-change/request/` and `email-change/sent/`, `profile_edit`, `account_delete`, `two_factor*`, `recovery_codes*` and the old tips are removed. An account that must replace its password keeps the stand-alone page (D-005); an account whose mandatory second factor is not set up sees the card of the second factor only.
  * **Personal details (K15).** The form of the card has the name, the address (the address widget of D-033 stays) and, under "Weitere Angaben (optional)", the fields salutation, company, address addition, post office box and phone number; the registration (D-058) does not ask for them. The rules of the model are unchanged.
  * **Email addresses and the second address (the model `User.secondary_email`, `PendingEmailChange`).** The card shows the login address and an optional second address, each with its own status ("Bestätigt", "Ausstehend" with the deadline and "Anfrage abbrechen"). A change needs the current password, and the code where a second factor is set up (K12; the 5-minute window of D-055 is not used for this). A request stores a row `PendingEmailChange` (account, kind login or secondary, address, deadline of 24 hours; one per account and kind) **for every target address, whether or not it belongs to another account**, so the answer is the same (no enumeration, D-054); the worker creates the token only where the change can work and checks again that the row exists (a cancel in between wins). The link of the mail (the two-step pattern of D-054: the page of the link, then a POST) completes the change: for the login address the same as before (all other sessions end; mail to the old and the new address), for the second address only `secondary_email` is set. **The second address is never used to log in or to reset the password**; it is not unique (it can be the same as the second address of another account, because it receives only notifications), it is stored only after its confirmation, and it can be removed at once (password required; notification). **Notifications go to every address of the account** (`User.notification_addresses()`, `mail_queue.enqueue_for_account`, one job per address): changed password, changed or removed address, second factor on, off, new recovery codes, deletion.
  * **The stop link.** Every request for a new login or second address sends a notice to the **old** login address and to the confirmed second address: "Haben Sie diese Änderung nicht veranlasst? Dann klicken Sie hier, um sie sofort zu stoppen." The link (`/email-change/stop/<token>/`) works without a login (the person who got the notice may not have the password any more) and follows the two-step pattern (a page, then a POST with a CSRF token, so a mail scanner does not stop anything). It cancels all pending changes of the account, deletes all confirmation and stop tokens, **ends every session of the account** (the password may be known to somebody else) and records the security event `email_change_stopped`; the page says "Änderung gestoppt" and recommends a new password. One stop token per recipient; it is valid as long as the latest pending change.
  * **Password card (K11).** Current password, new password twice with the rules list (the policy of `docs/SECURITY.md`, section 3); the other sessions end.
  * **Second factor card, inline.** States: active (with "Neue Wiederherstellungscodes erzeugen" and "Deaktivieren"), not set up ("Einrichten"), setup step 1 (the password; where the 5-minute window of D-055 holds, none) and step 2 (QR code as an own SVG file, the key as text with a mono font, the code), the ten recovery codes **shown once, in the answer of the POST that creates them** (never stored in plain text, not in the session, not on a reload), with "Kopieren" (`static/js/recovery-codes.js`, no network), a checkbox "Ich habe die Codes gespeichert" and "Fertig", which the server checks (`saved`); without the tick the card says so and shows no codes again (a deliberate deviation: the codes cannot be shown twice, so the person is told to create new ones); new codes need the password and a code; deactivating needs the password and a code; every change is mailed to all addresses. The term is "Wiederherstellungscode" everywhere (team lead, phase 4: UI, mails, mail text defaults, catalogue, tests); "Notfallcode" does not occur any more (the older decisions D-040 to D-055 keep the old term "emergency code" as written at the time).
  * **Delete the account (WL-17).** The red start card; the confirmation shows the notice of `legal/VORGABEN.md` section 7 with the link to the privacy address of the portal settings (when set), the password (and the code, K12), a checkbox that is never ticked in advance and a red button. After the deletion the page "Ihr Konto wurde gelöscht" is rendered directly in the answer (the session has ended); the mail goes to all addresses (they are taken before the deletion). The rules of D-055 (uniform deletion, retention obligation) are unchanged: the notice of the package names the legal retention of invoices and booking records in general terms, the same text for every account; it never says whether this account has an obligation (the one thing D-055 forbids).
  * **Security events.** New: `email_change_cancelled`, `email_change_stopped`, `secondary_email_changed`, `registration_completed` (D-058, B8); the texts are in `security_log.MESSAGES`. They hold no personal data (hosts, counts, internal account number only).
  * **Deviations from the images, noted on purpose:** (a) the buttons of the cards are at least 44 px high on a desktop (K5 wins over the 40 px of the images, decision of the team lead after the acceptance of phase 4) and 48 px on a phone, where the button of a card sits below its rows; (b) the edit state of the email card shows one form with the login address, the second address and the password (the image shows only one new address, because the images do not know the second address); (c) "Fertig" without the tick cannot show the codes again (see above); (d) **the page "Link nicht mehr gültig" of the confirmation of an address keeps the neutral text** ("Dieser Link kann nicht mehr verwendet werden. Falls Sie die Änderung soeben bestätigt haben, melden Sie sich mit der neuen Adresse an.") instead of the text of the image ("... Ihre E-Mail-Adresse wurde nicht geändert ..."): a second call after the confirmation (a double click, a mail scanner) must not claim that nothing changed (security review of phase 3, A3; acceptance of phase 4, D12). Measured against `navigation-profil-hell.png` and `profil-mail-ansicht-hell.png`: card tops, row lines, the value column (x = 621) and card heights agree at 1440 px.
  * **Acceptance of phase 4 (block 4c):** the message of an action is shown inside the card that changed, under its title (`role="status"`; views tag the message with `card-<name>`, `render_profile` sorts them); after an action `static/js/profile-focus.js` moves the focus to the message, the first field of the open card or the heading of the card of the address (the page works without it); the button "2FA einrichten" is primary only when no other card is open (R1); on a phone the form rows are one field per row (the pairs of the registration stay), the two-factor card keeps title and status in one line, the recovery codes stay in two columns without an extra box and the QR code is left aligned; the labels "PLZ" and "Code aus der App" (placeholder 000000); the destructive submit buttons ("2FA deaktivieren", "Konto endgültig löschen") are red outlines; the error of a field stands directly under the field, before its hint; the summary names the box of the deletion "Bestätigung"; the buttons "Anfrage abbrechen" have the address in their accessible name; the mails round the validity of a link up ("24 Stunden", never "23"); the password card says "Ändern", the second address says what it is for, and a result page says "Zum Profil" to a person who is logged in.
  * **Dead code removed:** the rules `.member-*`, `.portal-header*`, `.portal-brand`, `.tip-card`, `.tip-actions`, `.step-indicator*`, `.setup-instructions`, `.info-details`, `.field-row*`, `.form-section`, `.hr-text-plain`, `.status-blocked`, `.status-deactivated` and the duplicate `.recovery-codes` of `portal.css`.
* Sources: `LEG-Design-Vorgaben/navigation/VORGABEN.md` (section 3.3), `navigation/bilder/profil-*.png` and `handy-profil-*.png`, `profil/bilder/*`, `legal/VORGABEN.md` (section 7), team lead decisions K11, K12, K15 and the term "Wiederherstellungscode" (`lead-decisions.md`, designer-gap-analysis section F); OWASP Forgot Password Cheat Sheet and Authentication Cheat Sheet (notification of changes, same response for every address); Django 6.1.2 documentation (forms, `Form.bound_field_class`, sessions).
* Security implications for the review: (1) the stop link without a login and the sessions that it ends; (2) the pending rows for every target address and the equal response (no enumeration), the check again in the worker; (3) notices to several addresses (a confirmed second address is a recipient of every security message); (4) the recovery codes appear only in one answer (cache headers `no-store`, `sensitive_post_parameters`, the copy script); (5) the second address is not an identifier (never used for the login or the reset); (6) the re-authentication of every change (password, and a code where a second factor exists), the rate limit of the address change request (D-038 limits; HTTP 429 of the card for the limit of the client address and of the instance only); (7) the deletion result page and the mail taken before the deletion; (8) GET on the setup paths renders the profile and changes nothing.
* **Security review of phase 4 (block 4b, 2026-10-16).** H1: the stop links are deleted as soon as no request is open (confirmation of either kind, cancel, expiry; a new request deletes the older links), and the pages and `stop_all` refuse a link without an open request and end no session; the result page recommends a new password and names the ended sessions. M1: `ProfileForm.save` writes only the fields of the form (`update_fields`). M2: `delete_expired_reset_tokens` (part of `cleanup_mail_requests` and `run_scheduled_tasks`) also removes expired second-address and stop links, expired requests and stop links without an open request; the deletion of an account removes every link of an address change. M3: the limits are checked for every kind that the posted values ask for, before the password and the code are checked; `request_changes` raises `RequestRefused` when a submission is refused and everything of the call is rolled back (HTTP 429). M4: bot hits and repeats within the pause are counted per client address (`SCOPE_MAIL_SILENT`, 30 per hour). M5: the mail kind and text "secondary_email_changed" (migration 0020) has no sentence about logging in again, and `email_changed.txt` has `autoescape off`. Recommendations taken: the honeypot is named `contact_fax_number`; the notice is queued before the confirmation link; the box of "Fertig" is `required`; `sensitive_variables` for the codes; the card "Passwort" asks for the code where a second factor is set up (K12). **Open question for the owner, not built:** an attacker with the password and a session who confirms the new address in his own mailbox at once leaves no window for the stop link; a short undo window after a confirmed login-address change (mail to the old address with a link) would need a product decision.
## D-060: Design Package, Phase 5: Error Pages, Grouped Administration and Documentation

* Date: 2026-10-16
* Status: Order of the project owner (D-056); implemented in phase 5. Not yet reviewed by the security reviewer.
* Decision:
  * **Error pages (WL-20).** `error_base.html` stays independent of the session, the database and every context processor (D-054) and gets the design of the package: a centred card (1 px line, 12 px radius, no shadow, at most 480 px) on the page background with the logo (40 px high; on the plate in dark mode, the name as text without a logo), a heading of 32 px (24 px on a phone), one sentence and one way out. The name and the logos come from the settings through the tag `instance_branding` (`accounts/templatetags/instance_branding.py`), because the page of a server error is rendered without a request. Texts of `navigation/VORGABEN.md` 8a: 404 "Diese Seite gibt es nicht mehr." with the question "Zurück zur Startseite?" as a link; 500 "Hoppla, da ist etwas schiefgelaufen." and "Bitte versuchen Sie es gleich noch einmal." with the button "Zur Startseite"; the same friendly tone for 403 ("Hier haben Sie keinen Zugriff.") and the failed security check ("Die Seite ist abgelaufen. Bitte laden Sie die Seite neu und versuchen Sie es noch einmal. Es wurde nichts gespeichert."). These two texts are proposals of the designer, not words of the package. No technical detail is shown (D-054); the tests render the 500 page without a request.
  * **Administration (WL-23).** `PortalAdminSite.get_app_list` shows three groups on the start page and in the side navigation: "Benutzer" (accounts, groups), "Inhalte" (portal settings, email texts) and "System" (Microsoft Graph, security log, blocked and failed logins, address directory); a model that is not named goes to "System", and a group only lists what the account may see. The page of one app keeps Django's list. The pages of django-axes are named "Gesperrte und fehlgeschlagene Anmeldungen" and "Protokoll der fehlgeschlagenen Anmeldungen" (the names in `_meta` of the two models are set in `accounts/admin.py`; the product name no longer appears). The title and the header of the administration are the name of the instance (`INSTANCE_NAME`, "LEG-Portal"), as the portal's; the earlier "LEG Platform" / "LEG-Plattform" is gone. The start page has the hint "Texte der E-Mails und Adressen der Fusszeile pflegen Sie unter Inhalte." (template `admin/index.html`). WL-26 (the hidden tasks of an account, read-only on its page) was already built in phase 2 and is tested (`test_frame.py`).
  * **Documentation (WL-24).** *(Superseded in part by D-073: branding is now maintained in the administration, with the environment as the fallback.)* `docs/DESIGN.md`, section 3.2, lists where the operator of a deployment changes each branding value (environment, static directory, administration); branding stays with the deployment (the contrast checks and the logo files belong there; the administration only has the two footer addresses). `docs/STARTUP.md`, `README.md` ("Current Status") and `docs/ACCEPTANCE.md` describe the new state: ports (8000 for the acceptance, 8001 for the developer), the pages after the login, the registration steps and limits, the test accounts. The obsolete entries of the Swiss German catalogue were removed.
* Sources: `LEG-Design-Vorgaben/navigation/VORGABEN.md`, section 8a; designer-gap-analysis WL-20, WL-23, WL-24, WL-26 (`lead-decisions.md`); Django 6.1.2 source (`AdminSite.get_app_list`, `django.views.defaults`, template tags without request).
* Security implications for the review: (1) the error pages use no request data (no path, no exception text, no host); (2) the administration grouping changes only the display: every view still checks the permissions, and a group shows only the models that `get_app_list` of Django lists for the account.
* **Final security review (block 5b, 2026-10-16).** M-A: `User.discard_email_change_requests()` deletes the open requests and all confirmation and stop links of an account; it runs after a password reset, after a password change, when the status becomes anything but active (`set_status`, and so in `delete_or_deactivate`) and in `remove_second_factor` (reset by the administration, by the link of the owner, by the emergency command); no-op without a request. A person who changes the password of their own account therefore also loses their own open request (they can ask again). L1: all 18 mail templates have `autoescape off`; one test covers every key. L2: confirming a login address deletes all stop links of the account. L3: `cycle_key()` when opening the link of the second factor reset. L4/L5: wording of SECURITY.md and of the docstring. R2: the code (app or recovery code) of a form is checked, and so used up, only when the password and every other field are valid (`ReauthenticationForm.check_changes`). R3: comments of `error_base.html` and `context_processors.py` state that only the 500 page is fully independent; a test with a session cookie and a failing session store shows that 403/404/CSRF then give the 500 page without detail. R4: the hint on the start page of the administration only for accounts that see the group "Inhalte". R5: `confirm_change` also catches `ValueError` of `change_email`. M-B stays open (see above).

## D-061: Design Package, Final Acceptance (Block 5c): Sizes and the Administration

* Date: 2026-10-16
* Status: Order of the project owner (priority: "the administrator finds the dynamic settings easily"); implemented in block 5c after the final acceptance of the designer. Not yet reviewed by the security reviewer.
* Decision:
  * **Sizes (A1-A3).** The label of a field in an entry card is 16 px (600), hints and messages below a field are 14 px (K6: 14 or 16); the hint of the profile form moved from 13 to 14 px; on a phone the button of a task tile fills the width of the tile also when it is a form button (the "2FA einrichten" tile). A4 stays: when the password is wrong, the message appears in the summary and under the field. A5 stays: the "open menu" control is an `<a>` that works without JavaScript (an anchor to the drawer); it is operable by keyboard and Escape closes the drawer (deviation from the word "button" of the package, noted for D-057).
  * **Same code twice.** A second save with an app code that was already used shows the message "Der Code ist falsch oder wurde bereits verwendet." in the card (test); it is never silent. The log of the designer showed no second POST, so the cause was not on the server.
  * **Start page "Erste Schritte" (P1-1).** A box with the live status and one link each: address of the privacy notice (set / not yet set), address of the legal notice, email texts ("18 texts, n customised") and the email delivery (Microsoft Graph, ready / not ready). Read only, no secret and no address of an account is shown; an item appears only for an account that may open its page (`has_view_permission` of the model admin).
  * **Email texts (P1-2).** The list shows the process (registration, login and password, email addresses, 2FA, account) in that order, the plain words "Standardtext" or "Angepasst" instead of the red cross, "-" as the last change of an unchanged text (after "use the default text again" the state is "Standardtext" again), and no table of placeholders (it stays on the page of each email). The subject field has the full width.
  * **No predefined roles (P1-3).** The suggestion of the designer to ship groups by migration is **not taken** (`AGENTS.md`, section 6; D-012): the portal ships no group, role or rights set. Instead the names of the rights in the lists of the administration are German ("Mailtext: ändern", `accounts/permission_names.py`), and the pages "Gruppen" carry the help "Welche Rechte brauche ich wofür?" (text only: see accounts, change accounts, read and change the email texts; what only system administrators can do).
  * **Staff without rights (P2-4).** The hint about "Inhalte" appears only for an account that sees that group; an account that sees nothing gets "Ihr Konto hat noch keine Rechte in der Verwaltung. Bitte wenden Sie sich an einen Systemadministrator." in place of Django's sentence. The menu entry "Verwaltung" stays as decided (K13).
  * **Portal settings (P2-5).** Wide address fields, the examples "https://www.beispiel.ch/datenschutz" and ".../impressum", the hint "Muss mit https:// beginnen. Öffnet sich in einem neuen Tab." and the message "Gespeichert. Die Adressen erscheinen jetzt in der Fusszeile.".
  * **Blocked logins (P2-6).** The lists carry an explanation (the "username" is a code, nothing is shown in clear; a temporary lockout ends after the time of the lockout) and how to unblock a blocked account (the action of the account page). No name or address is added to the list.
  * **Words (P2-7, P2-8).** "Zwei-Faktor-Anmeldung (2FA)" everywhere (administration and the 18 mails; the first mention in a text has the abbreviation); the breadcrumb reads "Benutzer > Konten" (the app name is "Users"); "Geschichte" is "Änderungsverlauf"; "Versionen" are "Frühere Fassungen"; the link "Website anzeigen" of the header reads "Zum Portal". The look of the administration stays that of Django (`docs/DESIGN.md`); the only own stylesheet is `static/css/admin-portal.css` (widths and the two boxes).
  * **Not built (P3).** The read-only page "Instanz", the reset of hidden tasks and "Mitteilungen" (stay "später"). The texts of the login and registration pages can only be changed through the translation files; this is documented in `docs/OPERATIONS.md`, section 5a, and `docs/INSTALLATION.md`.
* Sources: the final acceptance report of the designer (blocks A and B); `AGENTS.md`, section 6; D-012; Django 6.1.2 admin source (`ModelAdmin.get_queryset`, `AdminSite.index`, templates `admin/index.html`, `admin/change_form.html`).
* **Block 6 (spot check of the designer, delta review of the security reviewer, 2026-10-16).**
  * Security. L-1: the password policy of Django (`SetPasswordForm.clean()`) runs before the code is checked, as Django 6.1.2 does it (read in the installed source, not assumed); a test with a too short and a too common new password and a recovery code shows that the code stays valid. L-2: the test `test_acceptance_final.FirstStepsBoxTests.test_the_box_lists_only_the_items_of_the_rights_of_the_account` of the tester assumes a right `view_graphconfiguration` that does not exist (the model has no default rights, so a staff member cannot get any right for it); the code is right, the module was not touched. L-3: the box says "Zertifikat abgelaufen" instead of "bereit" when the certificate of Microsoft Graph has expired. L-4: a test shows that the German names are display only (`Permission.name` and `codename` unchanged, the option value is the key); `makemigrations --check` is clean; the model `PendingEmailChange` got a name (migration 0021, options only) because its rights showed an English name. L-5: the detail pages of the blocked and failed logins no longer show the browser data (`user_agent`, `http_accept`).
  * Design. (1) A group is offered exactly the four documented rights (view and change of accounts and of email texts); a right that a group already holds stays in its list so that saving never removes it, and a forged post with another right is refused. The widget that chooses the rights is German: the installed Django translates its new widget texts only in English, so `locale/de_CH/LC_MESSAGES/djangojs.po` has the six missing texts (checked in the browser). (2) Help texts and descriptions are at least 14 px in `admin-portal.css`. (3) The breadcrumb names the group of the start page ("Startseite > Inhalte > Mailtexte"), also on the pages of the earlier versions, the preview, the history and the delete page (`portal_group` of `PortalAdminSite.each_context`, `admin/_crumb_group.html`). (4) The text of an email has the width of the subject. (5) One message for a wrong code everywhere, at the login and in the profile: "Der Code ist falsch, abgelaufen oder wurde schon verwendet. ... Warten Sie auf den nächsten ..."; it still says nothing about the account. (6) The same message after "Save" and "Save and continue editing"; when both addresses are empty: "Die Adressen wurden entfernt. Die Fusszeile zeigt Datenschutz und Impressum ohne Link." (7) The history says "Standardtext wiederhergestellt." (8) The status column of "Erste Schritte" has a fixed width. (9) The page of an email has one line about the earlier versions and, for system administrators, the change history. (10) The box says that name, logo and website address of the footer are set by the operator at the installation.
* Security implications for the review: (1) the dashboard reads the settings rows without creating them and shows no secret (a test checks a stored certificate and key); (2) the box depends on `has_view_permission`, so no new permission exists; (3) the readable names change only the display of the rights, the stored permissions are unchanged; (4) no group or role is created in a migration or in code.

## D-062: One Proof per Confirmation ("2FA wins")

* Date: 2026-10-15
* Status: Decision of the project owner (2026-10-15), specification of the designer (`reauth-spec.md`), implemented by the developer. **Replaces** K12 of the lead decisions and the part of D-059 that says the confirmations in the profile ask for "the password and the code". **Reviewed** by the security reviewer (commit b24fe40): no objection to the decision; finding H-1 (below) corrected in block 7. Block 8 reviewed (da2f797): M-1 and L-1 to L-5 corrected in block 9.
* Decision:
  * **Rule.** Every re-authentication in the profile asks for exactly one proof: the second-factor code (the 6-digit app code; since block 8 a recovery code is no proof here) if one is active, otherwise the current password, never both. It applies to: the e-mail addresses (change, second address add or remove), the password, new recovery codes, turning off 2FA, deleting the account, and the forced password change. A staff user or administrator without a second factor gives the password. Setting up 2FA after more than five minutes stays the password (2FA is not active yet; D-055).
  * **Forms.** `OneProofMixin` (`forms.py`) removes the field that is not needed: state A (2FA active) has `otp_token` and no `current_password`, state B the password only. A posted `current_password` in state A is ignored and grants nothing. The code is checked after every other field is valid and only when it has the shape of an app code (a recovery code is no proof in the profile since block 8, so it is neither accepted nor used up by these forms; it is used up only at the login and by the way "phone lost"), under the abuse protection of the login: the lockout and the pause of the account still refuse it. A wrong code is counted **for the session** since block 9, not against the budget of the account (see "Block 9").
  * **Shape check (owner/lead decision c).** After `totp.normalize_code` (no spaces and hyphens, lower case) only **exactly six digits** count as an app code (block 8; before, eight characters of a recovery code counted as well). Anything else is refused with "Das ist kein 2FA-Authentifizierungscode. Er besteht aus 6 Ziffern." **before** `verify_second_factor` and does **not** count against the budget of wrong codes (D-040), so a password typed into the field by habit (at least 15 characters) cannot pause the account for 24 hours. The shape check depends on the input only, so it tells nothing about the account. The value is never written back into the field.
  * **Password card.** In state A the form asks for the code and the new password twice; "the new password may not equal the current one" is checked only **after a valid proof**, with `django.contrib.auth.hashers.check_password(new, user.password)` **without a setter** (no rehash, no change of the stored password; H-1/L-1 of block 7). Before a valid proof the answer is the same whatever the new password is. Accepted consequence: with the code as the proof, the code is consumed before this comparison, so it is used up when the answer is "choose another password". `PasswordChangeView.post` does not check the code again under the lock (it is used up); it compares `get_session_auth_hash()` (password and session version) with the value at the start of the request and refuses the change with "Ihr Konto wurde inzwischen geändert. Bitte versuchen Sie es erneut." when the account changed in between (D-040). In state B the password is checked again under the lock as before.
  * **Attributes (lead decision e, changed by the owner in block 8).** The code field is a **plain text field**: visible text, `autocomplete=one-time-code`, the placeholder `000000`, no `inputmode`, no `maxlength`, no `pattern`, no special style (the server accepts up to 256 characters instead of 32, so that a long password pasted by habit gets the message "that is not a code" and not Django's message about the length). Autofocus only where it is the first field (not in the e-mail form). Every password field of the profile (also the one of the way "phone lost" and of "Passwort ändern") is hidden by default and has the eye button that shows it.
  * **Texts (block 8).** Label "2FA-Authentifizierungscode"; lead hint between label and field "Geben Sie den 6-stelligen Code aus Ihrer Authenticator-App ein. Ihr Passwort wird hier nicht benötigt."; **no word about recovery codes** in the normal forms; below the field (e-mail form) the note about the link; errors: empty "Geben Sie den 2FA-Authentifizierungscode ein.", no code "Das ist kein 2FA-Authentifizierungscode. Er besteht aus 6 Ziffern.", wrong "Der 2FA-Authentifizierungscode ist falsch oder abgelaufen. Warten Sie auf den nächsten Code in Ihrer App und versuchen Sie es erneut." (the login keeps its own common text, because it accepts recovery codes). The error summary uses the label. When the budget of wrong codes is used up, the abuse protection answers with its lockout page and its own text about the pause (the form adds no text of its own).
  * **Outdated images.** The images of the owner's package `profil-2fa-deaktivieren-*`, `profil-2fa-neue-codes-*`, `profil-loeschen-bestaetigen-*`, `profil-loeschen-bereit-*`, `profil-email-formular-*`, `profil-mail-bearbeiten-*`, `handy-profil-mail-bearbeiten-*`, `handy-profil-loeschen-bestaetigen-*` show "Aktuelles Passwort" and are valid only for state B; `profil-email-passwort-falsch-*` and `profil-mail-passwort-falsch-*` are valid for state B. State A has no image (`docs/DESIGN.md`, "One proof per confirmation"). The files of `LEG-Design-Vorgaben` are not changed (owner files); the text of `navigation/VORGABEN.md` section 3.3 (items 2 to 4) is outdated in the same way (code instead of the password with 2FA).
* Sources: owner decision 2026-10-15 ("Ein Nachweis statt zwei"); `reauth-spec.md` of the designer (sections 2.1 to 2.6, 3 to 5); OWASP Authentication Cheat Sheet (re-authentication for sensitive actions); NIST SP 800-63B-4 (a single authenticator for re-authentication); the code of `totp.py` (`normalize_code`, recovery codes of five random bytes in base32, eight characters, app codes of six digits).
* **Review of block 7 (security review of b24fe40).** **H-1 (corrected):** the comparison "new password = current password" was a guessing oracle (it ran without a proof and without the abuse protection). It now runs **only after a valid proof**: with the password as the proof, after `clean_current_password` verified it under the abuse protection; with the code, after `check_code` accepted it. Without a proof the answer is the same whatever the new password is (tests with a wrong code, no code, a shape error and a wrong password, comparing the messages, and a check that the comparison is not even called). The comparison is `django.contrib.auth.hashers.check_password(new, user.password)` **without a setter**, so it never rehashes or changes the stored password (L-1). **Accepted consequence:** with the code as the proof, the code is used up when the answer is "choose another password" (a recovery code is then lost). **L-2 (documented):** a code can also be used up when the request fails late (a limit reached after the form, an integrity error, the account changed in between, the session-hash comparison of the password card); nothing is changed then. **L-3:** tests for an account with recovery codes only and for an unconfirmed device (both are state B), for the second factor set up or removed between building and checking the form (the form fails closed with "Ihr Konto wurde inzwischen geändert"), for the shape error leaving no IP place and no Axes entry, and for 15 real wrong codes followed by the pause (429; since block 9 these are sent at the login step, because the confirmations of the profile count for the session). **L-4:** the unused `ReauthenticationMixin` was removed.
* **Block 8 (owner decision of 2026-10-15, "Handyverlust und klarere Re-Auth-Felder").** (1) **A recovery code is no proof in any confirmation of the profile** (e-mail addresses, password, new codes, turning off 2FA, deleting the account, forced password change): only the 6-digit app code counts (`totp.verify_code(..., recovery=False)` plus the shape check). Recovery codes count at the login and in (2). (2) **The way "phone lost"**, only on the card "turn off 2FA" and reachable without a script (the text link "Handy verloren?" under the code field opens the state `?edit=two_factor&step=lost_phone` of the same card; `POST /profile/two-factor/lost-phone/`): it asks for the **password and a recovery code**, both right. `LostPhoneForm` checks in this order: required fields and the shape of the code (8 characters of `a-z2-7`), then the password (`verify_password`, with Axes and the IP counter), then the code (`verify_second_factor(..., app=False)`, budget D-040, used up only if everything before was right). A wrong password and a wrong code (and the code of another account) give **the same message** ("Das Passwort oder der Wiederherstellungscode ist falsch. …"); a wrong password never reaches the check of the code. After it: the second factor is removed (`totp.disable(..., via_recovery_code=True)`), every other session ends, every address is told (the mails "recovery code used" and "2FA removed"), and the security log has the event "recovery code used" and the removal with its **own event** `two_factor_removed_phone_lost`, kept for the long period (block 9, L-2; the log line no longer carries the way as a detail). It is not available to staff users and administrators (the existing rule) and not without a second factor. The form tells people with neither the app nor a code to go through the operator (D-004: an administrator starts the removal, the owner confirms by link). (3) The labels and texts above. (4) The code field is a plain text field.
* **Residual risk (stated honestly, after block 8).** With an active second factor, an **app code alone** now suffices to change the e-mail address, the password or to delete the account; before, the password was needed in addition. A hijacked session plus an app code intercepted in real time (phishing with forwarding of the code) or an unlocked phone is enough. **A found recovery code does not suffice for these any more**; for "phone lost" it is not enough either, because the password is needed as well. What stays: the notifications to **every** address of the account (recovery code used, address change requested with the stop link, password changed, account deleted), the end of all other sessions after a change, the 24 hours of a pending address change with the stop link, the budget of wrong codes and the Axes lockout. The owner question of an undo window after a confirmed address change (D-059) stays open. **Block 9 adds:** whoever only holds a session can no longer burn the login budget of the account, but can still try five codes per session (see below); "the same message" for a wrong password and a wrong code in "phone lost" is a statement about the text, not about every side channel (timing, the pause answers with the lockout page only when the password was right), and the correctness of the password is observable at step 1 of the login anyway (D-017).
* **Block 9 (security review of da2f797).** **M-1 (availability):** before, every form-valid wrong app code of a confirmation counted against the budget of the account (10: notice, 15: pause for 24 hours, which also refuses the **login**), so a hijacked session without the password could pause the login of the owner. Now the wrong codes of the confirmations (all forms in state A and the code part of "phone lost") are counted **for the session**: `second_factor.verify_second_factor(..., per_session=True)` does not call `totp.record_failed_code` and does not send the failed-login signal, so neither the budget of the account nor Axes nor the IP counter is touched; the lockout of Axes and an existing pause of the account **still refuse** the check. The counter is the table `SessionCodeFailure` (migration 0022), found by a keyed digest of the session key (`keyed_digest`, purpose `LEG-Platform session code failures v1`), changed only under the row lock of the account and in the transaction of the check; it is **not stored in the session** (a deviation from the wording of the review), because parallel requests of one session each hold their own copy of the session and would each count from the same value, which would make the limit worthless. The browser can neither see nor set it (tests). After **five** form-valid wrong codes (`SESSION_CODE_LIMIT`) this session is ended (`logout`, the other sessions stay; done in `ProfileCardView.dispatch`, so every view is covered), one entry `session_ended_wrong_codes` is written to the security log, and **one** notice goes to every address of the account (new mail `session_ended_wrong_codes`, no link, editable like the others; 19 mails now). A request of the same session that was already running is refused before it is checked or counted (it ends the session again, no second notice). A correct code deletes the row; a format error and a wrong password count nowhere. The next page of the login shows "Aus Sicherheitsgründen wurden Sie abgemeldet: Es wurde mehrmals ein falscher Code eingegeben. Bitte melden Sie sich erneut an." (the message names the cause, so an honest person who mistyped five times understands it). Rows of sessions that never ended cleanly are removed after 30 days (when the next failure is recorded) and with the account. The budget of the account stays in force **only at the login step**. *(Changed in block 10: the passwords count for the session too, see below.)* **Consequence:** a person holding one session can try at most five codes per session; they cannot create sessions without the password. A wrong code of the confirmations no longer locks the login (no Axes entry), so the honest owner can log in at once after the session ended. **L-1:** the success path of "phone lost" is one `transaction.atomic()` under the row lock of the account: the state (`requires_second_factor`, a second factor present) is checked on the locked row **before** the code is consumed, then the form (password, code) and `totp.disable` run in the same transaction; a refusal of the removal rolls the code (and its notice and event) back, and of two parallel posts with the same code exactly one wins while the other is refused unseen (tests with a promotion between the start and the check, with a removal in between and with threads). **L-2:** the event `two_factor_removed_phone_lost` (kept for the long period; migration 0022) replaces the detail of the log line. **L-3/L-5:** this entry, SECURITY.md and the comments in `forms.py` were aligned (no recovery code in the profile, `check_password` without a setter only after a valid proof, "same message" instead of "no oracle"). **L-4:** tests for the paused budget with the right password and the right code (lockout page, nothing removed), for the second factor removed or the account promoted between the start and the check, and for M-1. **Also (owner, block 9):** the first code of the setup is a plain text field like every other field (no `inputmode`, no `maxlength`; the label "Code aus der App" stays), and the way back in the "phone lost" form is called "Abbrechen" like everywhere else.
* **Block 10 (delta review of block 9, 2026-10-15).** **M-1 (corrected):** the count per session hung on the key of the session, which Django renews (`cycle_key`, for example when the login form is posted in a logged-in session, or on a landing page of a link): the count started again at 0 and a holder of the session and the password could guess without end. It now hangs on a random **`confirmation_counter_id`** that the server writes into the session once (at the login; for a session that has none, at the first use, with the current key, which is the same for parallel requests) and that stays when the key is renewed; the table keeps the keyed digest of it, read also with the fallback keys of `SECRET_KEY_FALLBACKS` (**L-b**: a row found under an older key is moved to the current one). The browser cannot choose it (tests). **Cap of the account:** 15 wrong entries over **all** sessions in 24 hours (table `ConfirmationFailure`: account and time) end **all** sessions of the account (`end_all_sessions`), write the event `all_sessions_ended`, send **one** notice to every address (new mail "alle Sitzungen beendet", 20 mails now) and start the count again; it **does not pause the login** and the login budget is untouched. A request that was already running when the sessions ended finds the session hash changed under the lock (`confirmation_refused`) and is refused unseen, so a flood of parallel requests cannot go on after the end. **M-2 (corrected):** wrong **passwords** in the confirmations (state B in every form, the password card in state B, the password part of "phone lost") count in the same way (same count, same limit of five, same consequence) and **no longer reach Axes, the IP counter or the lockout**: `second_factor.verify_password` has no failed-login signal any more (all its callers are confirmations of a logged-in person; the login itself is unchanged under Axes). A lockout that failed logins caused still refuses the check. A right password starts the count of the session again, except in "phone lost" (`reset_on_success=False`), where the code follows and decides; otherwise a right password between wrong codes would reset the count and the code could be guessed without a limit. The honest statement: **a holder of a session can neither trigger the budget of the codes nor Axes; at most they end their own session (five wrong entries) or all sessions (15 in 24 hours); a holder of a session and the password can still guess the code, five tries per session and 15 per 24 hours, and gets no new session without the password and the code.** **L-a:** a normal logout deletes the count of the session; the end by wrong entries keeps it (it refuses a request that was already running); the clean-up of old rows is the command `cleanup_confirmation_failures`, part of `run_scheduled_tasks` (not done any more when a failure is recorded). **L-c:** tests for the renewal of the key (login form, landing page of a link, a session without an identifier), the cap, a request that was running when the sessions ended, parallel requests with threads (one session with and without an identifier, many sessions, passwords) and the rotation of the secret key (`test_session_limit_review.py`). The message of the logout names codes **and** passwords ("… falscher Code oder ein falsches Passwort …"). Adapted tests that expected the Axes lockout for wrong passwords in the profile are listed in the report of the block.
* **Accepted low points of the final delta review (decision of the team lead, 2026-10-15; no critical, high or medium finding was left).** The loop of reviews ends here on purpose. These four points are known, judged small and **accepted as residual risk**, to be taken up with the next change of this area: (L-1) `verify_password` and `verify_second_factor(per_session=True)` do not themselves check that the caller is the logged-in person (all four callers are views of logged-in people; a future call from a path without a login would need the guard `request.user.pk == user.pk`); (L-2) when Axes already refuses the request because of an existing lockout, the check still sends the failed-login signal, which counts against the IP address of the session holder only (no new ability for them; on a shared address it can hit the owner); the sentence "no failed-login signal" is to be read as "no failure by a wrong entry"; (L-3) the login with the right password in a logged-in session also starts the session count again for accounts without a second factor (the password is their only proof anyway); only the tests for accounts with a second factor exist; (L-4) the clean-up of the counts of the sessions runs only through `run_scheduled_tasks` (without the scheduler the rows stay until the account is deleted; data minimisation, no way to abuse). Source: final delta review of the security reviewer, read by the team lead.
* Security implications for the review: (1) the shape check as a possible oracle or weakening of the budget; (2) the recovery code as the only proof; (3) the session-hash comparison under the lock instead of a second code check (race with a parallel change); (4) no value of the code field in the page; (5) the pause message when the budget is used up (the person cannot confirm anything in that time and goes through the support, D-004).

## D-063: Tests for the Login After the Registration and the Five-Minute Window; Mail Greeting Postponed

* Date: 2026-10-15
* Status: tests added (no application code changed); the greeting by name (R-13) is **not implemented** and stays open (**implemented later in D-068**).
* Context: the security review of D-055 (finding 5) left two test gaps open. The project owner then limited the scope of this work unit: finish the tests, and implement the mail greeting only if it is nearly done.
* Done (tests only; the code of D-055 was read and no defect was found):
  * `test_registration.py`, `ConcurrentCompletionTests.test_two_simultaneous_submissions_activate_the_account_once`: of two simultaneous completions of one registration, exactly one browser is logged in, as the new account; the other holds neither `_auth_user_id` nor the remembered time of a login (`authenticated_at`), and a request for the profile is redirected.
  * `test_recent_authentication.py`, `CrossAccountWindowTests` and `RegistrationInABrowserOfAnotherAccountTests`: a fresh login of account A gives account B no window (separate browsers, both directions); a started setup belongs to one account and cannot be finished by another; switching the account in the same browser ends the first session (Django's `login()` flushes it, so neither the time nor other session data of the first account stay) and starts a new time for the second account; the password step of an account with a second factor in a browser that is logged in as another account neither starts nor extends a window and logs nobody in (the code page redirects a logged-in browser, so the code is not even checked); after a logout the next account's password step leaves no time in the session; completing a registration in a browser that is logged in as another account replaces the session and the time.
  * Verified by reading: every path that ends the user of a session uses `logout()` (a flush); `login()` of Django flushes the session when another account is logged in. The time `authenticated_at` is not bound to an account id; it is safe because the session never changes its account without a flush. A future code path that changes the account of a session without `login()` or `logout()` would need to bind the time to the account (not done: no such path exists).
* Open (postponed by the project owner's scope decision, nothing of it is in the code; **superseded: implemented in D-068, 2026-10-16**, with the changes decided there): the greeting by name in the security notifications (R-13). Design notes for the later work, not yet decided with the owner: a new placeholder `{greeting}` (the neutral "Guten Tag" or "Guten Tag First Last"), offered only in the notification mails to the addresses of the account (`MailJob.NOTIFICATION_KINDS`), never in the mails to unverified or foreign addresses (registration, new address, second address, existing account); the name is captured when the job is queued (the account may be deleted before the worker runs, so the job would need a new field, i.e. a migration) and only for addresses that belong to the account at that time (not for a removed second address or a former login address); control and format characters and line breaks are removed; a name that looks like a web or email address falls back to the neutral greeting; the default templates would start with the placeholder, which changes the message IDs of about 14 templates in the catalogue; a saved text without the placeholder keeps working; the preview and the test email would use the name of the administrator.
* Sources: `docs/DECISIONS.md` D-055 (A1, A3 and the review of 2026-10-14); Django 6.1.2 `django.contrib.auth.login()` and `logout()` (source read); the tests named above.

## D-064: Outcome-Oriented Deployment Instructions for Stage and Production

* Date: 2026-10-15
* Status: Documentation workflow revised at the project owner's request; live verification of the existing stage setup and the revised update procedure remains open. No infrastructure choice or security exception is approved by this entry.
* Context: the owner reported that stage had been set up, but the setup agent found the deployment document too dependent on technical questions. The owner requested review of an alternative instance runbook, consolidation into `docs/DEPLOYMENT.md` and deletion of the alternative. The procedure must cover production too, although current work targets stage.
* Decision: `docs/DEPLOYMENT.md`, section 0.5, is the entry point for a deployment request. Distinguish an update from initial installation; reconstruct and persist a non-secret instance record from the existing authorised setup. Reuse prior decisions and inspect technical facts rather than asking the owner about tables, privileges, proxy headers or migration aliases. Verify both CI variants for the exact SHA directly. Stage without an explicit revision uses the configured release branch resolved once to a SHA; production uses the release accepted on stage and requires explicit per-deployment approval. Existing gates remain binding.
* Update procedure: quiesce all application writers before changing live code/packages/schema, wait for the importer rather than interrupt it, take and validate a pre-update backup on stage as well as production, inspect and apply ordinary non-destructive migrations, refresh static files, restart all affected processes, verify and persist the result. Destructive migrations, restores, security exceptions and missing owner policies remain approval boundaries.
* Alternative runbook review: retain its clear separation of operational steps and its idea of reusing installation facts, not its unattended-password pseudo-terminal script, fake privacy URL default, automatic self-signed TLS fallback, stage backup omission or assumed one-shot importer. `setup_initial_administrator` explicitly requires hidden interactive human input; `process_address_imports` loops unless `--once` is passed. A code-only checkout rollback is not proof of schema or package compatibility. The alternative file is removed after consolidation.
* Verified by repository inspection: `leg_platform/accounts/management/commands/setup_initial_administrator.py`, `leg_platform/addresses/management/commands/process_address_imports.py`, `leg_platform/import_settings.py`, and the existing deployment, installation, operations and security documents. No server access, deployment, restore or live security check was performed for this documentation revision.
* Documentation review: the `security-reviewer` reviewed the revised workflow read-only and found an ambiguous stage-acceptance requirement for explicitly named production revisions. Section 0.5 now requires the same accepted full SHA for every production deployment; a focused second review confirmed the correction and preservation of per-deployment approval. Hash/CI lookup and recording plain-language acceptance are operator tasks, not owner tasks. `git diff --check` and editor diagnostics passed for the revised documents; runtime/deployment tests were not run for this documentation-only change.
* Note 2026-10-16 (D-074): the owner stated that Infomaniak operates stage and production; the rules of this entry are unchanged.
* Sources: project owner instructions of 2026-10-15; `CONDUCT.md`; `AGENTS.md` sections 3, 8 and 9; `docs/SECURITY.md` sections 7 to 10; `docs/OPERATIONS.md` section 2; D-005, D-019, D-020, D-027, D-052 and D-053. This entry supplements D-053's historical proposal; it does not claim that its unverified installation examples have been executed.

## D-065: Address Suggestions and Address Notice Redesign, Approximate Matching in One Postal Code

* Date: 2026-10-15
* Status: implemented; **partly superseded by D-066 (2026-10-15, owner decisions): the registration details step now has the suggestions, the source hint, the info notice, the noscript text, the texts for an unavailable, old or limited directory and the one-time notice after saving no longer exist; the places are marked "Superseded by D-066" below**; the security reviewer reviewed it (2026-10-15: no critical, high or medium findings, four low findings L-1 to L-5 and one informational note, all corrected as recorded under "Corrections after the security review" below; the delta review of the corrections (2026-10-15) found no critical, high or medium findings; its low points N-1 (deployment text without a switch) and N-4 (stale status) are corrected, N-2 (the time budget covers only the Python loop; no statement time limit decided) and N-3 (only the ten best-ranked streets are looked up) are accepted and recorded); not verified against the real directory, on touch devices or with a screen reader.
* Context: designer findings R-04 (the suggestion list stands far from the street field), R-05 (a typing error finds no suggestion) and R-21 (the warning is far from the fields, premature and unstyled) from the review in D-054. The designer's specification (`address-ux-spec.md`, outside the repository) is input data; its claims were checked in the code (the list was placed after the whole street row; `lookup(check)` returned "not found" for an incomplete address; the `change` event called `clear()`; the warning after saving went into the card header). Owner questions answered by the main session: (1) after saving the warning is a one-time flash, not a persistent status (**superseded by D-066: no notice after saving**); (2) the registration details step is not part of this work (UX-004 stays open) (**superseded by D-066: it is in scope and done**); (3) the swisstopo attribution stays visible as a hint under the street field (**superseded by D-066: no source in the user interface**); (4) approximate matching as below.
* Decision, placement and behaviour (`docs/DESIGN.md`, section 6):
  * The suggestions are included inside the street field (`accounts/_field.html`, new optional context `after`), so that the panel sits directly under the street input; the source hint and a visually hidden live region follow it (`addresses/widget.html`). The notice is its own always-present, empty element after the postal code and town row (`addresses/widget_notice.html`, `role="status"`). Without JavaScript both stay empty; the form is unchanged. (**Superseded by D-066:** there is no source hint and no noscript text any more; the live region stays.)
  * `static/js/address-directory.js`: `suggest` (after 300 ms, only for input in the street and the house number) and `check` (1000 ms after the last input in any address field, at form load, and after a choice) are separate requests with their own sequence number and `AbortController` (stale answers are dropped). The panel is not cleared while typing; it is replaced or hidden when an answer arrives. The notice shows only for a complete address (street, four-digit postal code, town, country CH): `alert-warning` for "available and not found", `alert-info` with the server text for every other status (**superseded by D-066: the info variant is removed; only the not-found warning remains**). It never takes the focus, never blocks saving, and its id is added to `aria-describedby` of the four fields while it is visible (the existing tokens are kept and restored). No `check` on `change` or `blur` (a notice that appears then shifts the Save button between mouse-down and mouse-up). `mousedown` on the panel is prevented so that the street field keeps the focus; choosing an option is the only action that changes fields. Keyboard: options have `tabindex="-1"`; ArrowDown/ArrowUp/Escape as in the specification. Options and texts are built with `textContent` only; the texts come from `data-*` attributes with `%(name)s` placeholders replaced in a single regular-expression pass (a typed text that contains a placeholder is never searched again); no inline script or style, so the Content Security Policy is unchanged.
  * After saving (`ProfileEditView`): the warning is produced once, only for `match is False` and status `available` (an unavailable, old or limited directory is not shown after saving). It carries the tag `address-warning` next to `card-personal`; `render_profile` takes it out of the card header and `_personal.html` renders it as an extra `dd` of the Address row (`profile-row-alert`, with the link "Check address" to `?edit=personal#card-personal`). The header message is then "Saved. Please check your address below." (it keeps the focus, `profile-focus.js`), otherwise "Saved.". The tag parsing now splits the tags by white space. Deviation from the specification: the notice is a second `dd` of the row instead of a `div` child of the row (a `div` inside the `div` that groups `dt` and `dd` is not valid HTML; several `dd` per `dt` are). (**Superseded by D-066: removed completely, together with `ADDRESS_WARNING_TAG`, `check_address`, the tag, the CSS and the texts; saving shows "Saved." and makes no lookup. The tag parsing by white space stays.**)
  * The old msgid "This address was not found…" is replaced by the longer text of the specification; the msgids "Address suggestions" and "Official address directory" are no longer used and were removed from the catalogue.
* Decision, approximate matching (`addresses/services.py`, `approximate_matches`):
  * The JSON of the lookup gets `kind`: `"prefix"` or `"approximate"` (always present in `lookup()` answers; the error answers of the view are unchanged and the script treats a missing `kind` as `"prefix"`). The answer contains directory data only, as before; the view, the CSRF protection, POST-only, the 4 KiB body limit, the rate limit and `no-store` are unchanged.
  * Used only when the prefix search returned nothing, the mode is `suggest` and the postal code is exactly four digits (`clean_input` already allows digits only). The typed street must have 3 to 64 characters (`APPROXIMATE_MIN_LENGTH`, `APPROXIMATE_MAX_LENGTH`).
  * Bounded candidate set: one query for the distinct streets of the current generation and that postal code (index `addr_generation_postal_street`), ordered by street and limited to `APPROXIMATE_STREET_LIMIT + 1` = 1001 rows. If the postal code has more than 1000 such streets, no approximate result is returned. The house number is **not** part of the candidate query (security review L-3: a prefix filter on it is not covered by the index and could make the database read every row of the postal code without the distinct-street limit ever triggering); it only restricts the per-street address queries below, each bounded by the indexed street equality. The `LIMIT` bounds the distinct streets returned, **not the rows the database reads**; the earlier comment "never a scan of more rows" overstated this and was corrected.
