A street that has no address with the typed house number gives no rows (the ten best streets are looked up; a lower-ranked street that has the house number is not offered). No `LIKE '%x%'` and no cross-postal-code query. The town text is ignored (the postal code fixes it).
  * Further hard bounds, all failing closed (no approximate result, status stays `available`; review L-1, L-2): the typed street must have 3 to 64 characters **before and after folding** (a street of only combining marks, or `a` plus combining marks, folds to fewer than 3 characters and finds nothing; an empty fold is never a "prefix" of every street); a candidate whose folded text is longer than 2 × 64 characters is skipped (the importer allows 255 characters and folding can expand a character up to 18 times, so the 64-character bound alone was nominal); at most `APPROXIMATE_MAX_RATIO_CALLS` = 300 full `ratio()` calls and `APPROXIMATE_TIME_BUDGET` = 0.25 s (`time.monotonic()`, checked before each candidate) per request, exceeding either returns nothing. The values 300 and 0.25 s are heuristics chosen so that the measured worst case below (0.6 s for 1000 synthetic candidates) is cut to roughly a third; they are unverified against the real directory.
  * Matching in Python with `difflib.SequenceMatcher` (standard library, no new dependency), on `casefold()`ed, accent-stripped (`unicodedata`, NFKD without combining marks) text; `autojunk=False`; the typed street is the second sequence (set once, because the matcher caches data about it), `real_quick_ratio()` and `quick_ratio()` (upper bounds of `ratio()`) skip most candidates; threshold `ratio() >= 0.75` (`APPROXIMATE_THRESHOLD`, a heuristic, not a standard value; the tests show accepted and rejected examples). `ratio()` is 2.0·M/T (M matching characters, T characters of both strings).
  * If a candidate street starts with the typed text (compared case- and accent-insensitively), the first search missed it only because it also filtered by the town text or by accents; these are returned as `kind: "prefix"` (so that the note "No street starting with … was found" is never untrue) and the fuzzy matches are not added. Otherwise the streets are ranked by similarity (best first, then street), at most `RESULT_LIMIT` (10) streets are looked up, one bounded query each (`distinct`, limit = remaining slots), ordered by street, house number, postal code and town; the result has at most 10 rows. The client shows 5 and a line "More addresses match…" if there are more.
  * Cost, informal local measurement (Python 3.14.8, not a benchmark): 1000 realistic street names against a typed street take about 3 ms; an artificial worst case (1000 candidates of 64 characters over a two-letter alphabet, all passing the quick bounds) took about 0.6 s. The candidates are directory data, not user input; the user input is bounded to 64 characters and to the 4 KiB request.
* Security implications (for the review): the endpoint has no new input (same fields, same limits) and no new data in the answer (directory rows, no account data); the added work per request is at most one candidate query (1001 rows), at most 10 small address queries and the comparison above (bounded by the CPU bounds), inside the existing 60/minute per client IP and 600/minute per instance budget; the directory is read through the existing SELECT-only connection. The client builds the DOM with `textContent` (no HTML injection); the notice text comes from the server (translated fixed texts). The message tag is a fixed constant and not user-controlled. Nothing in the script blocks or alters a form submission. The one-time warning is not stored beyond the Django message storage of the session.
* Not verified: behaviour against the real directory and its real postal-code sizes (the limit 1000, the threshold 0.75, the bounds 300 calls and 0.25 s are unverified for real data), **the MariaDB query plan, index use and collation behaviour of the candidate and per-street queries (open operator step with the `EXPLAIN` statements in `docs/DEPLOYMENT.md`, section 10.2; the tests run on SQLite and check the SQL text only)**, real touch devices, screen readers, dark mode contrast (not computed), a real browser run of the whole profile page (the script was exercised in a browser with a stub of the endpoint only); the single-pass placeholder replacement was not run in a browser or in Node (not installed); only a static test checks its form.
* Corrections after the security review (2026-10-15):
  * L-1: length bounds apply to the folded text too; tests for combining marks only and `a` plus combining marks (`services.py`, `test_directory.py`).
  * L-2: skip of overlong candidates, call cap and time budget, each with a test.
  * L-3: house number removed from the candidate query, comment corrected, plan check recorded above and in `docs/DEPLOYMENT.md`. A statement time limit (`max_statement_time`) for the SELECT-only account is a possible defence in depth, not decided.
  * L-4: documented in `docs/INSTALLATION.md` ("Trusted Proxies"): the per-IP limit and the polling need the correct `AXES_IPWARE_PROXY_COUNT`. No code change. Not changed: the load-time `check` is not skipped for an unchanged address, because at load it also fills the panel for an address that is not found (`atLoad`) and the notice of a stale or unavailable directory; skipping it needs a client-side comparison with the saved address and a design decision. The global bucket row is locked on every request (D-052 design), accepted. Noted, not changed.
  * L-5: added tests: failing candidate or per-street query after a successful prefix query (fails closed, logged without detail), accent-only difference with `kind` "prefix", escaping of the post-save notice, the message is not shown to another session or another user (the session that saved still sees it once), boundaries 3 and 64 characters. The script tests remain static.
  * Informational: the placeholder replacement of the script is single-pass. The note that the flash message cookie belongs to the browser, not the user, is accepted: the text contains no personal data and the same holds for all messages of the project. The widget `data-*` attributes with a `"` in a future translation: not changed (no test per language yet).
* Sources: Python 3.14 `difflib.SequenceMatcher` (documentation `https://docs.python.org/3/library/difflib.html` and the docstrings of the installed 3.14.8: `ratio`, `quick_ratio`, `real_quick_ratio`, `set_seq2` caching, `autojunk`), `str.casefold` and `unicodedata` (`https://docs.python.org/3/library/stdtypes.html#str.casefold`, `https://docs.python.org/3/library/unicodedata.html`); Django `extra_tags` of messages are split by white space in the profile view (verified by the tests that tag two values); the `LIMIT` of the sliced candidate query is verified by the SQL captured in `test_approximate_candidates_are_bounded_and_nothing_is_returned_above_the_bound` (Django documentation not re-read for this entry); `docs/DESIGN.md` section 6; D-052 (the lookup endpoint, its limits and indexes) and D-054; the designer's specification of 2026-10-15 and the owner decisions above.

## D-066: Address Suggestions Wherever an Address Is Edited; No Directory Source and No Directory Warning in the User Interface; Only While Editing

* Date: 2026-10-15
* Status: four binding decisions of the project owner (2026-10-15), implemented by the developer. Supersedes the parts of D-065 that are marked there and of D-049 and D-054 where named below. Reviewed by the security reviewer on 2026-10-15: no critical, high or medium findings; the low findings are corrected as recorded below. One question for the owner was open (the wording of the swisstopo terms against "only in the privacy notice and the imprint", see "Owner question"; closed 2026-10-16, D-074).
* Decisions of the owner (translated into English; the owner's German wording is not copied):
  1. **Everywhere an address is entered.** The suggestions and the correction ("Did you mean ...?"; choosing it takes over the address) are offered in every form in which a postal address is entered. This includes the registration step "Your details" (UX-004, now in scope) and the profile edit form.
  2. **No source in the user interface.** No visible reference to swisstopo, to the "official address directory" or any attribution appears in the user interface (hint, notes, texts, Swiss German translations). The attribution belongs to the privacy notice and the imprint, which are owner content (D-022); the software shows no attribution of its own.
  3. **No warning when the directory cannot answer.** If the directory is missing, unreachable, old, rate limited, answers invalidly, fails, or the browser has no JavaScript, the person sees no warning and no information, neither while typing, nor at load, nor after saving: the person cannot do anything about it. On the server, as before, database errors (a failing directory status or lookup query, a failing limit check) and an invalid value in the limit check (`ValueError`) are logged with fixed texts only (a warning without address content); all other states (a missing or old directory, an unresolvable client address, which is refused, a 429 and a 400) write no log line, and no logging was added (security review, L-1). The operator page of the administration may keep its status. The only notice that remains is "not found in the directory" for a complete address while editing (the directory is available and the address is not in it), and the suggestion panel ("Did you mean ...?").
  4. **Only while the address can be edited.** Suggestions, "Did you mean" and the not-found notice appear only in an edit form. In the profile overview (read mode) nothing is shown and no lookup is made; they appear only after the person clicked "Edit". The one-time notice after saving, its link "Check address" and the header text "Saved. Please check your address below." are removed ("Saved." again).
* Inventory of the forms with address fields (grep for `street`, `postal_code`, `town` in forms, templates and the admin, 2026-10-15):

  | Form | Where | Suggestions |
  | --- | --- | --- |
  | `RegistrationDetailsForm` | registration step 3 (`registration_complete.html`) | **Yes** (new). The form has no country field; the country is fixed to Switzerland (K15, `clean()`). The include passes `address_country_value="CH"`, the widget renders `data-country-value="CH"` instead of `data-country-id`, and the script sends `country=CH` explicitly. The lookup stays Swiss only: the server answers only for country `CH` as before. |
  | `ProfileForm` | profile card "Personal details", edit state | **Yes** (already, D-065); the script is loaded only when this form is shown (`edit == "personal"` and the card is rendered). |
  | `UserAdminForm` | Django admin, change page of an account (`UserAdmin.fieldsets`) | **No, not covered.** The page is the generated change form of `ModelAdmin` (fieldsets, no custom form template for the address block), not a custom form of the portal. A widget there needs an override of the admin fieldset template or a script that moves markup, and the admin keeps the Django look (D-054, D-061). Staff correct the address of an existing account there for support; this is a closed, small user group. Recorded as not covered; the owner may decide otherwise (open question 2). |

  No other form has address fields (the grep found none in other forms or templates).
* Implementation:
  * `static/js/address-directory.js`: only the not-found warning of an available directory is ever shown (`data.status === "available" && data.match === false` with a warning text); a failed request, a non-success status, a non-JSON or invalid answer, `missing`, `unavailable`, old data or a rate limit show nothing. The `data-unavailable` text, the info variant and the noscript text are gone. A form without a country field names its fixed country in `data-country-value`; with neither a country field nor a fixed value the widget does nothing.
  * `addresses/services.py`: the JSON answer carries a `warning` only for a check that finds no match in an available directory that is not old. `attribution` is removed from the JSON and from the code (the script never read it; the admin page showed it, now not). `directory_status()` still returns an operator text for the administration page (`The address directory is unavailable.`, `The address directory is more than 92 days old.`; the sentences "You can continue with the address as entered." are removed because the page is for the operator). `check_address()` is removed (its only caller was the post-save notice). `addresses/views.py`: the 400 and 429 answers keep their status, `status` value and headers and carry an empty `warning`. CSRF, POST only, the 4 KiB limit, the rate limit and `no-store, private` are unchanged.
  * Profile: `ProfileEditView.post` saves and shows "Saved." only; `ADDRESS_WARNING_TAG`, `address_warning`, the Address row notice, `.profile-row-alert` and `.profile-row .alert` (CSS) are removed. The registration view no longer reads `directory_status()`; the note "Your address could not be checked and is saved as you entered it." is removed.
  * Removed msgids (and their Swiss German texts): the source name, the source hint, the noscript text, the unavailable, old, limited and invalid texts of the user path, "Check address", "Saved. Please check your address below.", and the registration note. New operator-only msgids: the two sentences of `directory_status()` above. The not-found text stays: "This address was not found in the official directory. ...". It says "official directory", not the name of the source; the owner decides whether this wording stays (open question 3).
* A directory that is more than 92 days old counts as "cannot answer" (owner decision 3 lists "stale"): the not-found notice is then not sent either, because the data may lack a new address. This is the developer's reading of the owner's list; the earlier rule (D-065) showed the not-found notice for an old directory too (status "available"). Open question 4.
* Referrer Policy of the registration step (D-054, R-01): the lookup is a same-origin POST from the page `/register/complete/`, which is not a token page; its `Referrer-Policy` is the project default `same-origin`, so a browser sends `Origin: http://<host>` and not `Origin: null`. Verified against a running development server (below): the POST with the token of the form and the own `Origin` is answered 200; the same POST with `Origin: null` (what `no-referrer` would cause), without a token, or with a foreign `Origin` is refused with 403. Nothing was added to `CSRF_TRUSTED_ORIGINS`. The Content Security Policy is unchanged (`script-src 'self'`; no inline script or style is added).
* Security implications for the review: (1) an anonymous person now reaches the lookup from the registration step, which the endpoint already allowed (D-052: CSRF with the token of the form, 60 requests per minute per client IP, 600 per instance); the endpoint, its input limits and its answer (directory data only) are unchanged, tests show the CSRF refusal, the rate limit (the 61st request is refused) and the unchanged headers for an anonymous client. (2) Less information leaves the server: the answer has no `attribution` and no text for an unavailable directory. (3) The widget sends the fixed country `CH` in the registration; the server decides, as before. (4) Removing the post-save lookup removes one directory read per save.
* Verified (2026-10-15): (a) official terms of use of swisstopo, fetched from `https://www.swisstopo.admin.ch/en/terms-of-use-free-geodata-and-geoservices` (the page that `https://www.swisstopo.admin.ch/ogd-conditions` redirects to; version "1.3.2021"; this closes the "could not be fetched" note of D-049): "A reference to the source is mandatory. In the case of digital or analogue representations and publications, as well as in the case of dissemination, one of the following source references must be attached in any case", followed by the six texts listed in D-049; "A different source reference must be used for the following geodata. The exact wording can be found in the documentation of the corresponding geodata" (the list of such geodata is not part of the fetched text); the page does not say where the reference must stand. The dataset page `https://www.swisstopo.admin.ch/en/official-directory-of-building-addresses` links to these terms and names no other source reference (its collapsed "Product information" part could not be read). The STAC record (`https://data.geo.admin.ch/api/stac/v0.9/collections/ch.swisstopo.amtliches-gebaeudeadressverzeichnis`) still says `"license":"proprietary"` and links to the same terms. (b) Browser behaviour: the real script in headless Chrome against the real registration details page (captured from a running development server) and a stub of the endpoint: the panel with two options appears, a choice fills street, house number, postal code and town, a complete unknown address shows the not-found warning (and `aria-describedby`), and for `unavailable`, `missing`, old data, HTTP 500, HTTP 429 and an invalid JSON answer nothing is shown; the requests carried `country=CH`, the CSRF header and the own origin. Screenshots of the page with the panel and the notice (desktop width) showed the same styling as in the profile. (c) Server side against the running development server with the synthetic directory and an anonymous session: see "Referrer Policy" above.
* Owner question (**closed 2026-10-16, D-074**: the owner settled that the source reference goes into the privacy notice and the imprint, and that the texts of both are written by the operator elsewhere, D-022). The earlier text follows unchanged: the terms require a source reference "attached" to digital representations, publications and dissemination, without naming the place. Decision 2 puts it only into the privacy notice and the imprint. Whether that satisfies the terms is a legal reading that the developer cannot make; it is **not silently dropped** but also not assumed to be sufficient. The owner (or the operator, with swisstopo, whose contact is named on the terms page) should confirm it, or the reference must be shown in the user interface again. Until then the privacy notice and the imprint of every instance must contain one of the six permitted texts (for example "Bundesamt für Landestopografie swisstopo") for the address suggestions; this is an open point for the content of D-022.
* Corrections after the security review (2026-10-15, low findings): L-1 the wording of decision 3 now says that database errors (and invalid values in the limit check) are logged with fixed texts only and other states are not logged (code checked: `services.directory_status`, `services.lookup` and `limits.allow_request` log only on `DatabaseError`, `ConnectionDoesNotExist` or `ValueError`); no logging added. L-2 new test `RegistrationCompletionOriginTests.test_the_address_lookup_of_the_details_step_is_accepted_with_the_origin_of_a_browser`: the lookup POST with the token of the details page and `Origin: http://testserver` gives 200, with `Origin: null` 403. L-3 the "no lookup" tests of the profile overview, the profile save (two modules) now also patch `leg_platform.addresses.views.lookup` and `views.allow_request` and assert they are not called (the view imports `lookup` by name). L-4 `addresses.limits.allow_request` now calls the read-only `rate_limits.exhausted()` before `consume_all`, as `mail_queue.requests_refused` does, so that a client whose limit is used up causes only reads (no locks, no writes); the answers are unchanged. This does not prevent the first-request bucket rows of many distinct client addresses (each new address still gets its bucket rows once). New tests: the fail-closed test of the adapter also lets `exhausted` raise `DatabaseError` and `ValueError` (refused, `consume_all` not called, same log line; N-1), and `RateLimitDatabaseTests` runs the real counters (60 lookups allowed and the 61st refused; 5 import calls allowed and the 6th refused; a refused request changes no bucket, event or last-use time, so the global bucket is untouched; N-2). Still open for the operator: a statement time limit for the SELECT-only account (defence in depth, D-065 N-2).
* Other open questions: (2) the account change page of the administration is not covered (inventory above); (3) the wording "in the official directory" of the not-found notice (**closed 2026-10-15, D-067**: the notice now reads "This address was not found. Please check street, house number, postal code and town. You can still save it as entered.", approved by the designer; it names no directory); (4) the reading of "old" above.
* Not verified: a real browser run of the whole registration and profile pages with a keyboard or a screen reader (the browser check used a stub, the real script and a captured page; Edge could not be driven in this environment), real touch devices, dark mode, the 390 px width (the headless window did not go below about 500 px), and everything of D-065 that stays unverified (real directory, query plan).
* Sources: the owner decisions of 2026-10-15; the swisstopo terms of use (URL above, fetched 2026-10-15); `docs/DECISIONS.md` D-022, D-033, D-049, D-052, D-054 and D-065; Fetch standard behaviour of `Origin: null` after `Referrer-Policy: no-referrer` as recorded in D-054 R-01 (not re-read); Django 6.1.2 `CsrfViewMiddleware` (the refusal of a foreign and a `null` origin was observed on the running server, not read in the source for this entry).

## D-067: Invalid Confirmation Links Redirect to Fixed Pages; Lockout Page Not Stored; Wording Corrections; `requests` Minimum Settled

* Date: 2026-10-15
* Status: decided by the main session (team lead) on the verdict of the designer (P1, P3 and the optional wording, "approved"); the project owner is away. Implemented by the developer (work unit A). **Not yet reviewed by the security reviewer**; the points that need it are under "Security implications". The designer's findings P2 (greeting by name) and P4 (plain-text mails) are not part of this work unit.
* Context: after D-054/D-055 the three confirmation flows (address change, stop of an address change, removal of the second factor) still rendered their "Link no longer valid" page directly under the token URL (the registration and the password reset already redirected, D-058). Such a page needed the special handling `request.no_referrer_page` (D-054, security review finding 1), and the answer under the token URL was a different resource for a valid and an invalid link. The designer's report is input data: it was checked in the code (`accounts/views.py`, `urls.py`, the five templates) before anything was changed.
* Decisions and implementation:
  * **A1, invalid links redirect.** In `EmailChangeVerifyView`, `EmailChangeStopVerifyView` and `TwoFactorResetVerifyView` every invalid, expired, used or replaced token (and an account that is not active, a stop link without an open request, a removal link without a usable second factor) answers `302` to a fixed page of the flow; the three confirmation views (`EmailChangeConfirmView`, `EmailChangeStopConfirmView`, `TwoFactorResetConfirmView`) do the same when no valid state (session digest) exists, and when the state is lost between the page and the button (`form_valid`). The new pages are `/email-change/expired/` (`email_change_invalid`), `/email-change/stop-expired/` (`email_change_stop_invalid`) and `/two-factor-reset/expired/` (`two_factor_reset_invalid`), one view `LinkNoLongerValidView` (`TemplateView`, GET and HEAD only, `never_cache`, no data read from the request, the session or the database) rendering the existing templates unchanged. The paths cannot be taken for a token route: `email-change/stop/<slug:token>/` needs a slash after `stop`, so `/email-change/stop-expired/` does not match it (the designer's alternative `/email-change/stop/expired/` would, and is documented as a token route in a test); `expired/` is no sub-path of `verify/`. The verify views keep `TokenPageMixin` (`Referrer-Policy: no-referrer`, `never_cache`); the confirmation views are not token pages and keep the project default policy. The answer (status, `Location`, `Referrer-Policy`, `Cache-Control`, body) does not depend on the cause, and the token is in no part of it. No flash message is used (designer: the recovery step differs per flow, a flash disappears on reload).
  * **`request.no_referrer_page` removed.** It was set by the `no_referrer` decorator and read in `base.html` (header logout form) and `_frame_menu.html` (menu logout form). Verified dead: every view that uses the decorator (`TokenPageMixin`: the five verify views) now only redirects, so no template is rendered in a response with that policy; the "Link no longer valid" pages extend `entry_base.html`, which replaces the block `body` of `base.html` and therefore never contained the header or the menu (the designer's remark that the header forms appear on the new pages is not correct for the entry shell; no form that posts is on these pages). The two conditions in the templates and the attribute are removed. The class of problem (a form on a page with `no-referrer` gets `Origin: null` and is refused by the CSRF check, D-054) cannot arise on a token URL any more.
  * **Log redaction.** `TOKEN_URL_PATTERN` (`leg_platform/log_filters.py`) now also matches `/email-change/stop/<token>/`. This was a gap that existed before this unit: the stop link of the notice is a token URL, but the pattern covered only `.../verify/<token>/`, so a "Not Found" or request line of a stop link was logged with the token, and the session timeout middleware (`session_timeout.py`, which uses the same pattern) would have put the token into the `next` parameter of the login URL after an expired session. Tests: redaction of the stop URL, no redaction of the fixed and confirmation pages, no `next` for the stop link. The new fixed pages carry no token and need no pattern.
  * **A2, wording.** The lead texts of the registration and password reset "Link no longer valid" pages now point a person who has finished already to the login (designer's wording, EN source and DE text; the button layout is unchanged). The two pages had shared one msgid; they now have one each.
  * **A3, not-found notice.** "This address was not found. Please check street, house number, postal code and town. You can still save it as entered." / "Diese Adresse wurde nicht gefunden. Bitte prüfen Sie Strasse, Hausnummer, PLZ und Ort. Sie können sie trotzdem so speichern, wie Sie sie eingegeben haben." (`addresses/services.py`, `.po`, tests, `docs/DESIGN.md` section 6). Closes open question 3 of D-066. The notice no longer names a directory.
  * **A4, wording.** The English source "an recovery code" is corrected to "a recovery code" in the `two_factor_enabled` mail source and in the two messages of the login with a recovery code (same defect). This is safe for D-039: the editable texts are stored as the final Swiss German text and the default is rendered from the template through the catalogue (`email_texts.default_texts`, `compose`); the msgid is only a key into the catalogue and no msgid is stored. The `msgstr` is unchanged, so the sent text, the stored defaults ("a text equal to the default is stored as empty") and every saved administrator text are unaffected; no migration. The "Link no longer valid" page of the removal of the second factor said "an die LEG" while the mails say "an die Betreiber"; it now uses "die Betreiber" (both msgstr).
  * **A5, lockout page.** django-axes 8.3.1 renders the lockout page with `render(...)` and adds no `Cache-Control` header (source: `axes/helpers.py`, `get_lockout_response`). `AXES_LOCKOUT_CALLABLE` is the documented way to replace the response (https://github.com/jazzband/django-axes/blob/master/docs/4_configuration.rst, "A callable or a string path to callable that takes three arguments returns a response", read 2026-10-15; the source calls it with `(request, original_response, credentials)` and falls back to two arguments). `AXES_LOCKOUT_CALLABLE = "leg_platform.accounts.axes_handler.lockout_response"` replaces `AXES_LOCKOUT_TEMPLATE`: the function renders the same template with `AXES_HTTP_RESPONSE_CODE` (429) and calls Django's `add_never_cache_headers` (`Cache-Control: max-age=0, no-cache, no-store, must-revalidate, private`, `Expires`). It also covers the pause of the second-factor code entry, which uses the same lockout page. Consequence: the JSON answer that Axes gives to `X-Requested-With: XMLHttpRequest` and the context of Axes (`username`, `failure_limit`, cool-off) are no longer produced; the portal has no script that posts a login and the template used none of them (it shows the duration from the context processor and never the submitted value). Two other ways were considered and not taken: a project middleware that adds the header when `request.axes_locked_out` is set (one more middleware in every request and dependence on an Axes request attribute) and a subclass of `AxesMiddleware` (its asynchronous path calls the lockout response with a different signature).
  * **A6, `requests` minimum.** Official sources checked on 2026-10-15 (details in D-048, "Verified facts"): they name no supported older release line, so the minimum is **not lowered**: `requests>=2.34.2` stays; nothing was installed or tested with another version. The item is removed from the backlog in the README.
* Tests: `accounts/tests/test_invalid_link_redirect.py` (new; all five flows with seven causes each (forged, wrong length, expired, used, replaced, other purpose, account state), the answer is identical for every cause and for a logged-in person, no token in the answer, a valid link does not reach the fixed page, the three confirmation views with five kinds of broken state and with a state lost before the button, the fixed pages: 200, `no-store`, GET and HEAD only, the same content for everybody, no account data, routing), and changed: `test_token_page_forms.py`, `test_frame.py`, `test_entry_pages.py`, `test_profile.py`, `test_phase4_review.py`, `test_second_factor_reset.py`, `test_acceptance_final.py`, `test_acceptance_negative_authorization.py`, `test_usability.py`, `test_address_notice.py`, `addresses/tests/test_directory.py`, `test_translations.py`, `test_second_factor.py`, `test_session_timeout.py`, `tests/test_instance_configuration.py`. Registration and password reset (already redirecting, D-058) are covered by the same all-causes test.
* **Security review of unit A, corrections (2026-10-16; findings M-1, L-1 to L-6, one extra; all claims were checked in the code first).**
  * **Hardening, recorded (L-4).** The lockout answer for `X-Requested-With: XMLHttpRequest` is now the same HTML page as for any request. The JSON answer of django-axes 8.3.1 echoed the submitted `username` and set `Access-Control-Allow-Origin` from `AXES_ALLOWED_CORS_ORIGINS`, whose default is `"*"` (`axes/conf.py`, `axes/helpers.py`, read in the installed package). Its removal (A5) is therefore a hardening, not only a side effect; a test pins it (HTML, no `Access-Control-Allow-Origin`, no echoed username, `no-store`). Tests for `no-store` were added for the lockout of an account on the login form and for the 429 of the second-factor form after wrong codes (the lockout of an address and the pause page were covered before).
  * **M-1, the identical-answer test.** `answer()` now compares the status, the body and every header except `Date` and `Expires` (the clock; `never_cache` sets `Expires`), including `Vary` and `Content-Language` if present, and the cookies by name and attributes (without value and `expires`). For the five verify views and every cause (seven causes, and for the address change also a deactivated and an unverified account) the answer is identical, for an anonymous and for a logged-in visitor; no `Set-Cookie` is sent in any case. **One difference was found and is accepted:** the three confirmation views (L-5 below) now remove a stale state from the session, so a browser whose session held a state gets its `sessionid` cookie back with the redirect, and a browser without a state gets none (no session is created for it). The difference depends only on the session that the browser itself sent, never on the cause: all causes with a state are identical, and the answer of a visitor tells nothing that the visitor's own session does not hold. The test pins this exactly (identical without cookies for all, identical with cookies within each group).
  * **L-1, percent-encoded slashes.** The server decodes the path before routing, so a request line `GET /email-change%2Fstop/<token>/` reaches the stop view while `django.server` logs it raw. `TOKEN_URL_PATTERN` now matches `/`, `%2F` and `%2f` in every slash of the prefixes. Not covered, because it is not what the finding asked for and no production path produces it: other percent-encoded characters inside the prefix (for example `%65mail-change`); a reverse proxy log is outside the code. Tests: every token route in four encoded variants, as a message and as a request line.
  * **L-2, enumeration.** `test_instance_configuration.token_routes()` walks `get_resolver()` and returns every route with a `token` parameter; the redaction tests and the session-timeout test use it, so a new token route is covered without a change of the tests (a guard asserts that at least five routes are found; a regular-expression route with a token fails the test). The comment in `log_filters.py` now says what the test does.
  * **L-3.** The no-`next` test of `test_session_timeout.py` runs over all token routes (the five links), not two.
  * **L-5, state in the session.** The three confirmation views remove their session key whenever they redirect to the fixed page: in `dispatch` when the state is not valid, and in `form_valid` also when `confirm_link()` returns `None` (the removal of the second factor did not remove it). Not exploitable before (every use re-validates the digest in the database), hygiene only. Tests: the key is gone after a GET and a POST for every kind of broken state and after a late failure.
  * **L-6, documentation.** `docs/SECURITY.md` section 11 (the paragraph on links from an email) states the limit: body, status and headers are identical, the time of the answer is not equalised and was not measured, and the length of a token leaks only the length (no information with 256-bit tokens). The lookups differ by cause (wrong length returns before the keyed digest; the address change queries two purposes; the stop link also asks for an open request; the removal link checks the device); the tokens are looked up by a keyed digest in the database, so there is no comparison of a prefix.
  * **Extra, account state of the address change.** The code has the states `blocked`, `deactivated` and `unverified` (`User.Status`); the link is refused for each (`status != ACTIVE`). The test of the address change now has the three. An unverified account cannot be made from an active one (`AccountIntegrityError`: no customer number), so the test creates it as a registration does.
  * **Observation, recorded and not changed: `APPEND_SLASH`.** A token URL without the trailing slash gets a `301` from `CommonMiddleware` (`APPEND_SLASH` is the Django default and `CommonMiddleware` is in `MIDDLEWARE`; verified by a request in the test settings: `301`, `Location` with the token and the slash, `Referrer-Policy: same-origin`, no `Cache-Control`). The answer goes only to the requester, carries the token the requester sent, is not logged (status below 400) and no email link omits the slash (`emails.py` uses `reverse`). Accepted.
  * **Observation, recorded and not changed: `SecondFactorMiddleware`.** It runs in `process_view`, before the view. A session of a staff user or system administrator without a completed second factor, or of an account that must replace its password, is redirected (to the login, the set-up or the password change) before a verify view runs, because the verify views are not in `UNRESTRICTED_VIEWS` or `PASSWORD_CHANGE_VIEWS` (`second_factor.py`). The decision depends on the visitor's own session, not on the token, so it is no oracle; it is functional: such a person must log out or complete the step first to see the fixed page. Accepted.* Security implications (for the review): (1) the redirect gives no oracle: the same status, `Location`, headers and an empty body for every cause; the time of the answer was not compared (the database lookups differ by cause; the digest lookup is by a keyed hash as before). (2) The fixed pages are public, read nothing and show nothing of an account; a person who is logged in sees the button "To the profile" (the existing template). (3) The redirect for a valid link still stores only the digest in the session and rotates the session key, as before. (4) The lockout answer is no longer stored by a browser or a proxy; the status, the page and the text are unchanged. (5) `no_referrer_page` is gone; no page that has a form is served under `no-referrer`.
* Not verified: the pages in a real browser (the redirect, the back button after the confirmation and the fixed page); a timing comparison of the causes; the behaviour of a reverse proxy with the redirect.
* Not done (recorded): the greeting by name (designer P2, D-063) and the section 8 text of the designer's `DESIGN.md` proposal about it, which describes behaviour that does not exist; the plain-text mails need no change (P4).
* Sources: the verdict of the designer of 2026-10-15; `docs/DECISIONS.md` D-039, D-048, D-054, D-055, D-058, D-063 and D-066; django-axes 8.3.1 (`axes/helpers.py`, `axes/middleware.py`, `axes/conf.py`, read in the installed package) and its configuration documentation (URL above); Django 6.1.2 `django.utils.cache.add_never_cache_headers` and `django.views.decorators.cache.never_cache` (`never_cache` calls it); the requests project pages named in D-048; OWASP Forgot Password Cheat Sheet ("URL Tokens", as cited in `no_referrer`).

## D-068: Greeting by Name in the Security Notifications (R-13)

* Date: 2026-10-16
* Status: decided by the main session (team lead) on the verdict of the designer (P2, "approved with changes"); the project owner is away. Implemented by the developer (work unit B). **Security review of unit B done**: no critical or high issue; its medium and low findings (M1, L1 to L5) are corrected or documented below (the corrections are not reviewed again yet). **Delta review done**: no critical or high finding; L-A and L-B corrected, M-1, L-C and L-D documented below (these corrections are not reviewed again). Recommended by the designer: a new personal-data field in the job table and output of a free-text name. Supersedes the design notes of D-063 ("Mail Greeting Postponed") and the "Not done" line of D-067 about the greeting.
* Context: the notifications to the addresses of an account greeted everybody with "Guten Tag". The project owner wanted a greeting by name (R-13). D-063 left design notes; the designer's verdict is input data and was checked against the code (`email_texts.py`, `emails.py`, `mail_queue.py`, `models.py`, the 14 templates, the catalogue).
* Decision:
  * **Placeholder `{greeting}`** in the editable texts (D-039), offered **only** for the 14 `MailJob.NOTIFICATION_KINDS` (password changed, email changed, second email changed, session ended after wrong codes, all sessions ended, 2FA enabled, 2FA disabled, recovery codes renewed, recovery code used, code attempts warning, 2FA reset link, 2FA reset, email change notice, account deleted) and refused for every other email (registration, existing account, password reset, new address, second address, existing account for a changed address) by the existing check "the placeholder does not exist or cannot be used here" when a text is saved, and by the existing fallback at sending time (an invalid saved text is replaced by the default and a warning without the text is logged). Not usable in a subject. A test asserts that the set of emails that offer it equals the notification kinds.
  * **Wording** (designer): "Guten Tag Vorname Nachname" for a person account with **both** names; otherwise the neutral "Guten Tag"; no comma (the notifications never had one; the start page keeps "Guten Tag, Name"), no Herr/Frau/Familie and no title (the profile's salutation field is not used). A company-only account gets the neutral greeting; a person name next to a company name wins; only one of the two names gives the neutral greeting. The greeting is built in Python (`accounts/greeting.py`) from two catalogue texts, `Hello` and `Hello %(name)s` ("Guten Tag", "Guten Tag %(name)s"), so no template line contains a name and the 14 long msgids no longer start with "Hello".
  * **Name guard** (`greeting.clean_name`, allow-list, applied to each part and then to the whole): NFKC normalisation; characters of the categories Cc, Cf, Cs, Co, Cn, Zl and Zp are **removed** (so a line break, a tab, a zero-width or bidi character cannot survive; they are removed, not turned into a space, as decided); whitespace (`str.split()` splits at every Zs character) is collapsed to one space; **only** letters (category L*), combining marks (M*; after a letter or another mark only, at most three in a row; invisible marks removed, see the corrections below), space, hyphen-minus, apostrophe (`'` or `\u2019`) and period are allowed; any other character (digits, `@`, `:`, `/`, `\`, `<`, `%`, `{`, quotation marks, symbols, emoji) gives the neutral greeting, the character is not cut out; every part needs at least one letter; a part, or the whole name, that `email_texts.looks_like_an_address` (the helper of D-039, renamed from `_looks_like_an_address` and made public; schemes, `//`, `@`, a dot followed by two letters) accepts gives the neutral greeting (so "M.Muster" and "J.Ph. Smith" are neutral, "M. Muster" is not); the cleaned name is limited to **64 characters** (`GREETING_NAME_MAX_LENGTH`), which is what two names of the model's 30 characters and one space need (61) with some room; a longer result, e.g. after the expansion of a compatibility character (U+FDFA becomes 18 characters), gives the neutral greeting. The decision "64" and "remove, do not replace by a space" is the developer's (open for correction by the team lead).
  * **Captured once, at queue time.** `mail_queue.enqueue_for_account` is the single place: it cleans the name of the account as it is at that moment and stores it in the new field `MailJob.greeting_name` (`CharField`, `max_length` 64, `blank`, default empty, not editable; migration `0024_mailjob_greeting_name`, one `AddField`, no data, no instance-specific value). The worker passes the stored name to the sender; the account is not read for the greeting, so a rename after queueing does not change a queued mail and an account that was deleted meanwhile does not matter. The removal link of the second factor (`TWO_FACTOR_RESET_LINK`) used to be queued directly; it now also goes through `enqueue_for_account` (and keeps the account key like the email change notice, `ACCOUNT_KEY_KINDS`), so there is no second place.
  * **Only for addresses that belong to the account at queue time**: the name is stored only for an address that is in `user.notification_addresses()` (login address and confirmed second address; compared normalised) when the job is queued. Hence the **former login address** (`EMAIL_CHANGED`, queued after the change), a **removed or replaced second address** (`SECONDARY_EMAIL_CHANGED`, queued after the change), and any other address get the neutral greeting. **`ACCOUNT_DELETED` is always neutral**: `enqueue_for_account` stores no name for it, and the sending function `send_account_deleted` takes no name (a job written around the queue function is neutral too), so no name of a deleted account is in the job. The requests of persons without an account (registration, password reset, new address, second address, existing account) are not notifications: they get no name, the handler call for them has no name argument, and the placeholder is not allowed in their texts. `enqueue()` (the low level function) stores an empty name unless the caller passes one **and** the kind is a notification kind other than `ACCOUNT_DELETED`; only `enqueue_for_account` passes one.
  * **Editor.** The placeholder is in the table of placeholders of the 14 emails (EN "Greeting with the name of the person the account belongs to, or a neutral greeting if no suitable name is known. Best written on a line of its own at the start.", DE "Anrede mit dem Namen der Person, der das Konto gehört, oder eine neutrale Anrede, wenn kein passender Name bekannt ist. Am besten auf einer eigenen Zeile am Anfang."). **Preview** uses the example name "Maria Muster" (`greeting.EXAMPLE_NAME`), like the example links; the **test email** uses the real rule on the administrator's own account (`greeting.name_for(request.user)`; the test email goes to that account's own login address, which belongs to it). Neither logs the name.
  * **Default texts.** In all 14 templates line 1 is `{{ greeting }}` and line 2 is empty; the rest of every `msgstr` is byte-identical (checked by comparing the compiled catalogue of `HEAD` with the new one: the `msgstr` of every moved msgid equals the old one without "Guten Tag\n\n"; the entry of the 2FA set-up mail changed its msgid already in D-067 A4). The editor shows `{greeting}` as the first line of the default; the sent default for a person without a suitable name is exactly the text of before.
  * **Saved texts and msgids.** The msgids of the 14 default templates changed. This is safe for the reason given in D-067 A4: only the final text is stored (a text equal to the default is stored as empty), the default is rendered through the catalogue, and the msgid is only a key. A saved administrator text without `{greeting}` keeps working unchanged (it has no greeting line, as before the change; it can add one); a saved text that is invalid for an email falls back to the default as before. A test saves a text without the placeholder and sends it. Not done: a migration or notice that adds the placeholder to saved texts (an administrator who wants it adds it; the list of placeholders shows it).
  * **Retention, jobs and data protection.** The name is personal data stored in a job until the job ends: the worker deletes the job after sending, after the third failed attempt the job is deleted, `cleanup_mail_requests` / `mail_queue.delete_stale_jobs` deletes notification jobs after 24 hours (D-038; part of `run_scheduled_tasks`, which the hosting has to schedule, `docs/INSTALLATION.md`), and jobs that carry the account key (`EMAIL_CHANGE_NOTICE`, `TWO_FACTOR_RESET_LINK`) are also deleted with the account (`ForeignKey(on_delete=CASCADE)`).   Jobs without the account key (the other notifications) are **not** removed when the account is deleted, because deleting them would also delete a security notice that was not sent yet; instead **the name is erased** (security review M1, decided by the team lead): `User.delete_or_deactivate()` (the one central deletion path, used by `AccountDeleteView` and the administration; it runs inside the same transaction as the deletion and the queueing of the deletion notice) and `User.delete()` call `User.erase_greeting_names()`, which blanks `greeting_name` on every queued job whose address is the login address or the confirmed second address of the account (read from the stored row before the second address is cleared; compared normalised; also for an account that is only deactivated under the retention obligation). The notices are still sent, with the neutral greeting. A name therefore lives at most until the job is sent, given up or purged (24 hours, if the scheduler runs), and never beyond the deletion of the account. The job still holds the address for that time, which is accepted (the address is of the same kind as before). A job to an address that another account also uses as its second address loses its name too (the neutral greeting is the safe direction). The name is never written to a log (the claim returns it to the worker, nothing logs the job's fields; the failure log has only the kind and the exception class; tests assert that the name and the greeting are in no log record, also when the send fails with an error message that contains the name). No other table, the security log or the administration log holds it; the administration log still records only that a text changed.
    * **Corrections after the security review of unit B** (no critical or high finding): **M1** as above. **L1**: `mail_queue.enqueue` blanks a non-empty name unless the kind is a notification kind other than `ACCOUNT_DELETED`, and `greeting.render` runs the guard again (`greeting.is_clean`: the name must be a fixed point of the guard, contain a space and be at most 64 characters), so a stored name that was written around the queue gives the neutral greeting. **L2**: a combining mark is allowed only directly after a letter or another mark, at most three in a row (otherwise the part is refused); invisible marks (variation selectors U+FE00 to U+FE0F and U+E0100 to U+E01EF, and U+034F) are removed like format characters; the address check also runs on a version with all marks removed (a mark inside a domain name splits the run of letters that the check looks for). **L3**: the notice about a deleted account is neutral also in the preview and the test email (`emails._values` forces it), and its placeholder table says so (the placeholder `GREETING_NEUTRAL`: EN "Neutral greeting without a name: the name of a deleted account is not kept. The preview and the test email show the same. Best written on a line of its own at the start.", DE "Neutrale Anrede ohne Namen: Der Name eines gelöschten Kontos wird nicht aufbewahrt. Die Vorschau und die Test-E-Mail zeigen dasselbe. Am besten auf einer eigenen Zeile am Anfang."); the 14 emails still offer `{greeting}`. **L4** (accepted, input for the privacy notice): the owner of the mailbox of a confirmed second address learns the full name of the account holder, also if the mailbox is shared, a role mailbox or later reassigned to someone else; this follows from the design (the second address is a notification address of the account, D-059). **L5** (accepted): a name that consists of letters only can still be a misleading phrase, such as "Support Team"; the reader is the holder or the owner of a confirmed second address, and the body is fixed; no list of blocked words.
        * **Corrections and decisions after the delta review of unit B** (no critical or high finding; the review read the code and ran nothing). **L-A** (corrected): `greeting._clean_part` now normalises again after it removes characters (`_normalised` repeats NFKC, removal and whitespace collapse until nothing changes), so a name such as "a" + U+200B + U+0301 becomes "á" and `is_clean(clean_name(...))` holds for everything the guard returns. **L-B** (corrected): the invisible Hangul fillers U+115F, U+1160, U+3164 and U+FFA0 are removed like format characters; a part with no letter left gives the neutral greeting. Not covered (cosmetic, accepted): other look-alike letters such as Lisu letters, and invisible marks outside the list (for example Khmer inherent vowels U+17B4 and U+17B5, which are marks and only pass after a letter). **M-1** (decided, no code): the mark rule (at most three combining marks in a row) and the allow-list make some legitimate names fall back to the neutral greeting: Burmese and Tibetan syllables with a stack of more than three marks, Hebrew names with geresh, gershayim or maqaf (U+05F3, U+05F4, U+05BE), Latin names with a typographic hyphen (U+2010, U+2011) or an acute accent used as an apostrophe, a comma, or a middle dot (Catalan "l·l"). This is the intended fail-safe (every uncertainty gives the neutral greeting); the cap and the list are not widened. U+200D (ZWJ) and U+200C (ZWNJ) are format characters and are removed silently, not refused, so a Persian name shows its letters joined and a Malayalam chillu made with ZWJ shows a plain virama; this is accepted. Operators can read this as the known limit of the name guard. **L-C** (accepted): `enqueue_for_account` does not lock the account row, so a parallel request of the same person (a second tab, a wrong-code warning, a password change) can commit a job with the name just after the erasure scan of a deletion; the name then stays at most 24 hours (`cleanup_mail_requests`) in a job addressed to an address of the person, and a job that the worker has already claimed can send once with the name. The window is very small and nobody else learns the name; no lock is added. **L-D** (accepted): `User.erase_greeting_names` scans in Python all jobs that have a name and compares the normalised addresses, inside the deletion transaction; acceptable at the current queue size (the table is purged after 24 hours). Note for later: filter in the database first (`address__iexact` for the two stored addresses) and keep the Python check for the rest if the queue grows. A database error in the scan rolls the deletion back and nothing leaks.
      * Tests: `accounts/tests/test_greeting.py` (new, 96 tests after all corrections; extended after the security review by the tests of L1 to L3 and M1: marks after a space, hyphen or apostrophe, more than three marks, invisible marks, an address hidden by a mark, the stored name that is not clean, `enqueue` with a name for a request kind and for the deleted-account notice, the preview and test email of the deleted-account notice, and the deletion through the real `AccountDeleteView` with other queued jobs, a retained account, the administration path and a refused deletion): the fallback matrix of the guard (person, company only, one name, person and company, unsuitable characters, address-like names, look-alike forms, control and format characters, line breaks and header injection attempts, whitespace, long and expanding names, the length limit exactly), the placeholder offered exactly for the 14 and refused for all others and in subjects, the defaults and the templates, the catalogue (`Hello`, `Hello %(name)s`, the six remaining "Hello" texts), every notification kind with a name, the two links, company and unsuitable names through the worker, capture at queue time (rename after queueing), a job queued before the account was deleted (now neutral), purge and give-up delete the name, `ACCOUNT_DELETED` neutral (also with a name in the job), the second address named, a removed or replaced second address neutral, the former login address neutral (real `confirm_change`), a foreign address neutral, the case of the address, requests of persons without an account without a name, a saved text without and with the placeholder, a stored `{greeting}` in another email, nothing logged, the editor, the saving, the preview with "Maria Muster" and the test email with the real rule (named, unsuitable name, company). `test_translations.py` is unchanged: it already covers the placeholder description, the sharp-s rule and the format placeholders of the new catalogue entries.
* Security implications (for the review): (1) the name is free text of the account holder (and of an administrator who edits a profile) and reaches a plain-text email: the guard removes line breaks and format characters and allows no character that is meaningful in a header, an address or a link (the email is built by Django's `EmailMessage`, which also refuses newlines in headers; the greeting is in the body only), and an address-like name is refused; look-alike letters of other scripts pass (a letter is a letter); a person can choose a misleading but harmless name such as "Support Team" (the greeting is addressed to the account holder at an address of the account, so the reader is the person who chose it, unless an administrator changed the name; a text that deceives needs the permission to edit texts or the account). (2) New personal data in the job table, kept as in the retention above. (3) The name is only used for the addresses of the account at queue time, so a person who gives up an address or a removed second address learns no name. (4) Neutral by default: every uncertainty gives "Guten Tag".
* Not verified: real mail clients and the Graph delivery of a greeting with non-Latin letters (the body is UTF-8 plain text as before); a security review; a run on stage. The 64-character limit and "remove, not replace" are untested against real data.
* Not done (recorded): a salutation (Herr/Frau), the title, the company name as a greeting, a name for the former login address or the deleted account, a greeting for the registration or any request mail, a migration of saved texts, language variants of the greeting (the catalogue has one Swiss German text). Deleting the queued notifications of a deleted account is not done on purpose (the notices are sent, neutral).
* Sources: the verdict of the designer of 2026-10-15 (P2); `docs/DECISIONS.md` D-038, D-039, D-063, D-067; Python 3.14.8 `unicodedata` (https://docs.python.org/3/library/unicodedata.html; installed Unicode database version 16.0.0, verified by `unicodedata.unidata_version`; the general categories are those of the Unicode Standard Annex #44, "General_Category Values"; verified locally: U+FDFA normalises to 18 characters under NFKC and every character of category Zs is whitespace for `str.split()`); Django 6.1.2 (installed): the field is a non-null `CharField` with an empty default, the convention in the field reference ("Avoid using null on string-based fields", https://docs.djangoproject.com/en/6.1/ref/models/fields/#null, read 2026-10-16); the migration was generated by `makemigrations` and read, and `makemigrations --check` reports no further changes; GNU gettext `msgfmt --check-format` (the .mo was compiled with it).

## D-069: Closing the Open Phase-1 Points the Team Lead Can Decide (2026-10-16)

* Date: 2026-10-16
* Status: decided by the main session as team lead on the delegation of the project owner ("all open points of phase 1 that you can decide yourself; a design change needs the agreement of the designer"). The designer approved the visible changes (P1 with changes, P2 with changes, P3, P4; verdict of 2026-10-15) before they were built; the security reviewer reviewed units A and B and their corrections (D-067, D-068).
* Decisions:
  * **Plain-text mails stay in phase 1 (P4).** No HTML and no button. Reasons: an HTML mail increases the attack surface (link spoofing, tracking, rendering differences), the Graph sender is restricted, and the designer sees no usability blocker (the link stands alone on its line). Acceptance step: after the real Graph test send, open one link mail in Outlook, Apple Mail, Gmail and a Swiss webmail and click the link. If a link is broken in a client, add the sentence "Falls sich der Link nicht anklicken lässt, kopieren Sie die ganze Zeile in die Adresszeile Ihres Browsers." to the link mails.
  * **Address suggestions in the administration are not built.** The account change page of the administration is the generated `ModelAdmin` form, used only by system administrators and staff users with the permission; it would need a template override or a script that moves markup, which is more surface than benefit in phase 1. The address check stays advisory everywhere (D-033).
  * **ALTCHA (proof of work) is not built in phase 1.** The stage-0 protection of D-058 (hidden field, signed form time stamp, read-only pre-check of the limits, pause per address and several windows per address, D-038) stays. The recommendation remains open for a later phase if abuse is observed; it still needs the approval of the designer and the study of the manufacturer's documentation (licence, data protection, self-hosted widget under the CSP).
  * **Backlog items that are no code work:** the proxy body limit for ASGI deployments (D-052) and the wrapping of new admin URLs with `admin_site.admin_view()` (the route walk test fails otherwise) stay as written rules; they are checked by the deployment steps and by the test.
  * **Translations:** the French, Italian and English texts of the login page stay as they are (D-056, D-074).
* Not decided here (stay with the owner or the operator): the swisstopo source reference (D-066), AGOV (D-004), the content of the imprint and the privacy notice (D-022), the operator of stage and production (D-053, D-064), the real Graph test send and the other deployment gates of the README, the push for the CI run, and the human review of the security-critical changes (D-019). (Update 2026-10-16, D-074: the source reference and the content of the imprint and privacy notice are settled; the operator is Infomaniak; AGOV is waiting; the human review is done by the owner.)
* Sources: the designer's verdict of 2026-10-15; `docs/DECISIONS.md` D-033, D-038, D-052, D-058, D-066, D-067, D-068.

## D-070: Stage Does Not Differ From Production (Owner Decision of 2026-10-16; Deviation From the Stage Allow-List of D-006 and D-028)

* Date: 2026-10-16
* Status: decided by the project owner on 2026-10-16: "the stage platform must from now on not differ from production; only then can the platform be tested correctly before go-live; no restrictions with email etc." Recorded as an approved deviation under `AGENTS.md`, section 4 ("Document approved, justifiable deviations including risk and rationale"). It weakens one protective measure of `docs/SECURITY.md`, section 8 (controlled delivery on stage) and is therefore a candidate for the human review of D-019. It does not touch a legal obligation or a protective measure that is indispensable (HTTPS, cookies, HSTS, CSP, limits, validation and secrets rules stay as they were on stage).
* Decision:
  * `APP_ENV=stage` is only the label of the instance role, so that separate settings, credentials, databases and logs stay distinguishable. It has no behavioural difference from `APP_ENV=production`.
  * Stage allows and requires only `EMAIL_MODE=graph` (unrestricted delivery via Microsoft Graph) and refuses `console` and `allowlist`, exactly as production does (`ALLOWED_EMAIL_MODES` in `leg_platform/environment.py`). `EMAIL_ALLOWED_RECIPIENTS` has no effect in `graph` mode; it is only read in `allowlist` mode, which exists only for `local`. **Add-on of 2026-10-16 (team lead decision): a non-empty `EMAIL_ALLOWED_RECIPIENTS` is refused at startup (`ImproperlyConfigured`, the message names the variable and `APP_ENV` and no address) with `APP_ENV=stage` and `APP_ENV=production`**, because in the only mode that exists there (`graph`, unrestricted) the list would suggest a restriction that does not exist. An empty or unset variable is accepted (the `.env.example` line stays empty). `local` is unchanged. Implemented in `parse_email()` in `leg_platform/environment.py` after the mode check (so the mode message still comes first); tests in `test_environment.py` (`AllowedRecipientsTests`, the allowed-combination and backend tests) and `test_production_settings.py` (`test_allowed_recipients_refuse_start`, real settings module in a child process for stage and production). Deployment impact: an instance that still has the variable set stops at the next start; the checklist in `docs/DEPLOYMENT.md` and `docs/OPERATIONS.md` says to remove it.
  * `local` is unchanged: `console` (default) and `allowlist`, the latter with a dedicated development app registration (D-028, D-020). The `allowlist` implementation in `leg_platform/mail.py` stays for local.
  * Every other rule already applied to stage and production alike (HTTPS-only settings, `__Host-` cookies, HSTS, CSP, strong `SECRET_KEY`, hosts, base URL, privacy URL, `DJANGO_DEBUG` refused). Inventory of 2026-10-16: the only stage-specific rule in code was the entry `APP_ENV_STAGE: (EMAIL_MODE_ALLOWLIST,)` in `ALLOWED_EMAIL_MODES`; no check, middleware, logging, template or script branches on `stage`. The documents and tests that described the allow-list for stage were changed.
* Risks (stated honestly; the owner has accepted them):
  * Stage sends real emails to real addresses typed by testers. A mistyped address in a test, or a test address that belongs to a person who does not know about the test, receives real mail (verification, password-reset and security notifications with single-use links).
  * Test emails can therefore reach foreign people, and mail that was not meant to be sent cannot be recalled.
  * A stage instance must be treated as production-grade for data protection: the same security configuration, a separate database and separate secrets, no copy of production data into stage, and no real personal data of third parties entered without need. Real personal data on stage is personal data under the same duties as in production.
  * Unrestricted sending from a stage instance uses the reputation of the sender mailbox and domain; a flood of test mails (for example through a test of the registration form) can burden it.
  * Tests of the stage instance can no longer be limited technically to a few addresses; the limit is now a procedure.
  * **Added after the security review of 2026-10-16 (finding M-1, D-072), verified in `accounts/views.py`, `accounts/mail_queue.py`, `accounts/registration.py`, `accounts/password_reset.py` and `accounts/rate_limits.py`:** stage is a real Internet host. Anyone who can reach it can use the public registration form (step 1, `RegistrationView`) to make the sender mail a verification link to an arbitrary third-party address that has no account; before this decision the allow-list dropped such mails on stage. The password-reset form mails only addresses of active accounts (`process_reset_request`), and an address that already has an account receives one static information mail per 24 hours. The only protection against abuse of the registration form is the one that applies to production: the bot check of the form, the per-address limit (3 in 10 minutes, 12 per hour, 24 per day), the per-client-IP limit (20 per hour), the instance-wide limit (300 per hour for each kind of request) and the pause before "send again" (D-038, D-058). Up to 300 real registration mails per hour to third parties are therefore possible from stage, and they use the reputation of the stage sender.
  * A recipient cannot tell a stage mail from a real one. There is no stage marker in the code and none is added (a banner was rejected, see the alternatives): the subject, the greeting and the portal name come from the instance configuration, and the links point to the stage host.
  * Replies and bounces of mistyped or abused addresses arrive in the stage sender mailbox. Who reads it, and whether the sender address is a no-reply mailbox that nobody reads, is the open question of D-006; stage delivers real mail without anyone being responsible for the answers.
* Mitigations that remain:
  * Separate database, credentials and secrets per instance and per environment (D-013, D-020; `docs/SECURITY.md`, section 7): a stage instance never shares the database, `DJANGO_SECRET_KEY` or Graph configuration with production. Stage uses its own Microsoft Graph app registration and certificate; no production secrets are kept on development machines (D-020).
  * The Graph app registration is restricted to the sender mailbox with RBAC for Applications (D-006, `docs/SECURITY.md`, section 8). This limits only which mailbox the application may send as, not to whom it sends; it is no replacement for the removed recipient restriction.
  * Testers use their own or test addresses by procedure (an address the tester controls, addresses under the tester's own domain, or a catch-all test mailbox); this is recorded in the `docs/DEPLOYMENT.md` checklist and in `docs/OPERATIONS.md`.
  * The registration and password-reset limits (D-038) and the Graph sender check apply on stage as in production.
  * The deployment gate 1 of the README stays: Graph delivery is tested with real credentials before go-live.
  * **Added after the security review (M-1, D-072); all three are deployment procedures and change no application behaviour (stage stays equal to production in code):**
    * Stage marker: the operator of a stage instance sets `INSTANCE_NAME` (and the portal name in the administration, once it is available) to a value that contains a clear stage marker, for example the name of the municipality followed by "STAGE (test system)". The name is part of every email subject and of the pages, so a recipient can recognise a test mail. This is required in the checklist of `docs/DEPLOYMENT.md`, section 5.4; the application cannot check it, because the value is instance-specific.
    * Access protection of stage: the stage instance is placed behind an access restriction at the reverse proxy (an IP allow-list of the testers or HTTP basic authentication, nginx). This removes the abuse path of the public registration form for outsiders without any code difference between stage and production. It is a recommendation of the checklist, not an enforced rule; the operator records in the instance record when it is not applied and why. Mail links must stay usable for the testers (they use the same restriction), and the delivery to Graph is unaffected.
    * Replies and bounces: the operator names who reads the stage sender mailbox, or uses a mailbox that is monitored, until D-006 is answered.
* Alternatives considered: keeping the allow-list on stage (rejected by the owner: stage would differ from production and the real delivery could not be tested end to end); a separate "stage-open" mode with a test banner (not requested; more code paths that differ from production).
* Tests: `leg_platform/tests/test_environment.py` (stage refuses `console` and `allowlist`, requires `graph`, the validated configuration, security settings, backend and silenced checks of stage equal those of production) and `leg_platform/tests/test_production_settings.py` (the real settings module with `APP_ENV=stage` produces the same values as with `APP_ENV=production` and passes `check --deploy`; both modes refuse `console` and `allowlist`). CI runs the deployment checks for stage as for production.
* Not verified: delivery from a real stage instance via Graph (gate 1 of the README); no Microsoft documentation is cited by this entry beyond the facts of D-006.
* Sources: the instruction of the project owner of 2026-10-16; `AGENTS.md`, section 4; `docs/SECURITY.md`, sections 3, 7, 8 and 11; `docs/ARCHITECTURE.md`, "Email" and "Environments"; D-006, D-013, D-019, D-020, D-028, D-038, D-064; `leg_platform/environment.py` (inventory by reading and by search for `APP_ENV_STAGE`, `stage` and `allowlist` in the repository).

## D-071: Scenario-Based Test Runs, a Fast Test Hasher and the Rule for Full Runs (Owner Complaint of 2026-10-16)

* Date: 2026-10-16
* Status: decided by the main session (team lead) on the owner's complaint that the tests take far too long and that agents run the full suite after the smallest change (partly more than 20 minutes). Step 3a of the topic; 3b (removal of obsolete and redundant tests, further speed-ups) is recorded in the addendum at the end of this entry. A process change; no product behaviour is changed. Open to review by the project owner.
* Measurement (2026-10-16, full run with `--timing --durations 50` and a throw-away per-test timer, all outside the repository): **1814 tests, 830.5 s** of test time (835 s with database set-up 3.5 s and tear-down 0.4 s; wall clock 838 s). About **80 % was password hashing**: the test settings did not set `PASSWORD_HASHERS`, so every hash used Django's default PBKDF2 with 1,500,000 iterations (0.24 s per hash on the measuring machine); the median test took 0.29 s. Other causes: five `test_production_settings` tests that start a Python process each (13 s), tests with many wrong passwords or codes (up to 19 s each), `TransactionTestCase` flushes (17 classes, 96 tests, 43 s). Database creation and migration are only 3.5 s. No test sleeps. The full tables are working notes of the session and are not committed; the key numbers are in this entry and in `docs/STARTUP.md`, section 13.
* Decisions:
  * **Scenario runs are the normal way to test.** `scripts/test_areas.py` is the one file that maps source paths to test labels ("areas": docs, packaging, testing, local-scripts, translations, templates, static, branding, environment, address-directory, registration, login, second-factor, profile, admin, mail, migrations, accounts-support). `scripts/test.ps1` (`-Area`, `-Changed`, `-Since`, `-List`, `-Full`, `-KeepDb`, `-Parallel`, `-Durations`, `-Timing`, `-DryRun`) resolves the areas and runs `manage.py test` with the same interpreter and settings as CI. Documentation-only changes run only the repository check. A migration runs the `migrations` area with a fresh database and `makemigrations --check`. **Safe default:** a core file (settings, test settings, urls, middleware, the user model, the authentication backend, `manage.py`, dependencies, shared test helpers) or a file that no area maps runs the **full suite**. The repository check (no database) is added to every scoped run.
  * **The mapping cannot rot:** `leg_platform/tests/test_areas.py` fails when a test module belongs to no area, a label or a path pattern matches nothing, or a file of the repository is mapped to nothing (and is not a core file); it also tests the resolution rules (documentation only, translations without the migration area, migration needs a fresh database, core and unmapped files give the full suite).
  * **When the full suite runs:** for a core change (the runner decides), before a deployment gate, in CI and once per commit block by the team lead. Agents run the scenario of the area they touch and do not start a full run unless the main session orders it. Reports name the area or labels and the number of tests and never present a scoped run as a full run. CI is unchanged: both jobs run the full suite (`.github/workflows/ci.yml` was not touched).
  * **Fast password hasher in the tests only:** `leg_platform/test_settings.py` sets `PASSWORD_HASHERS = [MD5PasswordHasher]` (Django documentation "Writing and running tests", subsection "Password hashing" of "Speeding up the tests", read on 2026-10-16: "The default password hasher is rather slow by design", with `MD5PasswordHasher` in the example; the same hasher is used by `leg_platform/addresses/tests/settings.py`). `settings.py` still does not set `PASSWORD_HASHERS` (D-023 is unchanged). `leg_platform/tests/test_production_settings.py`, class `PasswordHasherTests`, proves in a child process with the real settings module that the hashers are Django's default ones, the default hasher is `pbkdf2_sha256` with at least 600,000 iterations (OWASP minimum of D-023), and that a password hashed with it verifies and a wrong one does not; a second test pins that the test settings use only the fast hasher. No test hard-codes a hash string.
  * **`--keepdb` is a local opt-in and never masks a migration problem:** the runner ignores `-KeepDb` (with a warning) when the plan needs a fresh database (the `migrations` area, the full suite, core and unmapped files). Measured saving: about 2.6 s per run (database set-up 3.2 s fresh, 0.7 s kept), so it is useful only for many very short runs.
  * **`--parallel` does not work with the local application account and stays unsupported there:** Django clones the test database to `test_leg_platform_N`; the account has privileges only on `leg_platform` and `test_leg_platform` (D-027, `docs/INSTALLATION.md`, "Database privileges"). Verified on 2026-10-16: MariaDB error 1044, access denied to `test_leg_platform_1`. The grants are not widened for this (the setup script checks that there are no grants on other databases). The runner passes `-Parallel` through for an account that may create these databases (unverified).
* Result (same machine): the full suite **1833 tests in 185.2 s** (193 s wall clock; 19 tests were added by this unit and step 0), against 830.5 s: 4.5 times faster; the median test 0.047 s. Area runs, "Ran N tests in" (before = derived from the module sums of the baseline run): docs 0.4 s, packaging 0.4 s, branding 3.9 s (before 27.5), static 8.3 s (48.2), address-directory 10.1 s (14.1), environment 18.3 s, migrations 21.7 s (54.6), mail 25.4 s (121.4), translations 27.6 s (112.4), admin 29.9 s (147.9), login 40.2 s (218.9), registration 41.5 s (203.9), profile 58.4 s (223.4), templates 66.6 s (331.5), second-factor 71.2 s (361.9). Combined with the scenario choice, a change of a translation or a style sheet is tested in 8 to 28 s instead of 14 minutes.
* Changed agent and instruction files (self-improvement under `AGENTS.md`, section 12.2; the references to `CONDUCT.md`, the security rules, the separation of reviews, the `tools` list of the security reviewer and `.claude/settings.json` are unchanged):
  * `AGENTS.md`, section 7 (run the tests of the touched area; when the full suite is required) and section 10 (report the area or labels and the number of tests; a scoped run is not a full run). Reason: the rule must be shared by all agents.
  * `CLAUDE.md`, "Way of Working" (the same rule in one sentence). Reason: the main entry point of every session.
  * `.claude/agents/developer.md` (task: run the scenario of the touched area, no full run unless ordered, keep `scripts/test_areas.py` current; report: area and count). Reason: the developer was the main source of full runs after small changes.
  * `.claude/agents/tester.md` (task: scenario run; full suite only on order, for core changes, before gates or for the acceptance of a commit block; report: area and whether the full suite ran). Reason: as above; the tester keeps the full suite for the acceptance.
  * `.claude/prompts/proceed.md` (completion criterion: the tests of the touched area pass; new section "Test Runs": the full suite is a decision of the team lead, at most once per commit block). Reason: the team lead owns the full run.
* Consequences and risks: a scoped run proves less than a full run; the safe default (core and unmapped files run the full suite) and the check of the mapping reduce the risk of a missed dependency, but an area can still miss a cross-area regression (for example a change of a shared form that the mapping assigns to four areas). That is why the full suite stays required once per commit block, before gates and in CI, and why the cross-cutting acceptance modules are part of the account areas. The rule of the team lead, not a technical block, limits the full runs. Open for 3b (done, see the addendum below): the removal of redundant or obsolete tests, the five `test_production_settings` child-process tests (about 14 s), the slowest wrong-code tests, and `TransactionTestCase` classes that do not need real transactions.
* Sources: Django 6.1 `manage.py test --help` (options `--durations`, `--timing`, `--keepdb`, `--parallel`, read in the installed 6.1.2); installed Django sources `django/test/runner.py` (`run_tests`, timing output), `django/contrib/auth/hashers.py` (default PBKDF2 iterations 1,500,000, `MD5PasswordHasher`), `django/conf/global_settings.py` (`PASSWORD_HASHERS`); Django documentation "Writing and running tests" (https://docs.djangoproject.com/en/6.1/topics/testing/overview/#speeding-up-the-tests; the same page recommends `setUpTestData`, a candidate for 3b); OWASP Password Storage Cheat Sheet (as cited in D-023); D-023, D-027, D-046, D-051, D-070; the measurements of 2026-10-16 listed above.
