* Sources: the owner statements above; `docs/DECISIONS.md` D-030, D-031, D-033, D-049, D-052, D-053, D-065, D-066, D-074; `docs/SECURITY.md`, sections 4 and 7; `AGENTS.md`, section 4; the installed Django 6.1.2 source (`django/db/backends/sqlite3/base.py`, `creation.py`); the SQLite documentation (`PRAGMA` statements, write-ahead logging, the query planner and `ANALYZE`: https://www.sqlite.org/pragma.html, https://www.sqlite.org/wal.html, https://www.sqlite.org/queryplanner.html; read from memory of the author, **not re-read in this session**: the behaviour stated here was verified by the tests and the measurements above, not by the pages); the code and the tests of this change (local run on Windows with MariaDB 12.3, SQLite 3.50.4, Python 3.14 and Django 6.1.2).
* **Corrections after the security review (2026-10-16, team lead decisions; findings L-1 to L-4, L-10 and L-12 of the review of D-075 and D-076).** The statements of the decisions above that differ are superseded by these:
  * **L-1, the read-only default is inverted (supersedes decision 2).** Every connection to the directory is read-only (`PRAGMA query_only = ON`, the `connection_created` handler of `leg_platform/addresses/storage.py`) unless the process called `storage.mark_writer()`. The callers: `process_address_imports`, `import_address_directory`, `seed_local_address_directory`, `setup_instance` and `migrate` (the project's own `leg_platform/addresses/management/commands/migrate.py`, a subclass of Django's command: a command of an installed app replaces a core command of the same name, verified in the installed Django 6.1.2 `django/core/management/__init__.py`, `get_commands()`, which registers the core commands first and then the commands of the installed apps; `leg_platform.accounts` comes first in `INSTALLED_APPS` and has no `migrate`). The test process is a writer because `leg_platform/test_settings.py` says so. `wsgi.py` and `asgi.py` declare nothing, so a new entry point or command that forgets the call cannot write by accident (before, it wrote silently). `mark_web_process` and `is_web_process` are removed. Tests: the default is read-only (insert, update and delete fail), `mark_writer` switches it, the web entry points never name it, every writer command calls it in `handle` (a syntax tree test), no other module names it, the project's `migrate` replaces Django's, and a child process with the real settings shows that `migrate` and the seed write and `shell` cannot. The caveat stays: the same user may write the file, so this protects against a mistake in the code only (copied to `docs/SECURITY.md`, section 7, finding L-2).
  * **L-3, the prune batch (supersedes decision 7).** `importer.PRUNE_BATCH_SIZE` is 900, not 5,000: SQLite before 3.32.0 allows 999 variables in a statement (`https://www.sqlite.org/limits.html`, "Maximum Number Of Host Parameters", read 2026-10-16: "defaults to 999 for SQLite versions prior to 3.32.0 (2020-05-22) or 32766 for SQLite versions after 3.32.0"), and Django 6.1 accepts SQLite 3.31. A failed cleanup is logged ("Old address directory generations could not be deleted."; no detail) and does not fail the import; a test shows it for a "too many SQL variables" error, and another that 2,000 old rows are deleted in three statements.
  * **L-4, the file creation.** The backend creates every missing folder of the path with mode 0700 (`Path.mkdir(mode=..., parents=True)` applies the mode to the last folder only, https://docs.python.org/3/library/pathlib.html#pathlib.Path.mkdir), refuses a symbolic link at the file path (also a dangling one) as a database error without the path, keeps `O_CREAT | O_EXCL | 0600`, and narrows the mode of an existing file through a file descriptor opened with `O_NOFOLLOW` (`fchmod`), so that a link that appears after the check is not followed. Tests: every folder 0700, a link and a dangling link refused with the target untouched, `O_EXCL` used. **Not run here:** the POSIX assertions and the two tests with real symbolic links (this Windows account may not create links; the check itself is also tested with a patched `is_symlink`); the Linux CI run is the first to execute them.
  * **L-10, the in-place edit of `0001_initial` is safe for an existing database.** The old router (`git show HEAD:leg_platform/addresses/router.py`) allowed the directory models (`Directory`, `Address`) only in the old separate import database (`db == WRITE_ALIAS`) and `ImportRequest` only in `default` (`db == APPLICATION_ALIAS`). A `default` database that ran the old migration therefore holds the record `addresses 0001_initial` and the table `addresses_importrequest` only; the edit changed the `Directory` and `Address` operations only (key columns, lease columns, indexes), the `ImportRequest` operation is unchanged. `migrate` finds nothing to apply there, and `setup_instance` migrates the SQLite file from scratch (its own `django_migrations`). The old separate address database, if a stage instance still has one, is no longer used; the operator may drop it (the application never does). Test: `SetupInstanceTests.test_a_database_that_ran_the_old_directory_migration_needs_nothing_and_gets_no_directory_tables` (the record exists, the table has the columns of the model, no directory table is in `default`, `setup_instance` applies nothing there and the file has its tables). The claim "not deployed anywhere" of decision 10 is therefore not needed. **Unverified:** that no stage database holds data in the old import database that someone still wants (the directory is rebuildable public data).
  * **L-12, recovery of a corrupted directory file:** documented in `docs/DEPLOYMENT.md`, section 10.2 (stop the importer, delete the file and its `-wal` and `-shm` files, start the setup unit, start the importer, press the button or wait for the automatic attempt).

## D-076: First Start From an Empty Database: `setup_instance`, the Initial Administrator From the Environment, Forced Manual Re-Import (Owner Requirement of 2026-10-16)

* Date: 2026-10-16
* Status: Decided (project owner, explicit; deviation from D-005 and D-041 approved by the team lead under `AGENTS.md`, section 4). Implemented; reviewed by nobody yet (the main session starts the security review; the human review of D-019 stays open).
* Owner requirement (2026-10-16, translated): "I do not want to create accounts. I expect to create an empty database, start the tool the first time, a superuser is then created according to the .env, and that is it. No steps from me."
* Context: D-005 and D-041 required an interactive password prompt for the first administrator and a manual `migrate`; `docs/DEPLOYMENT.md` listed both as manual steps, and the SQLite file of the address directory (D-075) needed a third manual `migrate addresses --database=addresses`.
* Decision:
  1. **`manage.py setup_instance`** (`leg_platform/accounts/management/commands/setup_instance.py`), idempotent, safe on every start. Under one named MariaDB lock (`GET_LOCK`, name derived from a hash of the database name because MariaDB limits lock names to 64 characters; waits at most 600 s, then fails with a clear message): (a) applies the pending migrations of the default database (`migrate --noinput`, verbosity 0; never `flush`, never a deletion); (b) if no account with `is_superuser` exists (also a blocked or deactivated one) and `BOOTSTRAP_SYSTEM_ADMIN_EMAIL` is set, creates the initial system administrator through the existing locked `User.objects.create_superuser()` (the same shared function `leg_platform/accounts/initial_administrator.py` as the interactive `setup_initial_administrator`) with the password of the new variable `BOOTSTRAP_SYSTEM_ADMIN_PASSWORD`, `password_change_required=True`, and writes the long-kept security log entry; (c) migrates the address directory file (`migrate addresses --database=addresses`; the SQLite backend creates the file as 0600, D-075); (d) prints a short summary without secrets.
  2. **Password rules.** The password goes through the project's validators (at least 15 characters, not too common, not numeric, not similar to the email address); a missing (email set, password not), too weak or invalid value makes the command fail with a message that contains no password (validator messages are forced to English; a message that would contain the password is dropped); a password without an email is refused too. There is **no command line argument** for the password (it would show in the process list); a test proves that the parser has none. The password variable is **not read** while a system administrator exists; if it is still set, the start prints a neutral reminder to remove it from the environment file. Neither variable is loaded into Django's settings (`environment.parse_bootstrap_administrator()` reads the mapping it is given; its result hides the password in `repr`), so no setting page or debug page can show it; the name contains `PASS`, so Django's `SafeExceptionReporterFilter` masks it in `request.META` like `DB_PASSWORD` (tested). The email variable was already read this way (never part of `EnvironmentConfig`); both are allowed in every environment and are not validated at the start of the application, because the web process must keep starting when the password is left in the file after the first login.
  3. **The address directory is optional and never blocks the start.** The directory step runs last and is wrapped: any failure (a state folder that cannot be written, a damaged file) is written to standard error with the exception class only (no path, no detail), the command still exits 0 and the next start tries again. A failure of the main database or of the administrator (configured but refused) makes the command fail, so that the services that require it do not start with a half-configured instance. The file is missing at the first start: it is created (folder 0700 and file 0600, D-075; `StateDirectory=leg-platform` provides `/var/lib/leg-platform` on a server). Guard for the file: the named lock of the main database serialises two runs of the command; SQLite's own lock and `busy_timeout` (D-075) protect it against any other writer; the importer and the web process are ordered after the setup unit.
  4. **Deployment wiring** (`docs/DEPLOYMENT.md`, section 8.0): a `Type=oneshot` unit `leg-setup.service` (same `EnvironmentFile=app.env`, same hardening, `StateDirectory=leg-platform`, `TimeoutStartSec=15min`, no `RemainAfterExit`) that the web service, the mail worker and the address importer name with `Requires=` and `After=`; the cleanup service only with `After=` (a `Requires=` would run the setup every 15 minutes). Start, restart and update therefore migrate automatically; the manual `migrate` steps of the initial setup (6.2) and of the update flow (12.3, step 7: the unit is started once explicitly, with the services stopped, and its result is checked before anything starts) and the manual administrator command (section 9) are replaced. **Never `systemctl stop|restart leg-setup`** (documented): `Requires=` stops or restarts the dependents when the required unit is explicitly stopped or restarted (`systemd.unit(5)`, read on 2026-10-16), which would restart the importer during an import; `systemctl start` is safe. Kept: the backup before an update, the STOP checks, the importer rule of section 8.4, the verification table (new rows V3a and V4a).
  5. **What cannot be automated (stated honestly):** creating the **empty MariaDB database and its one account** (needs the database root or the hosting panel), **HTTPS, DNS and the proxy** (a certificate authority, the hosting), and **the environment file** (secrets and instance values, written by the operator; the initial password is typed by the human, never generated or seen by an AI). Everything else of the application is created by the first start.
  6. **Forced manual re-import.** A manual import request (the admin button; the queued job) calls `import_directory(force=True)`: it downloads again and reloads the directory as a new complete generation even when the official checksum equals the stored one, so that "sync again" repairs a damaged directory; the new generation replaces the damaged one as the published one (retention of D-075 unchanged: the previous generation is kept until the next import). Hash and STAC verification, size and time limits, the older-catalogue refusal, the atomic generation switch, the lease, the rate limits (5 per minute per IP, 20 per minute per instance) and the retention are unchanged. The **automatic** 90-day attempt keeps the unchanged-checksum shortcut. The operator command `import_address_directory` is unchanged (it keeps the shortcut).
* Risk of an initial password in the environment file (accepted by the owner; deviation from D-005 and D-041):
  * It is readable by everyone who can read the file, which also holds `DB_PASSWORD` and `DJANGO_SECRET_KEY`; whoever reads the file controls the instance anyway.
  * Mitigations: the password must be changed at the first login and the second factor must be set up before anything else (D-041; so the initial password is worthless after the first login); the variable is ignored as soon as a system administrator exists; every start prints a reminder while it is set; the file is mode 0600 root (`docs/DEPLOYMENT.md`, section 5.3); the debug pages and error reports mask it; it is never printed or logged; the human writes it into the file with a hidden prompt and removes it after the first login (documented commands).
  * Residual risk: between the first start and the first login the account can be taken over by whoever knows the password and reaches the login page first (the second factor is not set up yet); a backup or copy of the environment file taken in that time contains the password; systemd reads quotes and backslashes in an `EnvironmentFile=` specially, so the documentation restricts the characters (letters, digits, `-`, `_`, `.`); a password that is read differently than it was typed blocks the first login (no recovery without working email: a new empty database on a new instance, with the owner's approval). The password is not a one-time value that expires by time: if nobody logs in, it stays valid while the variable stays in the file.
* Alternatives considered: (a) a password link by email: not chosen, because sending needs the sender configuration (Microsoft Graph), which can only be configured after a login; (b) a printed one-time link or password at the first start: rejected, because the project redacts token links from logs and a secret in a log is the exact thing D-005 forbade; (c) a password generated at the first start and printed: rejected for the same reason; (d) the interactive command only (the state before): rejected by the owner; it stays as the alternative for operators who prefer it, but `setup_instance` refuses when the email variable is set without a password, so the alternative is used with the email variable set for that one command only (documented).
* Verified (local): the tests of `leg_platform/accounts/tests/test_setup_instance.py` run the real command against the fresh test database and a temporary SQLite file: migrations after a real rollback of one app, the administrator created with the flags and the security log entry, a second run that changes nothing, the weak, short, numeric, similar, missing and invalid values, the password not in any output, log record or setting, no CLI argument, the lock (held by another connection, released after a failure), four parallel starts creating exactly one administrator, the directory file created from nothing with only the two directory tables and recreated after a rollback, an unusable directory path and a failing directory migration reported without stopping the administrator, and the first login (password change, then second factor). The first-login behaviour is also tested for the interactive path (`InitialAdministratorTests`). The forced re-import is tested in `leg_platform/addresses/tests/test_directory.py` (reload with an equal checksum, repair of a directory that lost its rows, the shortcut of the automatic attempt, checksum failure, older catalogue, broken archive).
* Not verified: the command and the units on a real empty MariaDB database and on a real server (systemd behaviour of `Requires=` with an inactive `oneshot`, the hardening lines with the migration and the SQLite file, the modes on Linux); `GET_LOCK` was tested on MariaDB 12.3 only; two separate processes (the tests use threads with their own connections, which hit the same database lock); real systemd parsing of unusual password characters.
* Amended: D-005 and D-041 (a note each), `docs/SECURITY.md` (sections 7 and the test list), D-075 (open point c).
* Sources: the owner requirement above; `docs/DECISIONS.md` D-005, D-016, D-041, D-046, D-066, D-075; `docs/SECURITY.md`, sections 4 and 7; `AGENTS.md`, sections 4 and 8; Django 6.1 `call_command`, `migrate` and `MigrationExecutor` (behaviour verified by the tests and the installed 6.1.2 package in use, documentation not re-read in this session: `https://docs.djangoproject.com/en/stable/ref/django-admin/#migrate`); MariaDB documentation for `GET_LOCK` (`https://mariadb.com/kb/en/get_lock/`, **not re-read in this session**, behaviour verified by the tests); `systemd.unit(5)` `Requires=` and `After=` (`https://www.freedesktop.org/software/systemd/man/latest/systemd.unit.html`, read 2026-10-16); `systemd.exec(5)` for `EnvironmentFile=` quoting (from the existing runbook, not re-read).
* **Corrections after the security review (2026-10-16; no critical and no high finding; team lead decisions per finding).** They supersede the statements above where they differ.
  * **M-1, the first-login take-over window is documented honestly.** The initial password has no expiry: whoever knows it and reaches the login page first can log in, choose their own password and enrol their own second factor; the forced password change and the forced second factor do not prevent that. `docs/SECURITY.md` section 7 and `docs/DEPLOYMENT.md` sections 8.5 and 9 say so and the two sentences "valid only until the first login" are removed. The runbook now requires that the instance is not publicly reachable (nginx closed, or restricted to the administrator's address with `allow` and `deny`, https://nginx.org/en/docs/http/ngx_http_access_module.html, read 2026-10-16: valid in the `server` context and inherited by locations without their own rules) until the owner has logged in, changed the password and enrolled the second factor; it describes how a take-over is noticed (the owner's first login fails; a `PASSWORD_CHANGED` entry nobody on the owner's side caused) and requires a restart of the services after the variable is removed (L-6: the running processes keep it in `/proc/<pid>/environ` until then). **No code expiry is implemented.** Possible later hardening for the owner's decision: the bootstrap account accepts the initial password for a few hours only while `password_change_required` is set; `emergency_access` would have to issue a new one. Not decided, not built.
  * **M-2, the bootstrap runs only for a completely empty account table (supersedes decision 1 (b)).** `setup_instance` creates the administrator only when `accounts_user` has no row of any status. If accounts exist but no system administrator, it creates nothing, writes `System administrator: NONE EXISTS, but accounts exist, so this is not a first start ...` to standard error (with the advice: restore a backup, or the deliberate interactive `setup_initial_administrator`; `emergency_access` unblocks an account or resets its second factor but does not grant administrator rights) and **exits with 0**: a missing administrator is not a reason to take the web service down, and the migrations of the same run are done. The message is the same with and without the variables, and it adds the reminder to remove the password variable when it is set. An email address that belongs to an existing account is the same case (accounts exist), so it no longer fails the start. A refusal on an empty table (missing or weak password) still fails the unit. A database error while creating the account is reported as `database error: <class>` without its text (it could contain parameters), without chaining the original exception. Tests: accounts without an administrator (also blocked and deactivated accounts, with and without the variables) create nothing and exit 0 with the message in standard error and not in standard output; an email that matches an account leaves the account unchanged; a database error whose text contains the password and the address shows neither anywhere (output, log records, exception, no `__cause__`).
  * **M-3, a manual re-import is cheap when nothing is wrong (supersedes decision 6).** `import_directory(force=True)` is replaced by `import_directory(repair=True)`. After reading the small catalogue entry (before any download) the importer ends if the published directory already is the official file: the automatic attempt trusts the recorded checksum; a manual request also needs the integrity check, `PRAGMA quick_check` reports `ok` **and** `Directory.row_count` equals the number of rows of the published generation (https://www.sqlite.org/pragma.html#pragma_quick_check, not re-read in this session; behaviour shown by the tests) and `row_count` is above 0. If the checksum differs or the check fails, the archive is downloaded, verified and loaded as a new generation as before (a damaged directory is repaired). **Behaviour change beyond the finding, reported:** the shortcut for an unchanged checksum now also ends *before* the download (before, the 143 MB archive was downloaded and hashed first and the shortcut applied afterwards); the automatic attempt gains the same saving. Every manual request that passes the rate limit is written to the security log: the new event `address_import_requested` ("Address directory import requested", Swiss German "Import des Adressverzeichnisses angefordert"; `leg_platform/accounts/models.py`, `security_log.py`, migration `accounts/0026_address_import_requested_event`), with the requesting account as the actor and no other data, in the same transaction as the queue row (the request is not queued if the entry cannot be written); the normal retention class (not the long one: it records an operational request, not a change of rights; the registry pattern of `GRAPH_TEST_EMAIL_REQUESTED`). A coalesced request (one is already queued) is logged too; a rate-limited one is not. The rate limits (5 per minute per IP, 20 per minute per instance) are unchanged. `docs/INSTALLATION.md`, `docs/DEPLOYMENT.md` (10.2) and `docs/SECURITY.md` describe it.
  * **M-4, a free-disk guard.** Before the download the importer refuses the import when `shutil.disk_usage` of the folder of the directory file (the nearest existing parent while it does not exist yet) shows less than `MIN_FREE_DISK_BYTES` = 4 GiB free (`importer.py`; about 0.9 GB per generation, up to three generations at the peak, plus the download, on a disk that MariaDB shares). It is logged without a path (`Address directory import refused: less than 4 GB free on the disk of the directory file.`), raises the generic `ImportFailure` (the job becomes `failed`, nothing is written, the published generation is untouched); a check that itself fails is refused too. The value is a judgement call, not measured on a server. Tests: below the limit refused after one request (the catalogue entry only), exactly the limit accepted, the folder measured, the fallback to an existing parent, the failed check refused, the job `failed` and the lease released.
  * **M-5, an empty directory is due again after 24 hours.** The automatic attempt is due when the last automatic attempt is older than 24 hours **and** the directory is missing or empty (no `Directory` row, `row_count` 0, or a file that cannot be read); otherwise the interval stays 90 days (`jobs.EMPTY_DIRECTORY_INTERVAL`). Without any recorded attempt it is due at once. This fixes the statements in `docs/DEPLOYMENT.md` and `docs/OPERATIONS.md` ("after a restore the worker makes the first import at once"), which were wrong because the queue row (with its last attempt) lives in the restored MariaDB database and the file does not. A failed automatic attempt consumes the interval it was due in (24 hours while empty). Tests: both kinds of empty directory at the exact boundary, a populated directory keeps 90 days, a failed attempt is retried after 24 hours and not earlier, an unreadable file counts as empty, a first start imports at once. **Limit (delta review, N-3): this does not hold after a hard kill (out-of-memory kill, power loss, a stop during an import): the job stays `running` and needs the operator until its 6-hour lease has expired; see Addendum 2.**
  * **M-6, migrations at every start have no technical gate.** A prominent warning opens `docs/DEPLOYMENT.md` section 8.0: every start of the web service, the mail worker or the importer applies pending migrations without a backup; the code may only be changed through the update runbook (section 12.3, backup first). The step number mistake of section 12.3 step 7 is fixed (the backup is step 4). No code gate (team lead decision); a possible later hardening (refuse to migrate when the code revision differs from a recorded one unless a flag is passed) is not built.
  * **M-7, a failed setup unit is retried (`leg-setup.service`).** `Restart=on-failure`, `RestartSec=60s`, `StartLimitIntervalSec=15min`, `StartLimitBurst=5` are added. Verified: `systemd.service(5)` (https://www.freedesktop.org/software/systemd/man/latest/systemd.service.html, read 2026-10-16): "Note that `Type=oneshot` services will never be restarted on a clean exit status, i.e. `always` and `on-success` are rejected for them" and the restart is subject to the start rate limit; the source of systemd at tag v243 (`src/core/service.c`) refuses every `Restart=` other than `no` for `Type=oneshot` ("Service has Restart= setting other than no, which isn't allowed for Type=oneshot services. Refusing."), at tag v244 it refuses only `always` and `on-success`, so `Restart=on-failure` needs systemd 244 or newer; Ubuntu 24.04 ships systemd 255.4 (https://packages.ubuntu.com/noble/systemd, read 2026-10-16: 255.4-1ubuntu8.18). `systemd.unit(5)` at tag v255 (`man/systemd.unit.xml`): the start limit counts every start, also manual ones and those pulled in by a dependent, and `systemctl reset-failed` flushes it; the runbook therefore documents `reset-failed` and that the web service (`Restart=always`) reaches the limit in a crash loop instead of migrating again and again. A system with an older systemd removes the two restart lines and monitors a failed `leg-setup` (section 12.4). **Unverified on a real server:** that the start job of a dependent waits while the unit is in `activating (auto-restart)` and fails only when the limit is hit (new row V4b of the verification table, stage only); the numbers (60 s, 5 starts, 15 minutes) are judgement calls.
  * **L-5, L-6:** the `tee -a` example of section 9 first appends a newline only when the file does not end with one; the restart after removing the variable is documented (see M-1). **L-7, accepted:** the security log entry of the initial administrator is written after `create_superuser` has committed (it needs the named lock outside a transaction); if that write fails the account exists without an entry. The command reports a database error with its class only and says to check whether the account exists; the audit gap is accepted (a simple fix does not exist without moving the named lock into a transaction). **L-8, accepted:** `RELEASE_LOCK` in the `finally` block could mask the original exception, and a connection lost during `migrate` loses the lock silently; both are unlikely and `create_superuser` has its own lock and re-checks. **L-9:** the `createsuperuser` error text and help name `setup_instance` (and `setup_initial_administrator`). **L-11, accepted:** `Before=` only orders; the timer may run before the first setup and fails harmlessly.
  * **Tests of this correction:** `leg_platform/accounts/tests/test_setup_instance.py`, `leg_platform/addresses/tests/test_directory.py`, `leg_platform/accounts/tests/test_security_log.py` (every event has a message), `leg_platform/accounts/tests/test_models.py` and the migration checks (`makemigrations --check`).
  * **Not verified:** the systemd behaviour of the retry and of the start limit on a real server; the POSIX file modes and the links on Linux; `PRAGMA quick_check` on the real 1 GB file (its duration is not measured; it reads the whole file once, so expect seconds to a minute); the free-disk guard on a real server; the owner's decision on an expiry of the initial password.
  * Sources: the review text of 2026-10-16; the sources named in the points above; the installed Django 6.1.2 source (`django/core/management/__init__.py`).
* **Addendum 2: corrections after the delta review of the corrections above (2026-10-16, team lead decisions; no critical and no high finding; findings N-1 to N-9). They supersede the statements above where they differ.**
  * **N-1, the importer pulls the setup in with `Wants=`, not `Requires=` (supersedes decision 4 and M-7 for the importer).** The importer unit has `Wants=leg-setup.service` and `After=leg-setup.service`; the web service and the mail worker keep `Requires=` and `After=`. The importer only needs the state folder and the tables; a failed setup run or a refused start no longer blocks it, and an explicit stop or restart of the setup no longer propagates to it. **`StartLimitIntervalSec=0` is not set.** Verified: `systemd.unit(5)` at tag v255 (`man/systemd.unit.xml`, read 2026-10-16) allows it ("may be set to 0 to disable any kind of rate limiting"), but the limit is the only bound on a crash loop of the web service (`Restart=always`, 5 s) and the mail worker (10 s) that would run the setup (the migrations) again and again; `Restart=on-failure` plus `RestartSec=60s` bound only the rate of the setup's own retries, not their number, so with the limit off a setup that never succeeds would be retried for ever. The decision therefore is: keep the numbers (5 starts in 15 minutes) and document that every start of any dependent counts. **Found while verifying (reported to the team lead):** `Wants=` does not stop the counting. systemd v255, `src/core/transaction.c`, `transaction_add_job_and_dependencies()` (read 2026-10-16): a start or restart job adds a start job for every `Wants=` dependency (a failure of it is ignored), so an importer that fails and restarts every 60 s still starts the inactive oneshot setup each time and can use up the limit in about 5 minutes; the effect is that the web service and the mail worker then cannot start until `reset-failed` or until the 15 minutes have passed. What `Wants=` does change is only that the importer is not blocked or stopped by the setup. The remedy that removes the counting, `After=` without `Wants=` for the importer, is **not applied** (not decided); it is documented in `docs/DEPLOYMENT.md`, section 8.0, and the stage test V4b has a second step (importer loop with MariaDB stopped for 7 minutes) that records whether it matters. The monitoring text (section 12.4) says: alert on a failed `leg-setup` and on the `NONE EXISTS` journal line. **Unverified on a real server:** the whole behaviour.
  * **N-2, an older catalogue is refused before the download; repeated repairs are bounded.** (1) `importer.import_directory` compares the catalogue data date with the stored `Directory.data_date` right after the checksum shortcut (`_require_catalogue_not_older`) and refuses an older catalogue before the free-disk check and before any download; `_publish` keeps its own check under the lease. An unreadable file is not a reason to refuse. A damaged directory cannot be repaired while the catalogue is older than the directory (stated, an upstream problem). (2) A **repair** is a manual request for which the published directory is the official file (same checksum) but fails the integrity check or cannot be read. If the last repair ended with `PRAGMA quick_check` still failing after the new generation, or failed (any failure after the disk check: the download, the checksum, the import), the next repair is refused for 6 hours (`importer.REPAIR_RETRY_INTERVAL`): after the catalogue request, before any download, with the generic log line `Address directory repair refused: the last repair did not succeed less than 6 hours ago.` and the state `failed`. The time is stored in the new column `ImportRequest.last_failed_repair_at` (the application database: the directory file is the thing that is damaged, so it cannot hold it); a repair that ends intact clears it; a refusal does not extend it; a refused disk check, a manual request for new official data, an intact directory (the shortcut) and the automatic attempt are not repairs and are not bound. A successful repair costs one more `quick_check` (it reads the file once). **Migration:** the column is added by the new migration `0002_importrequest_last_failed_repair_at`, **not** by an in-place edit of `0001_initial`: the correction of L-10 showed that an application database may already have applied `0001` (it holds `addresses_importrequest`), so an edit would not reach it. On the SQLite file the migration is recorded and changes nothing (the router keeps `ImportRequest` out of it). Tests: `test_directory.py` (the refusal before the download for a new checksum and for a damaged directory, the manual and the automatic path, no disk check; the six hours at the boundary, the log line, the unchanged time; a failed repair and a repair that ends intact; the block applies to repairs only; a refused disk check does not start it), `MigrateTests` and `SetupInstanceTests` (the two migrations, the new column).
  * **N-3, a hard-killed running job is taken over after its lease (supersedes the M-5 sentence "the suggestions come back without anyone pressing the button" for a killed job).** M-5 holds for an empty directory and a normal or failed attempt. A hard kill (an out-of-memory kill, a power loss, a stop during an import) leaves the state `running`. A `running` job is now treated as taken over when `started_at` is older than `jobs.RUNNING_JOB_LEASE` (6 hours, the lease of the importer; a test keeps the two equal, because `jobs.py` must not import the importer: the web process reaches it) or has no start time: `process_one` sets it to `failed` (warning `An address directory import job exceeded its lease and was taken over.`, no detail) and goes on with the normal rules, so the automatic attempt follows its own interval (a lost attempt has consumed it) and `request_import` accepts a new request again (the button works). The lost request is not repeated by itself. A job whose lease has not expired is never taken over; **before that, a killed job needs the operator** (`docs/DEPLOYMENT.md`, section 8.4: the procedure; `docs/INSTALLATION.md`, `docs/DEPLOYMENT.md` F-6 corrected). Residual: the importer's own lease on the directory is renewed per chunk, so it can outlive the job lease by the duration of the killed import; an attempt in that gap fails generically and can be repeated. A genuinely running import takes about 15 to 45 minutes (estimates, unmeasured on a server), far below 6 hours; if it were longer than 6 hours, a second importer is still refused by the directory lease.
  * **N-4, the temporary download folder (documented and checked).** The download goes to `tempfile.TemporaryFile()` in the private `/tmp` of the service (`PrivateTmp=yes`), possibly another file system than the state folder. `_require_free_disk` now checks **both** the folder of the directory file and `tempfile.gettempdir()` against the same threshold (4 GiB; a margin, a judgement call), fails closed on an `OSError` and names the disk in the log (`... of the directory file.` or `... of the temporary download.`; no path). Documented: the check is a one-time pre-check, growth of MariaDB during the import is not covered, a small memory-backed `/tmp` would refuse every import, which file system holds the private temporary directory is **unverified** (`df`). Tests: both folders measured, the temporary folder refused, the failed check of either.
  * **N-5, "empty" means the same everywhere.** `importer._is_published` requires `directory.row_count > 0` besides `imported_at` and the checksum, so a row with `row_count = 0` (which `jobs.directory_is_empty` already treats as empty) is never taken as published and is loaded by the automatic attempt too. Test: `test_an_empty_published_directory_is_never_taken_as_published`. Not changed: a wiped `Address` table with an intact `Directory` row counts as not empty for the 24-hour rule; the manual repair covers it (stated by the review).
  * **N-6, the `NONE EXISTS` journal line is part of the checks.** The row V3a of the verification table runs `journalctl -u leg-setup | grep -c 'NONE EXISTS'` and expects `0`, and section 12.4 has the same check and an alert rule (the unit still ends with exit code 0 by decision M-2, so no failed unit shows it).
  * **N-7, N-8, N-9: accepted (no change).** N-7: `accounts_exist()` and `create_initial_administrator` are not atomic with each other; the named lock serialises the setup runs and `create_superuser` has its own lock and re-checks; only another process that writes accounts in that gap (a running web service) could break the empty-table rule, and the web service is ordered after the setup: theoretical. N-8: a rate-limited import request (more than 20 per minute for the instance) is not logged in the security log, as decided in M-3; an account with the permission can exhaust the bucket without a trace, a nuisance only. N-9: the symlink protection of the state folder covers the file path only: a link at a parent folder is followed, SQLite opens the path with link following after the check (`O_NOFOLLOW` protects the `fchmod` only), and the `-wal` and `-shm` files are not covered; an attacker needs write access to the state folder (the service user or root), and the text already says the protection is against a mistake.
  * **Tests:** `leg_platform/addresses/tests/test_directory.py`, `leg_platform/accounts/tests/test_setup_instance.py`; the area runs `address-directory`, `second-factor` and `docs` of `scripts/test.ps1`, then the full suite once (results in the report of the change).
  * **Not verified:** the systemd behaviour (the `Wants=` counting, the start limit, V4a and V4b) on a real server; the free-disk check on a real disk; `quick_check` duration after a repair on the real file; which file system holds the private temporary directory.
  * Sources: the delta review text of 2026-10-16; `systemd.unit(5)` (`man/systemd.unit.xml` at tag v255, `StartLimitIntervalSec=`, read 2026-10-16: `https://raw.githubusercontent.com/systemd/systemd/v255/man/systemd.unit.xml`); systemd source `src/core/transaction.c` at tag v255 (`transaction_add_job_and_dependencies()`, read 2026-10-16); the code and tests of this change; D-046 (one test run at a time).

  ## D-077: Phase 2: the Management Area for Staff Users, Block 1 "Foundation" (Owner Order of 2026-10-16)

  * Date: 2026-10-16
  * Status: decided by the team lead (the owner is away; everything that is not security, data protection or external-service critical is decided by the team lead, the rest is collected as open points). Block 1 is implemented: the domain, the access check, the menu, the settings page and the shared building blocks. The other pages (persons, locations, LEGs, metering points, the overview with its problem rows, printing) follow in the next blocks. Not yet reviewed by the security reviewer; the human review of D-019 stays open.
  * Context: the owner ordered the administration pages ("Verwaltung") for staff users, all pages of the design specification, linked to each other. The specification is the owner's folder `LEG-Design-Vorgaben/verwaltung/` outside the repository (`LISTEN-STANDARD.md` and one folder per page with `VORGABEN.md`, `UMSETZUNG.md` and the HTML templates in `referenz/`). Its texts are final and are taken 1:1 as the Swiss German translation; the source texts in the code are English (`AGENTS.md`, section 2). Later "Nachtrag" sections win over older ones; the templates contain example data that never appears in the product; colours only through the tokens of `docs/DESIGN.md`. Phase 1 stays as written ("LEG domain functions are not part of the first phase"); this entry opens phase 2 (`docs/PROJECT.md`, "Phase 2").
  * Decisions:
    1. **Names.** Verwaltung = management area (`management`), Standort = location, Trafokreis = transformer circuit, Messpunkt = metering point, Zuordnung = assignment, Person = person (an existing account), LEG = leg, Einstellungen = settings, Bezug = consumption, Einspeisung = feed-in, Belegung = occupancy, Verlauf = history, Übersicht = overview, Probleme = problems, Anteile = shares. Database objects, choice values, URL names and paths are English.
    2. **Two apps.** `leg_platform.community` (models, migrations, domain rules and services, permissions; no pages) and `leg_platform.management` (views, forms, templates, static files, URLs under `/management/`, the list framework and the PDF printing of the next blocks). Both are in `INSTALLED_APPS`; the community models are **not** registered in the Django administration (the pages are the interface; a read-only registration would only widen the exposure; the exact-set test of D-047 stays valid and a test pins that no community model is registered). The test areas `community` and `management` are in `scripts/test_areas.py`.
    3. **Access is permissions only** (D-012). One mechanism, `leg_platform/management/access.py`: every management view is a `ManagementAccessMixin` or wrapped in `@management_view`, and checks on every request, in this order: not logged in → redirect to the login (with `next` for a GET, without it for a POST); not active or not `is_staff` → **404** (the answer for a page that does not exist; this does **not** hide the area completely: a visitor who is not logged in is redirected to the login, a POST without the CSRF token is refused with 403, and the area is public in the open source code; the 404 only keeps a logged-in account without staff status from being told "no access" for a page that is not meant for it); the second factor not completed in this session → 403 (the second-factor middleware already redirects such a session to the setup; this is the second line, `second_factor.is_second_factor_complete`); the permissions missing → **403** ("You have no access here."). A view that names no permission fails closed (`ImproperlyConfigured`). Responses are `never_cache`. The menu uses the same permissions but is no protection. Rule for developers (README): a new management URL must use the mixin or the decorator; the test `leg_platform/management/tests/test_access.py` walks the URL configuration and fails for a route without it, and checks every route for the anonymous visitor, a person without staff status, a staff user without the permission, a staff session without the second factor, and the permitted accounts.
    4. **Rights.** Default rights per model and a few own ones, English codenames, assignable by a system administrator to roles at runtime (`assignable_permissions_filter` offers them; `rights_help` explains them; nothing is created by a migration): `Location`, `MeteringPoint`, `Leg`: view, add, change, delete; `TransformerCircuit`: view, add, change; `ManagementSettings`: view, change; `PersonRecord` ("person"): view, change and `correct_person_address`. Read access (list, detail, print) = view; create = add; change, archive and assign = change; delete = delete. The assignments, the history and the list preferences have no rights of their own: every assignment action needs `change_meteringpoint`. The overview needs at least one view right of an area. The settings page needs `view_managementsettings` to be shown and `change_managementsettings` to save (a GET with only "change" is refused, a POST with only "view" is refused). Reason for not using a plain `is_staff` check: it would give every staff user, whatever the system administrator assigned, all customer data; the owner's wish "staff sees the pages" is met by assigning the rights to a role.
    5. **Persons are accounts** (`accounts.User` with a customer number; the state is the account state; a temporarily locked account shows as locked). Customer master data is **not** editable by staff. The exceptions: the address correction (accept or not accept a suggestion, logged with the actor and the time; right `correct_person_address`) and the staff-only data in the side record `community.PersonRecord` (OneToOne to the account, not a column of the guarded user model): the note, the optional BKW customer number, the shares with their period; the IBAN and the paper invoice wish are stored there too and shown read-only (the customer edits them later). "Person anlegen" and "Anmeldung starten" have no specified dialog or effect and are **not built**; blocking or deactivating an account by staff is "not decided, not shown" in the specification and is not built.
    6. **Domain rules** (testable functions, not in views; `leg_platform/community/`):
       * **Metering point number** (`numbers.py`): country (two capital letters) + identifier (eleven capital letters or digits; both from the settings) + the typed digits left-padded with zeros to 20 positions; 33 characters of capital letters and digits are valid; unique. Transformer circuit number: converted to capital letters and trimmed before comparing, `TRA` and digits, unique; the name is free and not unique.
       * **Location** (`addressing.py`, `models.py`): street, house number, postal code (four digits), town, normalised (white space); unique on `address_digest`, the SHA-256 of the address with case and accents ignored (`addresses.keys.fold`, the folding of the directory search; a digest keeps the unique index small, Django check `mysql.W003` rejects a long unique column). A transformer circuit is optional; the remark is internal and never searched; `is_archived`; `needs_confirmation` for a location that a customer created.
       * **Status of a metering point** (`status.py`), derived and never entered, with no scheduler: registered (no date of notification), notified (date of notification), confirmed (confirmation date, start date missing or not reached), active (confirmed and the start date reached; automatic), terminated (**the termination date is reached**; before that day a recorded termination does not change the status; this reading of "Gekündigt zum" is a team lead decision, open point 3). A database condition (`status_filter`) gives the same result as the function.
       * **Assignments** (`assignments.py`; person and LEG alike): a new assignment must start after the start of the last one; the previous assignment ends the day before the new "valid from" (the "flying change"); an open assignment is ended with a date and stays in the history; an assignment is deleted only if it never took effect (it starts in the future) and was never ended, which also undoes the change of the previous one (the previous one's former end is remembered). Every change is one transaction that locks the metering point and writes the history. A metering point "has" a person or a LEG while an assignment is valid now or starts later. **Assigning a person whose assignment is in force now (valid from today or earlier) sets the retention obligation of the account** (D-016: the account takes part in the accounting and is kept, not deleted, when the person closes it); a future-dated assignment does not (security review M-1), so deleting an assignment that never took effect leaves the account untouched. An account with **any** assignment of a person is treated as a retention case by the deletion path (`User.delete_or_deactivate` deactivates it, `User.delete` refuses), so the protected reference can never crash a deletion. Setting the flag writes the security log event `retention_obligation_set` with the account and the actor numbers only (M-2). `assign` reads the person again under a lock and refuses an account that is not active or has no customer number (`AssignmentError`, L-1). This is a data protection relevant assumption: open point 4 (**the owner must confirm the legal basis**).
       * **LEG figures** (`legs.py`): members are the non-archived metering points with a LEG assignment valid on the day; consumers : producers count metering points by direction (the template's own help: "Verbraucher sind die Messpunkte mit Bezug, Produzenten die mit Einspeisung"); the discount tier is "price optimised" when all members lie in locations of one and the same transformer circuit, "basic discount" otherwise (a member without a location or without a circuit counts as a different circuit, because it cannot be shown to be the same one), none without members; one query for all LEGs of a list. The legal minimum quota of 5 % is a constant, not a setting.
       * **Problems** (`problems.py`, only those of the specification; archived objects have none): location: open address correction; metering point: no location, no LEG, no person; LEG: quota below 5 % (legal), below the warning threshold of the settings (the stronger one only: below 5 % is also below the threshold), stale (a member created on a day after "checked on"; the same day is not stale; the rule of the LEG software, owner decision), no consumer, no producer (only for a LEG with members); person: none yet (no triangle). `problem_counts` is the base of the overview.
       * **Address suggestion** (`addressing.py`, `locations.py`): uses `leg_platform.addresses.services` (`lookup`, `directory_status`): a suggestion exists only for a four-digit postal code, a directory that is available and not older than 92 days (D-066), exactly one directory address with the same house number whose street starts with or resembles the typed one (D-065), and a spelling of street, house number or town that differs from the typed one. Ambiguity, an exact match and an unreachable directory give no suggestion (no warning). The result is stored on the location (`address_correction_open`), so that lists filter without asking the directory per row. "Do not accept" remembers the address as typed (`suggestion_dismissed_for`); the warning returns only when street, house number, postal code or town changes (also a change of the spelling). The same fields exist on the person side record for the persons block.
       * **History** (`history.py`): `HistoryEntry` is append only (subject type and id without a foreign key, event as an English key, the actor, the time, small facts in `detail`, never a name or an address of a customer); `has_history` (an entry other than "Created") decides "delete or archive". **Settings** (`ManagementSettings`, one row, `load()`): country (default CH), identifier (empty until entered; instance-specific, never in code), warning threshold (default 10 %), the sender address; changes of the sender address, the country and the identifier are recorded with actor and time. **List preferences** (`preferences.py`): column widths (whole pixels from 90 to 2000, at most 12 columns, a known list key) and the expanded locations, per user and list.
    7. **The settings page** (`/management/settings/`, `einstellungen/`): three cards in the order of the specification (sender of the invoices, metering points, production quota), one button "Save". The directory check of the sender address is made **on the server when the page is shown** (a banner with "Accept" and "Do not accept", or the not-found notice, at the top of the first card; never blocking a save, D-066), not while typing: the address suggestion widget of the profile (`addresses/widget.html`) is bound to the markup of `accounts/_field.html` and its panel positions under the street field, which the template of the settings page does not have. "Accept" puts the suggestion into the fields and saves nothing ("the address is taken over only with the button Save"); "Do not accept" stores the memory of the dismissal only. The banner buttons sit in the same form; the hidden default button `management/_default_submit.html` is the first button of the form, so that the key Enter saves. With only the right to view the fields are shown disabled and there is no button. Validation: all fields but the address addition are required, postal code four digits, country two capital letters, identifier eleven capital letters or digits (typed lower case is capitalised), threshold a whole number from 1 to 100.
    8. **The menu** (`templates/_frame_menu.html`, `management/navigation.py`): the group "Management" ("Verwaltung") with the entries the account may use; adding an entry is one line in `navigation.MENU`; the group is left out when the account may use none; both menus (left and drawer) use the same include. The existing link to the Django administration is now named "System administration" ("Systemverwaltung"): the group would otherwise read "Verwaltung" twice (the old label "Administration" translated to "Verwaltung"); three existing tests that looked for "Verwaltung" in the menu were changed to "Systemverwaltung". The entry "Locations ... later" of the group "Coming next" was removed with the locations block (D-078).
    9. **Shared building blocks** (`leg_platform/management/templates/management/` and `static/management/`): the page frame (`page.html`: "VERWALTUNG", title, buttons), the dialog (`dialog.html`: a centred card over a dimmed page on a desktop, the whole view on a phone; a normal page with a form, a CSRF token and a "Cancel" link, so it works without JavaScript), the info button with its bubble (`_info.html`, 14 px, the hit area 24 px, hover and focus, wraps, never scrolls, open-left variants; `info-bubble.js` only adds Escape to dismiss, WCAG 1.4.13), the link icon (`_goto.html`, 16 px in 24 px), status dot and word, direction badge (consumption in the accent, feed-in green), warning triangle with accessible text, banner in the warning colour, empty row, and the safe way back (`returns.py`: only a path of this site, no scheme, host, backslash or control character; `return_url`, `with_next`). The stylesheet uses only tokens (`color-mix` for tints); the triangle takes `--warn` because the specification says "yellow" but the project's yellow token is for dots only (open point 5).
    10. **Printing** (a later block): server-side PDF for the lists on a desktop only, with an established package (ReportLab, BSD licence, to be verified first against D-015, D-026 and `docs/THIRD_PARTY_LICENSES.md`); the options "list only", "list and details of all shown", "list and details of the selected"; it respects the permissions and the filters; no HTML injection (ReportLab escapes through its own markup rules, to be tested). Not built in block 1.
    11. **Phones:** cards instead of tables, no sorting, no print button, no horizontal scrolling.
    12. **Open points for the owner** (collected, not guessed): (1) "Person anlegen" and "Anmeldung starten" (no dialog, no effect specified); (2) editing of customer data by staff, and editing of IBAN and the paper invoice (the specification: the customer does it); (3) the display of a termination ("Kündigung") and whether a recorded termination in the future already counts as terminated (taken: only from its date); (4) setting the retention obligation when a person is assigned to a metering point with an assignment in force (data protection; taken: yes; **the owner must confirm the legal basis**; the flag can never be unset, D-016); (5) the yellow of the warning triangle (taken: `--warn`; the designer decides); (6) the Messages / "Mailtexte" page (the existing administration email texts are linked, nothing new); (7) the 30-day confirmation period (the specification says: not used); (8) the default sort of the metering points of a person (taken: number ascending); (9) billing (January); (10) texts that the specification does not contain, in the smallest neutral wording: the validation messages of the country, identifier, metering point number, digits and transformer circuit number, "This address already exists.", the problem texts "No producer" and "No consumer", the names of the history events, the field names of the models, the rights in the group form and the help, "System administration", and "optional" in the management pages (the specification writes "(optional)", the profile "(freiwillig)"; a message context keeps both); (11) the phone label "Warnschwelle in %" and the desktop label "Warnschwelle" with "%" after the field are both produced from one template with two text parts; (12) the "stale quota" rule compares with the creation day of the metering point, as the specification says, not with the day of its assignment to the LEG.
  * **Corrections after the security review of block 1 (2026-10-17):** M-1 and M-2 and L-1 as written in point 6 (assignments); L-2 a single permission string is one permission (`access._names`) and a test checks that every permission string of the views, the menu and the templates is a row of the permission table; L-3 the wording of point 3 above and `NoStoreForManagementMiddleware` (`leg_platform/middleware.py`, in `MIDDLEWARE` after the CSP middleware) sets `no-store` on **every** response under `/management/`, also the 403, 404, the login redirect and the CSRF refusal; L-4 tests with `enforce_csrf_checks` for the settings, a dialog and the widths endpoint, and with hostile `next` values (absolute address, `//host`, backslash, `javascript:`, control characters) for the dialogs and the buttons of the locations; L-5 accepting and not accepting the suggestion for the sender address are written to the history with the actor and the time (`sender_suggestion_accepted` with the suggested address, `sender_suggestion_dismissed`); **retention of the history of the sender address:** the entries hold the old and the new sender address, which is a value of the organisation, not customer data; the history is append only and has no automatic deletion, so these entries are kept as long as the database; if the owner wants a limit, a rule for the deletion of old settings entries is needed (open point for the owner); L-6 rule for the persons block: the IBAN, the staff note and the BKW customer number of the person record are shown only with `view_personrecord`, are never part of a search, a log line or a history detail; `history.record` refuses these keys (and credentials) in `detail` and a test searches the location list for them; L-7 `_goto.html` renders a link only for an address of this site (one leading `/`, not `//`) and a test covers hostile values. L-8 (the names of the BKW come from the owner's specification) is not changed and goes to the owner's list.
  * Verified facts (installed Django 6.1.2, read or run on 2026-10-16/17): `django.contrib.auth.views.redirect_to_login` adds `next` always when the field name is set, so the POST case redirects without it (`access.check_access`); Django's check `mysql.W003` warns for a unique `CharField` longer than 255 on MariaDB (seen with `manage.py check`), hence the digest column; `Model.validate_unique` and `full_clean` behaviour for the digest were exercised by tests; `msgfmt --check` of the extended catalogue returns 0 and the `.mo` was regenerated with `compilemessages`; the existing "not found" text and the postal code message are reused. The directory services were read in full (`addresses/services.py`, `keys.py`, `views.py`, `models.py`) before writing `addressing.py`.
  * Tests: `leg_platform/community/tests/` (rules without a database, models and constraints, services, the address check against a real small directory file, the rights in the group form), `leg_platform/management/tests/` (the route walk and the negative authorization tests, the menu, the settings page, the building blocks, the dialog and the way back). Runs of 2026-10-17: `scripts/test.ps1 -Area community` 156 tests in 4 seconds of test time (all new modules together); `-Area community,management,templates,static,admin,migrations,translations,testing,address-directory,branding` and `-Area community,templates,admin,testing`: OK after correcting the three existing tests named in point 8 and 4 (`test_frame`, `test_acceptance_negative_authorization`, `test_admin_spotcheck`: the menu label and the list of rights) (the full suite was not run: `settings.py`, `urls.py` and the new apps make it the team lead's run).
  * Security implications: server-side checks on every request (404 for non-staff, 403 for missing rights, no group names); every POST is CSRF protected (Django middleware); object ids are never trusted (the settings page has none; later pages look objects up by id and check the right first); the way back accepts only paths of this site; searches never include remarks or notes; no personal data in logs or in the history details; the history is append only; the unique address digest and the exact-collation identifiers (D-031) hold the data consistent. Risks: a staff user with `view_personrecord` sees customer data (that is the purpose of the right); the retention flag is set by an assignment (open point 4); the banner buttons share the form of the settings (guarded by the hidden default button); the page is not yet reviewed for accessibility on a real screen reader.
  * Alternatives considered: a plain `is_staff` check (rejected: all customer data for every staff user); registering the models in the Django administration read-only (rejected for now); the address widget of the profile on the settings page (rejected, see 7); `ON DELETE SET NULL` for the person of an assignment (rejected: it would lose who was assigned); a scheduler for the status (rejected by the specification).
  * Sources: the owner's design specification (the files named in the context, read in full on 2026-10-16); `docs/DECISIONS.md` D-012, D-016, D-031, D-033, D-047, D-065, D-066, D-071, D-073, D-075; `docs/SECURITY.md`, sections 4 and 6; Django 6.1 documentation: "Using the Django authentication system" (permissions, `has_perms`), "Model Meta options" (`default_permissions`, `permissions`), "Constraints reference" (check and unique constraints, `%(app_label)s` in the name of an abstract model), "Databases" (MariaDB notes, `W003`), "Translation" (`pgettext`, contexts), `url_has_allowed_host_and_scheme`; WCAG 2.2, success criteria 1.4.13 and 2.5.8 (w3.org/TR/WCAG22).
  * Consequences: README "Current Status", `docs/PROJECT.md` ("Phase 2"), `docs/ARCHITECTURE.md` ("Management Area and the Community Domain") and `docs/SECURITY.md` (section 4, "Management area") describe the result; the next blocks add one `MENU` line, one URL and one view each, and the entries of `HistoryEvent`, `LIST_KEYS` and the problem rows of the overview as they need them; the full suite is run by the team lead.

  ## D-078: Phase 2: Block 2 "Locations" and the Reusable List Framework (Owner Order of 2026-10-16)

  * Date: 2026-10-17
  * Status: decided by the team lead and implemented (same authority as D-077). Not yet reviewed by the security reviewer; the human review of D-019 stays open.
  * Context: the specification folder `verwaltung/standorte/` (`VORGABEN.md` with all "Nachträge", `UMSETZUNG.md`, `adresskorrektur/`, the templates in `referenz/` for desktop and phone) and `LISTEN-STANDARD.md`. D-077 holds the names, the rights and the rules of the domain; this entry records what block 2 added or decided.
  * Decisions:
    1. **The list framework** (`management/lists.py`, templates `_list_head.html`, `_list_form_start.html`, `_list_foot.html`, `_status_legend.html`, `_missing.html`; `static/management/js/list.js`). Not a big abstraction: `Column` describes a column; `parse(request.GET, path=, columns=, default_sort=, filters=)` reads and validates the state (`q`, the filter names, `sort`, `dir`, `per` = 30/50/100/all with default 50, `page`, `problems`; an unknown value is the default; the defaults are left out of the addresses); `paginate` returns a `Page` (a page beyond the end shows the last); `list_context` gives the template its head cells (sort link, arrow, `aria-sort`, saved width), the counter "n of m" and the address of the endpoint for the widths. Sorting toggles ascending, descending, ascending; the active column is blue with an arrow. The widths are saved per account and list in `ListPreference` through a CSRF-protected JSON POST (`management:list_widths`, any view right of the area, whole pixels 90 to 2000, at most 12 columns, a known list key, 4 KB); the script only enhances (without it the table has its start widths). The filter forms submit on change; the search is debounced; both also work with the buttons without JavaScript. Phones show cards, no sorting.
    2. **Search** is server-side, every word must match (AND), case and accents ignored through the column collation (the exact-collation identifiers are compared in capitals); it reads the parts of the address, the circuit number and name, the metering point numbers, the names of the LEGs and, **only with `view_personrecord`**, the names of persons; never the remark.
    3. **Locations list** (`location_views.LocationListView`, `community/location_list.py`): default sort by street, then the house number as a number, then as text; the sort by occupancy puts locations without a metering point first, then those nobody uses, then by the number of persons; filters circuit (each BKW number once), occupancy (all, occupied, free, empty, archived; archived locations only with "archived"), search, "Filter problems (n)" (only while problems exist in the current view). **Rows expand** (`LocationExpandedView`: a form `toggle` or `ids` + `open`, or the script's JSON; only existing ids are kept; at most 500 per request, 2000 stored) and the state is remembered per account and kept on return; "expand all/collapse all" acts on the rows of the page; the goto icon never toggles. The sub-table shows direction badge, number, status (dot and word), LEG and person with goto icons, "● no LEG" and "● no person" as red-dot texts (`LISTEN-STANDARD.md`, 11). The goto links to LEG and person pages appear as soon as those URL names exist (`management_ui.optional_url`), the person's name only with `view_personrecord`. The "Print" button is **not shown** (no dead button; the printing block adds it).
    4. **Location detail** (`LocationDetailView`): cards address (with "Edit"), grid (the field with the pencil, results, "+ Create new transformer circuit", "Edit transformer circuit", the counts of locations and metering points per direction in the circuit; archived ones do not count), remark, metering points (with "Create metering point"), history of the assignments (only with `view_personrecord`), the read-only change history. The one top-right button is "Delete location" (red outline) only for a location without a history **and without metering points** and only with `delete_location`, else "Archive location" (with `change_location`); an archived location offers "Restore location". Deleting is a question page, then a POST; it also removes the location's single "Created" entry. The state "new" (`needs_confirmation`, set only by the customer code paths of later blocks) shows the tag "new" in the list and the notice with "Confirm address" on the detail page.
    4a. **Address and merge.** The address (street, house number, postal code, town) is the identity; **the address addition is not part of it** (two locations differing only by the addition would be the same place). Changing it asks the directory again; the banner "Accept"/"Do not accept" decides (history with actor and time; the check runs again only when the address text changes). A new address that is the address of another location is a **merge**: the page warns, names the target and the number of its metering points, and needs a second explicit step ("Transfer and save"); it moves the metering points to the target, deletes the changed location, is irreversible and logged (`locations_merged` on the target with the numbers, no address). It needs `change_location` **and** `delete_location` (the changed location is deleted); a forged confirmation without the right is a 403.
    4b. **Transformer circuits.** Created at a location or from the create dialog (a link with `next`, the circuit comes back chosen). An existing BKW number gives the hint "already exists" with "Use this one" (a second one is never created, the existing one is not changed). Changing the name or the number affects all locations; the dialog says how many. The location's circuit is set by the field with the pencil (a button, no script needed).
    5. **Metering point at a location** (`metering_points.create_metering_point`): the location is preset; direction required; the number is optional (**`MeteringPoint.number` is nullable**, several without a number are possible; a known but not yet announced point), only its last digits are typed, country and identifier come from the settings (a missing identifier says so); the LEG is optional (only with `view_leg`) and is assigned **from the day of the entry** (the dialog asks for no date); no person. A number that exists gives the notice (the link to the metering point comes with the metering point pages); the unique constraint decides a race.
    6. **Dialogs are normal pages** (`dialog_form.html`): a form, a CSRF token, "Cancel" with the safe `next` (`returns.py`) and the default back page; hostile values fall back to the default (tested).
    7. **Menu.** `MenuEntry("locations", …, ("community.view_location",))`; the entry "Locations — later" of the group "Coming next" is removed (the phone bottom bar tab "Standorte" stays "later" until the phone navigation is decided: open point).
    8. **Neutral texts** that the specification does not give and that the code invents (to be confirmed by the owner): "Apply" (the filter button without JavaScript), "Restore location", "Edit address", "Edit remark", the lead of "Delete location" and the merge warning, "Edit transformer circuit", "Show or hide the metering points", "Change the width of the column", "Details", "History", "By", "Event", "No locations yet. Create the first location.".
  * Verified facts: the layout, spacing, wrapping and the phone views were compared in the browser at 1440 and 390 px against the `referenz/` pages (list, detail, "new", dialogs) using a local development server with synthetic data that is **not** in the repository; no horizontal overflow on any checked page; the arrow of the row is enlarged after the check. The identifiers' unique constraints and the digest of D-077 are unchanged; migration `community/0002` adds `address_addition` and makes the metering point number nullable (a unique column with NULLs: several allowed).
  * Tests: `community/tests/test_locations.py` (the services, the circuits, creating a metering point, the list queries), `management/tests/test_lists.py` (the framework and the widths endpoint incl. CSRF), `management/tests/test_location_pages.py` (views, permissions, dialogs without script, hostile `next`, merge, CSRF), additions to `test_components.py` and `test_access.py` (the route walk now accepts a 404 or 405 for the sample ids but never a refusal or a login redirect for the permitted accounts). `test_frame.py`: "Standorte" is no longer in the "later" group of the menu.
  * Security implications: every view uses the access mixin; ids are looked up after the check; every state change is validated on the server; the widths and expanded ids are validated and bounded; person names and links are gated by `view_personrecord`; the merge needs two rights and an explicit step; no remark, address or name in logs or history details; scripts contain no `innerHTML`, `eval` or inline styles (CSP).
  * Open points for the owner: the texts of 8; the bottom bar tab "Standorte"; whether a location with metering points but without any history should be deletable (taken: no, archive); whether the address addition should be part of the uniqueness.
  * Sources: the owner's specification (the files named above); D-077; Django 6.1 documentation ("Class-based views", "Cache decorators", `Cast`, `Exists`, "Test client" `enforce_csrf_checks`).

  ## D-079: Phase 2: Block 3 "Metering Points" With the Shared LEG Switch (Owner Order of 2026-10-16)

  * Date: 2026-10-17
  * Status: decided by the team lead and implemented (same authority as D-077). Not yet reviewed by the security reviewer; the human review of D-019 stays open.
  * Context: the specification folder `verwaltung/messpunkte/` (`VORGABEN.md`, `UMSETZUNG.md`, `detail/VORGABEN.md` with all "Nachträge", `handy/VORGABEN.md`, the templates for desktop and phone), `LISTEN-STANDARD.md` and, for the move mask, `legs/referenz/leg-messpunkt-wechseln-desktop`. D-077 holds the names, the rights and the domain rules, D-078 the list framework; this entry records what block 3 added or decided.
  * Decisions:
    1. **Pages and URLs** (`management/metering_point_views.py`, `metering_point_forms.py`, `summaries.py`, templates `management/metering_points/*.html` and `_duplicate.html`, `static/management/js/lookup.js`): `management:metering_points` (list), `_new`, `_detail`, the dialogs `_notify`, `_confirm`, `_start`, `_terminate`, `_leg`, `_person`, `_person_end`, `_person_delete` (a question page, then a POST), the buttons `_archive` and `_delete` (a question page, then a POST) and three small JSON endpoints `_check_number`, `_location_search`, `_person_search`. The menu has one new line (`navigation.MENU`, symbol `i-metering`). No "Print" button (the printing block adds it).
    2. **Rights.** List and detail: `view_meteringpoint`. Create: `add_meteringpoint` (and view). Every step, assignment and archive: `change_meteringpoint` (and view). Delete: `delete_meteringpoint`. In addition: choosing a LEG needs `view_leg`; choosing a location and the link to a location need `view_location`; **everything about a person needs `view_personrecord`**: the name on the page, the search in the list, the field "Person" with its suggestions, "Assign/Change person", "End assignment" and "Delete assignment" (decided consistently: a user who may not see persons cannot assign them either, because assigning needs the identification of the person; "Zuordnung beenden" and "löschen" name the person in their text, so they need the right as well). The addresses of the locations are shown to everyone who sees the metering points (they are the data of the metering point); the link to the location only with `view_location`, to the LEG only with `view_leg`, to the person only with `view_personrecord` (and when the page exists: `management_ui.optional_url`).
    3. **The list** (`metering_point_list.py`, `list.html`): columns number (480 px, never cut, direction badge, warning triangle with the text of its problems), location, LEG, status or next step; the number is the default sort; the last column has no start width (the table must fit 1440 px with the narrow link column of the standard, 56 px, which opens the detail page; on a phone the icon is next to the number); the columns and the saved widths use the keys `number`, `location`, `leg`, `status`, `next_step`. Quick filters are links (`step=`): all, "Notify the BKW" (status registered), "Confirmation pending" (notified), "Start date pending" (confirmed, no start date) and "archived" (the standard asks for an entry "archiviert"; archived points appear only there). **Counts** in the filters and in "Filter problems (n)": the metering points of the current view, i.e. with the search and the filter by LEG applied; the count of the problem button also with the quick filter; when "Filter problems" is on, the counts of the quick filters count the rows with a problem (the counts always equal the rows that the filter shows). The next step is a button that opens the dialog of the step (only with `change_meteringpoint`), "LEG fehlt" as grey text without a button for a registered point without a LEG; "Kündigen" is not in the list. The search reads the number, the parts of the address, the name of the LEG and, with `view_personrecord`, the name of a person; never the remark. The filter by LEG is a field on a desktop only (the phone template has none). The quick filters on a phone are a horizontal strip, the active one is scrolled to the left (the script); the order on a phone is search, quick filters, cards (the reference).
    4. **Creating** (`MeteringPointCreateForm`, `dialog_create.html`): the number first and required (only the last digits; country and identifier from the settings, grey in front), then the direction, then "Optional": location (a text field that finds a location by street or by a metering point number; a suggestion list of at most 10 locations that are not archived; the text must name exactly one location), LEG, PV power, battery, wallbox, remark. The location and the LEG fields exist only for accounts that may see them (a forged field is ignored). The number is checked on leaving the field (`metering-points/check-number/`, the script shows the message or the notice below the field and blocks the button) and again on saving (the service answers `DuplicateNumber` with the existing point; a race is decided by the unique constraint). The notice (colour of the warning) shows number, direction, location, LEG and status of the existing point with the link "Zum bestehenden Messpunkt wechseln"; the same notice, now with the link, appears in the dialog at a location (block 2). Without the script the same checks happen on saving.
    5. **The steps** (`metering_points.py`; each is one transaction that locks the row and checks the status again): `mark_notified` (registered -> notified; the date not in the future), `record_confirmation` (notified; the date not in the future and not before the registration; the start date optional and free), `record_start_date` (confirmed without a start date; opened from the button "Startdatum erfassen", which uses the dialog "Bestätigung erfassen" with the field "Startdatum", as the specification says), `terminate` (active, not yet terminated; the date must be the last day of a month and not before the start date; a date in the future keeps the status active until it is reached). **Active is derived**, no scheduler and no change of the row when the start date is reached. A step cannot be done twice or out of order or for an archived point: a GET in the wrong status goes back with a message, a POST shows the refusal on the dialog and changes nothing. The registration does **not** require a LEG on the server (the list shows no button without a LEG, the detail page shows it as the reference does and the banner names the problem; open point 1). History: `registered_with_bkw`, `confirmation_recorded` (the start date is part of the detail and of the text "Bestätigung erfasst, Startdatum 01.11.2026"), `start_date_recorded`, `termination_recorded`; the history table is oldest first and shows the date only, as the reference; assignment events name the LEG, and with `view_personrecord` the person (the entries hold only ids).
