    6. **The detail page**: header with the small label, the full number (never cut) and the direction badge; one banner with one sentence per problem; cards "Metering point" (direction, location, transformer circuit read-only; the PV power, the battery, the wallbox and the remark only when entered, the reference shows none), "Status" (state, start date or "fehlt" in the warning colour with the button and the note "Anmeldung auf Standby, Startdatum fehlt.", "Gekündigt zum" when entered), "LEG" and "Person". A chain of two assignments (a change that is entered) shows "bisher" with "bis ..." and "neu" with "ab ..." (also for the LEG, the same partial); one assignment shows the name, "Gilt ab" and, when ended, "bis ...". Buttons: "LEG zuweisen"/"LEG wechseln"; "Person zuweisen"/"Person wechseln", "Zuordnung beenden" (the open assignment, a date), "Zuordnung löschen" (only an assignment that never took effect and was never ended, with a question first, which also undoes the end of the former one); top right "Messpunkt löschen" (without a history and with the right to delete) else "Messpunkt archivieren"; an archived point offers only "Restore metering point"; on a phone the button is at the end of the page. The "history" of the card is the table "Verlauf" with "Datum, Ereignis, Von" (the reference words; `msgctxt "metering point"`, because "History" and "By" of the locations have other translations).
    7. **Persons** (`person_search.py`): a person is an active account with a customer number (what `assignments.assign` accepts). The field is a text field; the script asks for suggestions (at least two characters, at most **10**, the text of a suggestion is "name · customer number", never the e-mail address); the server resolves the typed text itself: a suggestion, a customer number, an e-mail address (exact) or a name that only one account matches; none or several are refused with a message. Name, customer number and e-mail address are searched (every word must match). The date "Gilt ab" is free (no deadline for the administrator); a date that is not after the start of the last assignment is refused. Assigning a person in force sets the retention obligation (D-077, security review M-1/M-2, unchanged).
    8. **The LEG switch, shared with the LEG block** (`leg_switch.py`, `LegView`, `dialog_leg.html`, `metering_points_leg`): "LEG zuweisen" (no LEG) and "LEG wechseln" (with a LEG; the LEG that the point has is not offered) are one view at `/management/metering-points/<id>/leg/?next=...`. The **move mask** lists the other metering points (not archived) of the **same person at the same location** (`related_points`), none preselected; only the ticked ones move; the ids of the form are checked against the list the server computes (a forged id is refused); the date applies to all; everything is one transaction (if one assignment is refused none is made); a point that already has the chosen LEG is skipped; an archived LEG is refused. The LEG pages link to this address (the icon "Tausch" of a row) and need no code of their own; they need `view_meteringpoint`, `change_meteringpoint` and `view_leg`.
    9. **Archive and delete** (`metering_points.py`): delete only without a history (an entry other than "Created"); a point created with a LEG has the entry "LEG assigned" and is therefore archived, not deleted (a consequence of the rule of the specification); the delete removes the entry "Created" with it. Archive and restore are written to the history.
    10. **Neutral texts that the specification does not give and that the code invents** (to be confirmed by the owner): "Restore metering point", "No metering points yet. Create the first metering point.", "Go to the location", "Go to the details of the metering point", "Enter the last day of a month.", "No person found.", "Several persons match. ...", "Choose a location from the list.", "Several locations match. ...", the refusals of the rules ("The date must be after the start of the current assignment.", "The end date must not be before the start of the assignment.", "This assignment has already ended.", "This assignment cannot be deleted. End it instead.", "This account cannot be assigned.", "This LEG is archived.", "A selected metering point does not belong to this one.", "This change is not possible.", "This metering point is archived.", "This step is not possible in the current status of the metering point.", "The date must not be in the future.", "The date must not be before the earlier step."), the lead of "Delete metering point" ("The metering point %(number)s is deleted for good.") and of "Delete assignment", the variant of the lead of "LEG wechseln" for a point without a location, "Applies from" = "Gilt ab" for the English source (the specification has "Gilt ab" for assigning and "Gültig ab" for the switch). The texts of the dialog "Messpunkt anlegen" are the specification's, including the lead "Ein Messpunkt gehört zu einem Standort. Wählen Sie den Standort aus, ..." although the location is optional (open point 2). The translation of "Only the last digits. Country and identifier come from the settings." of block 2 was corrected to the specification ("Nur die letzten Stellen. Land und Identifikator kommen aus den Einstellungen.").
  * Verified facts: the layout, spacing, wrapping, the states and the phone views were compared in the browser at 1440 and 390 px (Edge, local development server on port 8001, synthetic data that is **not** in the repository) with the templates of the folders `messpunkte/referenz`, `detail/referenz`, `handy/referenz` and `legs/referenz` (read as source: the templates need the runtime of the design package, which is not in the folder): list (all, step filter, problem button, warning triangle), create (with the duplicate notice and the blocked button), notify, confirm, terminate, person (suggestions filled from the endpoint: 2 results for "mus"), change person, LEG switch with the mask, end and delete assignment, the detail page registered and active with a flying change and bestätigt/standby, and the phone list, detail and dialogs; no horizontal overflow on any checked page (the script reads `scrollWidth - clientWidth`: 0). Differences kept: the status and next-step column take the rest of the width (spec widths 170/220 px do not fit with the 56 px link column at 1440 px); the buttons of a phone card may be wider than the 150 px value column of the reference (the column grows, `minmax(150px, auto)`) so that nothing leaves the card; the dialog buttons on a phone are stacked (the block 2 pattern, not the bar at the top of the reference).
  * Tests: `community/tests/test_metering_point_rules.py` (19 tests, 0.8 s: the steps and their refusals, next step, delete/archive, the chain, the LEG switch with the mask and its refusals, the person search and its limit, the list queries: counts, filters, problems, search without remarks, sort, locations by typing) and `management/tests/test_metering_point_pages.py` (22 tests, 5 s: list, step view, problem filter, search, detail states, flying change, the rights of every action, the steps through their dialogs with the refusals of steps out of order, the person and the LEG dialogs, create with the duplicate notice and the ignored forged fields, the endpoints, delete and archive, CSRF and hostile `next`). The negative authorization of every new URL (anonymous, no staff status, no right, no second factor) is covered by the route walk of `test_access.py`. Runs of 2026-10-17: `scripts/test.ps1 -Changed` (areas community, management, static, templates, translations): 1044 tests, OK, 98 s (the full suite was not run: the team lead's run).
  * Security implications: every view uses the access mixin; ids are looked up after the check; every step and assignment is validated on the server and in one locked transaction; the person search is bounded (10), needs `view_personrecord` and `change_meteringpoint`, never shows the e-mail address and finds only accounts that can be assigned; the location search needs `view_location`; the move mask accepts only ids of the server's own list; every POST has the CSRF token; the way back is checked (`returns.py`); no remark, name or address in logs; the history holds only ids and dates; the script builds nodes with `textContent` (no `innerHTML`, no inline style), the link of the duplicate notice is used only for an address of this site. Risk: a staff user with `view_personrecord` and `change_meteringpoint` can find out through the search field whether an e-mail address belongs to a customer (the purpose of the right).
  * Open points for the owner: (1) should the registration at the BKW require a LEG on the server (the list says "LEG fehlt" without a button, the detail template shows the button); (2) the lead of "Messpunkt anlegen" says the location must be chosen first, the rules say it is optional; (3) what happens to the assignments of a terminated metering point (taken: nothing, they stay as entered); (4) the display text of the state "gekündigt" and whether a termination date in the future needs its own display (taken: the row "Gekündigt zum" and the state active until it is reached); (5) the neutral texts of 10; (6) whether a start date may lie before the confirmation (taken: yes, free).
  * Sources: the owner's specification (the files named above); D-077, D-078; Django 6.1 documentation ("Class-based views", `select_for_update`, `Exists`, `Subquery`, "Translation" (`pgettext`, `ngettext`), "Forms" (`ModelMultipleChoiceField`)).

  ## D-080: Phase 2: Block 4 "LEGs" (Owner Order of 2026-10-16)

  * Date: 2026-10-17
  * Status: decided by the team lead and implemented (same authority as D-077). Not yet reviewed by the security reviewer; the human review of D-019 stays open.
  * Context: the specification folder `verwaltung/legs/` (`VORGABEN.md`, `DETAIL-ENTWURF.md` with all "Nachträge", `UMSETZUNG.md`, the templates in `referenz/` for desktop and phone), `LISTEN-STANDARD.md` sections 9 to 12 and, for the dialog "LEG wechseln", the block of the metering points (D-079). D-077 holds the figures of a LEG (`legs.py`) and the problems, D-078 the list framework, D-079 the shared LEG switch; this entry records what block 4 added or decided.
  * Decisions:
    1. **Pages and URLs** (`management/leg_views.py`, `leg_forms.py`, templates `management/legs/*.html`, `static/management/js/leg-quota.js`): `management:legs` (list), `legs_new`, `legs_detail`, the dialogs `legs_quota` and `legs_edit` (master data), the buttons `legs_archive` (POST only) and `legs_delete` (a question page, then a POST). The menu has one new line ("LEGs", symbol `i-network`, between "Locations" and "Metering points"). The links of the lists and detail pages of locations and metering points to a LEG (`management_ui.optional_url("management:legs_detail", ...)`) resolve now; the links to persons stay empty until the persons block. A test pins that the metering point list and the location page link to the LEG. No button "Print" (the printing block adds it; never on a phone).
    2. **Rights.** List and detail: `view_leg`. Create: `add_leg` (and view). Edit the quota or the master data, archive, restore: `change_leg` (and view). Delete: `delete_leg` (and view). On the detail page the table "Metering points of this LEG" needs `view_meteringpoint` (without it the page shows the cards and the count only), the link to a location `view_location`, the name of a person `view_personrecord` (a person that exists but may not be named is a dash; "● keine Person" only when there is none), and the swap icon `change_meteringpoint`; the icon opens the shared dialog `management:metering_points_leg` of D-079 with `next` = this page (that view also needs `view_leg`, which every account on this page has).
    3. **The list** (`community/leg_list.py`, `list.html`): the figures come from `legs.summaries` (one query for all LEGs asked for); the rows are sorted in Python (a few dozen LEGs; the derived columns are no database columns) and paged by `lists.paginate` through `lists.ListRows`. Columns: LEG (name only, bold, plain text), production quota (the value only, "–" without a quota), participants ("3 : 1", "–" without metering points), discount tier (dot and word, "–" without metering points), metering points, the link icon. A warning triangle stands behind the quota (all quota problems: below 5 %, below the threshold of the settings, stale) and behind the participants (no producer, no consumer) with the text of the problem as the hint; on a phone it stands behind the name (all texts). Sorting: every column (first ascending, then descending); the quota, the participants and the tier put a LEG without a value last **in both directions**; the participants sort by consumers, then by producers; the tier sorts "price optimised" before "basic discount"; the name breaks ties (case ignored). Search: every word must match the name of the LEG or the BKW number of a transformer circuit of its current members (case ignored; the circuit name is not searched, the specification says "Trafokreis" = the BKW number); never the remark. Filter "Display": all (the live LEGs) or archived (only the archived ones). "Filter problems (n)": n = the LEGs with a problem in the current view (search and display applied), the button only while n > 0, a switch. The widths are saved under the list keys `legs` and `leg_metering_points` (both existed in `preferences.LIST_KEYS`).
    4. **Problems.** An archived LEG has no problems (`build_summary`; out of sight like every archived object). The two problem texts that the specification names only in its example banner are now the final wording: "Problem: kein Produzent in der LEG" (reference banner) and, by analogy, "Problem: kein Konsument in der LEG" (the specification says "kein Verbraucher" in the list text and "Konsumenten" in the column; the newest wording of the column was taken). The earlier labels "No producer"/"No consumer" were replaced (the test of the label was changed).
    5. **The detail page** (`LegDetailView`): back link, title, the count of the metering points; the banner with one full sentence per problem (none without a problem); the cards "Production quota" (share, "Checked on", the line "✓ Ausreichend" in green / "Unter 10 %" in the warning colour / "Unter 5 %" in red, the hint with the legal 5 % written from the constant; the tick only on a desktop, as the phone reference has none), "Master data" (name, remark or "Keine"), "Composition" (consumers : producers, discount tier with the info button on a desktop and the written-out hint on a phone, the transformer circuits as tags on a desktop and as a comma list on a phone). The line of the quota level is not shown without a quota, the share and the date are dashes. Buttons: top right "Delete LEG" (red outline, only **without a history = no metering point was ever assigned** to the LEG, also none that moved on or was deleted again, and with the right to delete) else "Archive LEG" (neutral; an archived LEG offers "Restore LEG"); on a phone the button is at the end of the page. A LEG that is deleted takes its own history entries with it (the metering point history keeps its text without the name).
    6. **The table "Metering points of this LEG"** (read only): columns number with direction badge, address (the link icon to the location), transformer circuit, status, occupied by, and the column of the action (fixed 170 px, right aligned, no heading, a hidden "Action" for the screen readers). Every heading sorts (number, address, circuit, status, occupied by) and has the handle; the start widths are the last ones of the specification (400/150/100/130/120 px; the table fills the card, so the widths scale: 430 px for the number at 1440 px; the number is not cut at the usual widths of a desktop, at a very narrow desktop the table is squeezed as the other lists are). The members are the metering points with a LEG assignment **valid today**, not archived (a future assignment is not yet a member, one that has ended is no longer). A metering point without a person shows "● keine Person" (red dot), without the right to see persons a dash instead of a name; no triangle in a table without a filter (`LISTEN-STANDARD.md`, 11). Phone: one block per metering point (number and badge, address with link, "TRA... · person", status left and state with the swap icon right).
    7. **The state of the last column** (`leg_list.members`): green "● eigenes" = the metering point is already in the own LEG of its transformer circuit, nothing to do; blue "● <name of the LEG>" = the move to the LEG that covers only that circuit is recommended; grey "kein eigenes" = there is no such LEG; nothing for a metering point whose circuit is unknown (no location or no circuit). "Own LEG of a circuit" = a LEG that is not archived and whose members all lie in locations of that one circuit (price optimised, one circuit; the first by name if there are several; never the LEG itself). Rules taken from the specification and its source (`_dedicated_leg_names`): a LEG of **one** circuit (price optimised) has every point "eigenes" (confirmed Nachtrag, reference `leg-detail-ein-trafokreis-*`); a LEG of several circuits (basic discount) recommends or says "kein eigenes". **This departs from the brief** ("the column only when the LEG has metering points in several circuits"): the later Nachtrag and the one-circuit references show "● eigenes" for a one-circuit LEG, so the column and the legend are shown whenever a point has a state. The recommendation shows the name of the LEG as it is ("● LEG Bahnhof" is the name "LEG Bahnhof"; no prefix is added). The titles of the three states are the three sentences of the legend (the specification asks for "a help text on hovering" and gives none).
    8. **Dialogs** (`DialogView`, pages that work without JavaScript, safe way back): "Create LEG" (name required, the quota optional with "%" inside the field, "Checked on" next to it, remark; after saving back to the list), "Edit production quota" (quota and date together), "Edit master data" (name and remark). The name is unique ignoring case and white space ("Dieser Name wird bereits verwendet." under the field, nothing saved; a race is decided by the unique column and reported the same way; the check is made on saving, not while typing). The quota: 0 to 100 with at most two decimals, a decimal comma is accepted; **a quota needs the date, without a quota the date is dropped** ("Ohne Quote bleibt es leer"); the date is **today's** date as soon as the quota changes, set by a small script from the day of the server (`data-today`), which leaves the date alone once the person changed it and restores the old date when the quota is as it was; **the day of the reading must not be in the future** (a rule of the team lead: the stale check compares days, a future day would hide a stale quota; the existing text "The date must not be in the future." is reused). A form that is empty at the start has no date (the reference shows today's date in the empty create dialog, the text says "ohne Quote bleibt es leer": the text won). The dialog titles on a phone are the desktop titles ("Produktionsquote bearbeiten", the phone reference shortens them to "Produktionsquote" and "Stammdaten").
    9. **History** (`leg_services.py`, every change one transaction): `created`, `quota_changed` (old and new value and date; the quota without trailing zeros), `master_data_changed` (the names of the fields that changed, never the text of the remark), `archived`, `unarchived`; no entry when nothing changed. The LEG pages show no history table (the specification has none).
    10. **Archiving** is allowed while the LEG has members (the specification is silent; the members keep the assignment, the LEG leaves the list and the metering point list still names it; the LEG switch refuses an archived LEG as a target, D-079). Open point 1.
    11. **Neutral texts that the specification does not give and that the code invents** (to be confirmed by the owner): the subtitle "Die lokalen Elektrizitätsgemeinschaften." (the reference adds "Jede LEG rechnet für sich ab.", which contradicts the rule that the invoices carry the LEG platform as the sender; left out), the help of the column "LEG" ("Name der LEG. Eine LEG ist ein virtueller Netzzusammenschluss."; the reference text names the invoice sender and the remark, both superseded), the help of the column "Production quota" (the reference says that below 10 % the LEG is marked "knapp"; now: "Unter 10 % steht ein Warndreieck", with the threshold of the settings), "Noch keine LEGs. Legen Sie die erste LEG an.", "Noch keine Messpunkte in dieser LEG." ("Dieser LEG gehören noch keine Messpunkte zu."), "LEG wiederherstellen", the lead of "LEG löschen" and its refusal, the names of the history events, "kein Konsument in der LEG". The texts of the dialogs, the cards, the banner, the legend and the states are the specification's.
  * Verified facts: the layout, spacing, wrapping, the states and the phone views were compared in the browser at 1440 and 390 px (Edge, local development server on port 8001, synthetic data that is **not** in the repository) with the templates of `legs/referenz` (read as source: they need the runtime of the design package, which is not in the folder): list (desktop and phone, the triangles, the tooltip of the tier), detail (several circuits with the recommendation and "kein eigenes", one circuit with "eigenes" and the banner, the stale quota, dark mode), the dialogs create and edit quota and master data, no horizontal overflow on any checked page (`scrollWidth - clientWidth` = 0, including the table at 1440 px). The script of the dialog was exercised in the browser (today's date on a change, empty quota empties the date, a date changed by hand is kept, the old date returns), the resizing of a column was dragged and saved (the width was kept after a reload). The buttons "Edit" of the cards are 44 px high on a desktop (the rule K5 of `frame.css` wins over the 36 px of the reference, as in block 2). Read in the installed Django 6.1.2: `django/template/base.py` (`Variable`, line 948 and following) doubles every "%" of a literal of `{% translate %}` before it looks the text up, and `templatetags/i18n.py` (line 95) turns "%%" back, so a literal with a percent sign needs "%%" in the catalogue (found by a failing test); the quota texts take the "5 %" as a placeholder instead (`%(minimum)s`), which also removes the hard-coded 5 from the text; the project test of the placeholders (`test_translations_keep_every_placeholder`) reads "% a" and "% s" as conversions, so "5 % are" and "5 % sind" cannot stand in a catalogue entry.
  * Tests: `community/tests/test_leg_rules.py` (23 tests, 0.5 s: the formatting of the quota and the levels, the texts, the services with their history, the unique name, the quota-and-date rule, delete only without a history, the list queries: figures, display filter, search without remarks, problems, sorting with missing values last in both directions, the stale quota day-exact, the members, the recommendation, the dedicated LEG, the occupants and the sorting of the table) and `management/tests/test_leg_pages.py` (15 tests, 2.6 s: list, order, search, filters, pages, menu and the links from other pages, the detail page in the states, the rights that shape the table, the buttons, create, quota and master data with their refusals, hostile `next`, archive, restore, delete, CSRF, the access of every action). The negative authorization of every new URL (anonymous, no staff status, no right, no second factor) is covered by the route walk of `test_access.py`. Three existing tests were changed: the label of `NO_PRODUCER`, `optional_url` of the LEG page (it resolves now; the check uses the person page instead) and the menu of an account with only `view_leg` (it sees the entry "LEGs"); `FakeLeg` of `test_rules.py` got `is_archived`. Runs of 2026-10-17: `scripts/test.ps1 -Changed` (areas community, management, static, templates, translations): 1082 tests, OK, 94 s (the full suite was not run: the team lead's run).
  * Security implications: every view uses the access mixin; ids are looked up after the check; every change is one transaction (the unique name is decided by the database in a race); the quota and the dates are validated on the server (the script only helps); the delete of a LEG is possible only without a history and is checked again under a row lock; archive and delete are POST (CSRF, tested with `enforce_csrf_checks`); the search never reads the remark; the remark appears only on the detail page, which needs `view_leg`; the names of persons need `view_personrecord`; the history details hold no remark, no name; all texts are escaped by the template engine (the title attributes too), the script has no `innerHTML` and no inline style (CSP). Risk: the table of the metering points shows addresses and numbers to an account with `view_leg` and `view_meteringpoint` (the data of the metering point, as in D-079).
  * Open points for the owner: (1) may a LEG with members be archived (taken: yes, the members keep it); (2) the second sentence of the subtitle of the list; (3) the column of the action for a LEG of one circuit ("eigenes" shown, as the references do; the brief said not to); (4) "Konsument" or "Verbraucher" in the problem text; (5) the live check of the name while typing (the reference text says "steht unter dem Feld"; built as a check on saving); (6) several LEGs that cover only one circuit (taken: the first by name); (7) the date of the reading in the future is refused (not in the specification).
  * Sources: the owner's specification (the files named above); D-077, D-078, D-079; Django 6.1 documentation ("Translation" (`{% translate %}` and the percent sign, `pgettext`), "Forms" (`DecimalField`, `DateField`), "Model instance reference" (`select_for_update`), "Queries" (`iexact`, `Exists`, `OuterRef`)); `django/template/base.py` and `templatetags/i18n.py` read for the handling of "%" in `translate`.

    ## D-081: Phase 2: Block 5 "Persons" (Owner Order of 2026-10-16)

    * Date: 2026-10-17
    * Status: decided by the team lead and implemented (same authority as D-077). Not yet reviewed by the security reviewer; the human review of D-019 stays open.
    * Context: the specification folder `verwaltung/personen/` (`VORGABEN.md` with all "Nachträge", `UMSETZUNG.md`, `detail/VORGABEN.md`, the templates `referenz/liste-desktop`, `liste-handy` and `detail/referenz/person-detail-desktop`, `person-detail-handy`; the older `person-desktop` and `person-handy` are not implemented) and `LISTEN-STANDARD.md`. D-077 holds the names, the rights and the rules of the domain (point 5: persons are accounts; the exceptions to "the customer changes the data"), D-078 the list framework; this entry records what block 5 added or decided.
    * Decisions:
      1. **Pages and URLs** (`management/person_views.py`, `person_forms.py`, templates `management/persons/*.html`): `persons/` (list), `persons/<id>/` (detail; the id is the id of the account, so the existing links of the pages of the locations and metering points, `management:persons_detail`, resolve now), `persons/<id>/note/`, `bkw-customer-number/`, `shares/` (dialogs that are normal pages) and `address-suggestion/` (POST). The menu line is `MenuEntry("persons", …)` after the overview.
      2. **Rights.** List and detail: `view_personrecord`. The three dialogs: `view_personrecord` and `change_personrecord`. The buttons "Accept" and "Do not accept" of the address banner: `view_personrecord` and `correct_person_address` (the right to change is not enough, and the banner is not shown without it). The table of the metering points of a person, the column "Metering points" and the filter of the list need `view_meteringpoint` (the data of the metering points; without the right the list has no such column and ignores the filter), the links in the table `view_location`, `view_leg` and `view_meteringpoint`. An account without `view_personrecord` sees no person on any page (tested on the pages of the locations, metering points and LEGs, and the person routes answer 403).
      3. **Who is a person and the three words of the account** (`person_list.py`). A person is an account with a customer number (an unverified account has none). **Active** ("Aktiv", green) = the status `active` and not locked; **Blocked** ("Gesperrt", red) = the status `blocked` **or** an active account that the lockout of the login protection holds now; **Inactive** ("Inaktiv", grey) = the status `deactivated` (the archive: only with the filter "archived", never in "All"). The temporary lockout is what Axes holds: Axes stores only a keyed digest of the login address (`login_protection.axes_username`), so the page sums the failures of the cool-off period per digest (the rule of `AxesDatabaseHandler`, django-axes 8.3.1: attempts newer than the cool-off time, the sum at the failure limit of the project, 5 in 15 minutes) and compares it with the digests of the active persons; nothing is computed while no lockout exists. The status of an account cannot be changed by the staff here (not decided, not shown).
      4. **The list** (`LISTEN-STANDARD.md`, `lists.py`). Columns name, customer number, address, metering points, account, the link column; sort on every column (default the name, ascending; ties by name and customer number); the name is sorted by its bold text. **Name:** the company name in bold and, for a company, the contact person (the first and last name that D-032 keeps "optional" with a company name) in grey below it; any other person: first and last name. **Customer number** in the format `482 915`. **Address:** street and house number (the post office box where there is no street; `street_address_display`). **Metering points = count and word:** the metering points of a person are the ones that are not archived and have an assignment of the person that is valid now or starts later (`not_ended`: an assignment that ends in the future still counts; one that ended does not); the count is the number of different metering points; the word is "Aufnahme" if at least one of them has the status *registered* (no notification to the BKW yet) and "angemeldet" if every one is at least *notified*; no word and "0" without a metering point. **Search:** every word must match the first or last name, the company name, the street, the house number, the postal code, the town, the e-mail address or the second e-mail address (what the list or the detail page shows; a word of digits also matches the customer number); case and accents are ignored through the column collation; never the note, the BKW customer number, the IBAN or the phone number (the phone is on the detail page but is not named by the specification; it is left out). **Filters:** Status (All, Active, Blocked, archived) and Metering points (All, with metering point, without metering point). No warning triangle (the specification: no problems of persons yet). **Not built:** "Person anlegen" and "Anmeldung starten" (no specified dialog or effect), the button "Print" (the printing block, desktop only), "block" and "deactivate" (not decided). On a phone each person is a card: the bold name with the link icon, the contact person, the customer number and the account as a dot whose accessible name is the word (the template shows the dot without a word); address and metering points are left out (the template). The search field has the placeholder of the desktop template on both layouts (the phone template says "Name oder Kundennummer"; one text avoids a second template line and the longer text fits 390 px).
      5. **The detail page** (`person-detail-*`). Back link, the name (the company name for a company), "Kundennummer … · ● Status"; cards "Details" (salutation, company, first name, last name, "Second person" = the address addition of the account, D-036), "Contact" (e-mail, second e-mail, phone as the person writes it), "Billing address" (street, postal code and town, country), "Payment and customer numbers" (IBAN in blocks of four, paper invoice yes or no, customer number, optional BKW customer number), "Cooperative", "Note" (staff only) and the table. Desktop: two columns (left the first four cards, right cooperative and note) and the table over the full width; phone: one column in the order of the template (the metering points after the payment card; `data-order` and `display: contents`). **Read only:** the cards of the customer's data have no "Edit" button (customer master data is not changed by the staff, D-077); "Edit" exists on the card of the payment and customer numbers (it edits the BKW customer number only), on "Cooperative" and on "Note", and only with `change_personrecord`. A history card (the same table as the locations) appears only when entries exist. **The table of the metering points:** one row per assignment (a person with two not ended assignments of the same metering point sees two rows); columns location (street and house number), metering point (country and then a space after every four characters, `numbers.format_metering_point_number`; "–" for a number that is not known yet), direction (the badge of the other pages), LEG (valid now, else the next one), "Assigned" and "BKW registration". **"Assigned":** "ab DD.MM.YYYY" and, if the assignment is ended (also in the future, for example with a flying change), "bis DD.MM.YYYY" in a second line below. **"BKW registration":** "registriert" (yellow-brown) without a notification, "angemeldet am DD.MM.YYYY" (green) with the date of the notification, "bestätigt am DD.MM.YYYY" (green) with the date on which the confirmation was recorded (a terminated metering point shows the last step that it reached; the specification has no text for the termination, open point 3 of D-077). **Sorting:** every column on a desktop, first click ascending, second descending; the default is the number ascending (open point 8 of D-077 taken as the UMSETZUNG says); rows without a value (no number, no location, no LEG) come last also when descending; no sorting on a phone. The information buttons carry the texts of the template; the sentence about 30 days is not there (the Nachtrag). The hint "Zuordnungen ändern Sie auf der Seite des Messpunkts." stands under the table and in the card on the phone. Links added next to the values (the template has none; the standard asks for the link icon): the location, the metering point and the LEG, each only with the right to see that page.
      6. **The dialogs** (`DialogView`). *Note:* free text of at most 5000 characters; an empty text clears it. *BKW customer number:* optional, at most 30 characters, printable; blanks are collapsed. *Shares:* the number (from 1), the first day (required with shares) and the last day (optional: open); the end is not before the start; "ending" shares is entering the last day; no number removes the shares with their period ("Keine Anteile erfasst."). The model holds **one** period of shares (`PersonRecord.shares`, `shares_from`, `shares_to`); a second period replaces the first (open point 1). Everything is checked again in `persons.py`. The record `PersonRecord` is created only when a value is saved (opening a dialog or a page creates nothing; saving an empty value for a person without a record creates nothing). Every change is one transaction that locks the account and the record; a change that changes nothing writes no history. History events `person_note_changed`, `person_bkw_number_changed`, `person_shares_changed` name the event, the actor and the time and **never a value** (L-6; `history.record` refuses these keys).
      7. **The address correction** (`persons.py`; "wie beim Standort"). The page asks the address directory for the person only for an account with `correct_person_address`, on every call, and stores nothing for a page that only looks (a GET writes nothing). There is a suggestion only for an account that is not inactive, whose country is Switzerland, that has a Swiss postal code and a street, when the suggestion fits the columns of the account (street 150, house number 30, town 40 characters, a postal code of four digits from 1000: D-032, D-036) and when "Do not accept" was not chosen for the address as it is now (`PersonRecord.suggestion_dismissed_for`, the same memory as for the locations: it returns only when street, house number, postal code or town changes). The buttons carry a digest of the suggestion that was shown; the server asks the directory again under the row lock of the account and refuses (message "There is no suggestion to decide on.") if it is not the same. "Accept" writes street, house number, town, Swiss postal code (and clears the foreign postal code) with `save(update_fields=…)` like the profile of the customer, after `clean_fields` of exactly these fields; the controlled fields of the user model (status, e-mail, staff flags, customer number, retention obligation; D-034) are not touched and a test compares the account before and after. Both decisions go to the history of the person with the actor and the time ("Adresskorrektur übernommen" / "nicht übernommen"; no address in the entry). **No security log entry:** the project does not log the customer's own profile changes either, and the security log is for security events (D-018); the history of the person is the record. The customer is **not told** about the correction (the specification says nothing; open point 2).
      8. **Neutral texts that the specification does not give and that the code invents** (to be confirmed by the owner; the others are 1:1 from the templates): "No persons found.", "Edit note", "Edit BKW customer number", "Edit shares", "Number of shares", "Without a number of shares the shares are removed.", "The end must not be before the start.", "This value is not valid.", "The shares could not be saved.", "No metering points assigned.", the labels "Shares" and "Period" and the display of a period ("from … to …"), the history events "Note changed", "BKW customer number changed", "Shares changed", the card "History" (the existing words), the filter words "with metering point" and "without metering point" (from the template), the screen reader names of the dots.
      9. **Changes to existing code and tests:** the test `test_components` expected that the link to a person does not exist yet (now it resolves); `AccessTests` of the route walk declares the database `addresses` (the page of a person asks the directory when the account may correct). The shared class `.btn-xs` has no effect on the pages of the earlier blocks: `.register-clear .btn` (48 px, 16 px) is more specific, so the small buttons of the cards (36 px, 14 px in the templates) show as 44 px; the person pages have their own, scoped rule `.register-clear .mgmt-person .btn.btn-xs`; the other pages are unchanged (to be decided by the designer: one general fix would change them all).
    * Verified facts: the layout, spacing, wrapping and the phone views were compared in the browser at 1440 and 390 px against `liste-desktop`, `liste-handy`, `person-detail-desktop` and `person-detail-handy` with synthetic local data that is **not** in the repository (a company with a contact person, a blocked account, a deactivated one, shares, a note, an address with a directory suggestion from the synthetic address directory of `docs/STARTUP.md`, section 10): no horizontal overflow on the list, the detail page and a dialog, on either width; the information bubbles of the last columns open to the left and the one of the BKW column opens upwards without scrollbars; after the check the order of the cards on a phone, the stretch of the cards and the one-row head of a card were corrected. The lockout rule was read in `axes/attempts.py` and `axes/handlers/database.py` (django-axes 8.3.1 as installed). `AccessAttempt` rows with the digest of an account were created in a test, and the account showed as blocked within the cool-off period and at the limit only.
    * Tests: `community/tests/test_persons.py` (18 tests, 0.8 s: the states and the lockout rule, the status filter, the count and the word, the search and what it never reads, every sort, the metering points of a person, the note, the BKW number and the shares with their refusals, the address correction against a real small directory file) and `management/tests/test_person_pages.py` (16 tests, 3.2 s: the list, filters, search and sort, the list without the right to see metering points, the detail page, its table and the sorting, a company, the 404 for an account without a customer number, the rights that shape the page, the dialogs with their refusals and hostile `next` values, a view-only account that cannot change anything, the banner only with the right to correct, accept and not accept, a stale decision, CSRF, an account without `view_personrecord` sees no person anywhere, the links of the other pages). Run of 2026-10-17: `.\scripts\test.ps1 -Changed` (areas docs, community, management, static, templates, translations) 1116 tests in 105.7 s, OK; `-Area testing,docs` 37 tests OK (the full suite was not run: no core file changed).
    * Security implications: every view uses the access mixin and the ids are looked up after the check; the person pages need `view_personrecord`, the changes `change_personrecord`, the address correction `correct_person_address` (tested: view-only cannot change, change is not enough for the correction); all POSTs are CSRF protected (tested with `enforce_csrf_checks`); `next` is only a path of this site; the IBAN, the note and the BKW customer number appear only on the detail page, never in the search, a log line, the history or a security event; the address of a customer is changed only through the directory suggestion, by the fields of the profile, under a lock, with a digest of the suggestion that was shown; the note is shown with escaping and line breaks only. Risks: a staff user with `view_personrecord` sees all customer data including the IBAN (the purpose of the right); the address of a customer changes without the customer being told (open point 2); the lockout state is derived from Axes digests (a changed secret of the keyed digest would hide a lockout from the list but not from the login).
    * Open points for the owner: (1) the shares: one period per person, or several (history of periods)? (taken: one); (2) tell the customer about an accepted address correction? (taken: no); (3) "Person anlegen" and "Anmeldung starten": effect and dialog (open point 1 of D-077); (4) the phone placeholder "Name oder Kundennummer" of the template versus the desktop text on both (taken: one text); (5) the invented texts of point 8; (6) the shared `.btn-xs` finding of point 9; (7) whether a person problem exists (a triangle in the list needs a rule in `LISTEN-STANDARD.md`, section 12).
    * Sources: the owner's specification (the files named above); D-077, D-078, D-079, D-016, D-032, D-034, D-036; django-axes 8.3.1 source as installed; Django 6.1 documentation ("Models" `Q`, `Exists`, `Subquery`, `Cast`, "Model instance reference" `clean_fields` and `save(update_fields)`, `select_for_update`, "Translation" `pgettext`).

  ## D-082: Phase 2, Block 6 "Overview and Printing": the Overview With Its Problem Rows, the PDF of the Lists, the Menu Link to the Email Texts and the Height of the Small Buttons (2026-10-17)

  * Date: 2026-10-17
  * Status: decided by the team lead and implemented (same authority as D-077). Not yet reviewed by the security reviewer; the human review of D-019 stays open.
  * Context: the specification folder `verwaltung/uebersicht/` (`VORGABEN.md`, `UMSETZUNG.md`, `referenz/uebersicht-desktop`, `-handy`), `LISTEN-STANDARD.md` sections 9, 10 and 12 (problems, printing, the rule that the printing is a desktop function only), the print dialog `legs/referenz/liste-drucken-desktop` and the frame "Liste drucken" of `leg-dialoge-handy` (texts only), and the print button notes of every list. D-077 holds the names, rights and the access rule, D-078 the list framework; D-083 the dependency and the font of the PDF.
  * Decisions:
    1. **The overview** (`views.OverviewView`, `overview.html`). Title "Übersicht" with "Verwaltung" above; the button "Probleme filtern (n)" with the border of the warning colour at the top right (full width on a phone) **only while n > 0**; below it a card with one row for each list with a problem: the name of the list (bold), "N … mit Problem" (grey; with the singular and the plural), the count behind the warning triangle and the link icon to the list **with the problem filter on** (`?problems=1`). Lists without a problem do not appear; the persons never do (no problem is defined for them, section 12). Under the card the hint of the reference ("Der Knopf "Probleme filtern" erscheint nur, wenn eine Liste ein Problem hat. Er zeigt in jeder Liste nur die Einträge mit Problem."; `VORGABEN.md`: "Hinweistext unter der Liste"). Without a problem: no button, no hint, one calm sentence "Keine Probleme." (**invented, the specification has no text**, open point 1). **The counts** come from `community.problems.problem_counts(only=...)` and only for the lists the account has the view right for: the count of a list the account may not see is not computed, shown or summed (tested: an account with only `view_leg` sees only the LEG row and its own sum; an account with only the settings or the persons sees "Keine Probleme."). n = the sum of the shown counts, which equals the buttons of the lists themselves (tested). **"Probleme filtern" with several affected lists** goes to the **first affected list the account may see in the order of the menu** (locations, LEGs, metering points) with the filter on; from there each list has its own switch; the rows of the card open each its own list. The order of the rows is the order of the menu (the reference shows "LEGs" before "Messpunkte", which is the same order).
    2. **Printing** (`management/printing/`, D-083 for the package). Every overview list (persons, locations, LEGs, metering points) has a button "Drucken" in the head, **left of the create button** (for the persons, who have none, alone), `mgmt-desktop-only` (display none up to 760 px; no print button in any phone layout, tested at the markup level and checked at 390 px in the browser). It opens `management:<area>_print`, **the dialog "Liste drucken"**: a normal page that works without JavaScript (a centred card over the dimmed page on a desktop, the whole view on a phone, like every dialog of the area; "Abbrechen" is a link back to the list with its state). The three options of the template: "Nur die Liste", "Liste und Detailseiten aller angezeigten Einträge", "Liste und Detailseiten ausgewählter Einträge" (one checkbox per entry, shown by CSS `:has()` while the third option is chosen, always shown without `:has()` support; the hint of the template under them), the lead "Die Liste wird so gedruckt, wie sie gefiltert und sortiert ist. Es werden N Einträge gedruckt." (N = the entries of the list, as in the template with its three entries and two ticks; the singular is invented: "Es wird 1 Eintrag gedruckt."), "Abbrechen" and "PDF erstellen". The form posts to the address of the dialog; the answer is the PDF as an attachment, so the dialog stays in the browser.
    3. **What "all shown entries" means.** All entries of the list as the address filters and sorts it, **not only the page** (the template says "Es werden 3 Einträge gedruckt" next to a list of three; a printout of 50 of 800 entries is not what "angezeigt" means to the person who asks for the list). The bounds make it safe (point 6).
    4. **The state of the list is the address, parsed again on the server.** The dialog URL carries `q`, the filters, `sort`, `dir` and `problems` (`ListState.url_to`, built from the validated state, never a page number); both the dialog and the PDF call the `build_list` function of the view module of the area (the same function as the page, extracted from the page views without a change of their behaviour: `leg_views`, `person_views`, `location_views`, `metering_point_views`), so the search, the filters, the problem switch and the sorting are exactly the ones of the page, and an unknown value is the default there as well (tested: `sort=password&display=zzz&evil=1`). The form of the dialog cannot widen the list: a posted `q`, `display` or `problems` is ignored (tested).
    5. **The PDF** (A4 portrait, the bundled font of D-083). Page 1: the title ("Verwaltung · LEGs" and the like), then the lines Datum, Suche (only if set), the filters that are set (the label of the filter and the text of the option, "Filter: Nur Probleme" for the problem switch; the quick filter of the metering points is "Schnellfilter" with the label of the step without its count), "Sortierung: <column>, aufsteigend/absteigend"; then the table **with the columns of the list**, without the link icon and **without the triangle: the text of the problem is text in the cell** (the location: under the address; the LEG: under the quota and under the participants, as the triangles stand; the metering point: under the number; "(neu)" behind a location that a customer created); the head repeats on every page; the page number and the title are in the foot. **The detail pages follow the list in the order of the list, each on a new page**: the title, the subtitle and the banner of the problems as the page shows them, then its cards as lines of label and value (the person: details, contact, billing address, payment and customer numbers, cooperative, note, and the table of the metering points; the location: address, grid, remark, the table of its metering points, the history of the assignments; the LEG: quota, master data, composition, the table of its metering points; the metering point: its data, status, LEG, person). **Not printed:** the change history ("Verlauf") of the pages, the buttons, the hints of the information bubbles, and the state of the last column of the LEG table ("eigenes", the recommendation of another LEG: an action help, not a datum; open point 2). The phone has no print, so the layout of the PDF is the desktop's.
    6. **Rights and bounds.** The right is the right to view the list (the view checks it; the dialog and the PDF are one view, GET and POST), as D-077 says for "print". The detail pages print only what the page shows that account: the names of persons need `view_personrecord` (without it "–" and no history of the assignments), the table of the metering points of a LEG or a person needs `view_meteringpoint`, the IBAN, the note and the BKW customer number are in the PDF of the persons only, remarks and notes where the page shows them (tested with an account that has no right to see persons or metering points). **Bounds** (constants in `printing/areas.py`, tested by patching them): 1000 entries per printout (`MAX_LIST_ENTRIES`), 100 detail pages (`MAX_DETAIL_PAGES`; "all" is disabled in the dialog above it, "selected" is refused above it) and 20000 rows of tables of the whole printout (`MAX_ROWS`); beyond them the dialog says so ("Die Liste ist zu lang zum Drucken …", "Es können höchstens 100 Detailseiten auf einmal gedruckt werden …", "Der Ausdruck ist zu gross …"; **invented texts**, open point 1) and makes nothing; a text cell is cut at 300 characters and a line of a detail page at 1000 (an unsplittable table row higher than the page cannot be drawn). 1000 list rows take about a second (measured: 1500 synthetic rows, 0.97 s, 39 pages); 51 detail pages of locations of the local database took 0.94 s, so 100 detail pages are expected to take about two seconds (extrapolated, not measured).
    7. **The selection is never trusted.** The ids of the checkboxes are matched against the entries of the list that the server computed; an id that is not in it (archived, filtered out, foreign, text, negative, empty) is ignored; nothing selected is "Wählen Sie mindestens einen Eintrag aus."; an unknown scope or more than 2000 ids is a 400 (`SuspiciousOperation`; Django's own limit of 1000 form fields, `DATA_UPLOAD_MAX_NUMBER_FIELDS`, refuses a form with about a thousand ticked boxes with a 400 as well, which is beyond the bound of 100 detail pages anyway); the same id twice is one page (tested with forged ids).
    8. **Everything typed is text** (D-083 explains the markup of ReportLab): `pdf.markup` is the only way into a paragraph (control characters removed, length cut, `& < >` escaped, a line break becomes `<br/>`); tested with markup, an image address, a font tag and entities in names, remarks, notes and the search, with ReportLab's own parser (`Paragraph.getPlainText()` returns the typed text) and by rendering (no `/URI` and no link in the file; the typed address is printed as text). A very long name without a space (200 letters) breaks inside the cell and nothing is cut off (checked in the rendered image); umlauts, é, è, ç, ß, ő, ń, ć, ł, the arrow and the dot render (checked in the rendered image and by the glyph table of both faces).
    9. **The response** is made in memory (no temporary file), `Content-Type: application/pdf`, `Content-Disposition: attachment; filename="legs.pdf"` (fixed per area: `persons.pdf`, `locations.pdf`, `legs.pdf`, `metering-points.pdf`; no name from the data), `Cache-Control: no-store, private` (plus the `never_cache` of the access mixin and the middleware of the area); the metadata of the PDF holds the title only (no author, no subject). A failure of the drawing is logged with the kind of the error only (never a text of the document) and the person sees "Das PDF konnte nicht erstellt werden." (invented text).
    10. **The menu.** The group "Verwaltung" is the six entries in the order of the specification (overview, persons, locations, LEGs, metering points, settings; the three phone navigation entries are the frame's) and, **as the last entry, "Mailtexte"**: the link to the existing page of the email texts in the system administration (`admin:accounts_emailtext_changelist`; no new page, no new function) for an account that holds `view_emailtext` or `change_emailtext`; the administration checks the right again. It is the only entry that leaves the area; it is never the active entry. The "Messages" page of the specification is not built.
    11. **The height of the small buttons** (the finding of block 5): the rule `.register-clear .btn` (48 px) was more specific than `.btn-xs`, so the small buttons of all pages that were not the person pages were 44 to 48 px high instead of 36 px. The person-scoped rule was replaced by `.register-clear .btn.btn-xs` (36 px, 14 px text; only the pages of the area use `.btn-xs`). Verified in the browser on the pages of the locations, LEGs, persons and metering points (36 px everywhere) and in a test of the stylesheet.
    12. **Cross-links** (walked in the browser as the system administrator, desktop and phone): overview → lists with the filter on → details (the link icons) → related details (location ↔ metering point ↔ LEG ↔ person, each only with the right to view the target) → back links (to the list with its state); the lists → "Drucken" → the dialog → "Abbrechen" back to the list. No link was missing; the phone shows the same without "Drucken".
  * Verified facts: ReportLab and the font (D-083); the layout of the overview, the dialog (both options, 1440 and 390 px) and the PDFs (all four lists, selected and all detail pages) compared in the browser (Edge through Playwright, local throw-away database) with the templates and rendered to images (pypdfium2 in a temporary environment outside the project); the counts of the overview equal the buttons of the lists; the print button is in no phone markup; Django 6.1 `never_cache`, `patch_cache_control(no_store=True)`.
  * Tests: `management/tests/test_overview_and_printing.py` (28 tests, about 5 s): the overview rows, sums and counts per right and without problems, the menu order and the link to the email texts, the stylesheet rule; the lines above the list, the columns and the problem texts, the order and the choice of the detail pages, what an account without the rights does not get; the dialog and the state of the address, the button, the PDF of every list (type, header, magic number, page count), the forged selection, the bounds, a failed drawing, the access of the dialog; the renderer with hostile texts, a very long name, all alphabets and 1200 rows. The four list tests that said "no button Drucken" now say that it is there. The route walk of `test_access.py` covers the four new routes (anonymous, no staff, no right, no second factor, all rights). **The dependency change widened the runner to the full suite, run once: 2528 tests, OK (2 skipped), 249 s** (the management and community areas alone: 378 tests, 35 s before the new module).
  * Not verified: the printed look on paper and in other PDF viewers (checked with PDFium only); a printout of 100 detail pages and of 1000 list rows with real data (measured with synthetic rows only); the German texts of the invented messages by the owner.
  * Open points for the owner: (1) the invented texts: "Keine Probleme.", "Es wird 1 Eintrag gedruckt.", the three refusals, "Wählen Sie mindestens einen Eintrag aus.", "Das PDF konnte nicht erstellt werden.", the lines "Sortierung", "aufsteigend", "absteigend", "Filter", "Nur Probleme", "Schnellfilter" and the step labels without a count of the PDF, "Mailtexte" (the reference menu has it); (2) the content of the detail pages in the PDF: which cards, the history ("Verlauf") and the state of the last column of the LEG table are left out; (3) "all shown entries" = the whole filtered list, not the page; (4) the bounds 1000 / 100 / 20000; (5) the phone dialog is the same dialog as on a desktop (the frame "Liste drucken" of the phone template has its own title bar; no print function exists on a phone); (6) "Mailtexte" opens the system administration, which has its own look; the page "Mailtexte" of the specification (placeholder list, preview) is not built.
  * Risks: a printout holds personal data (names, addresses, IBAN) in a file that leaves the application; the right to view the list is the right to print it (the owner's rule), so a role that may view persons may export them; the bounds limit the size, not the number of printouts (staff only; no rate limit by the decision of D-077); the font is bundled binary data (D-083).
  * Sources: the owner's specification (the files named above); D-077, D-078; Django 6.1 documentation ("Class-based views", "Cache decorators", `patch_cache_control`, "Translation" `ngettext`); ReportLab 5.0.1 as installed (`Paragraph`, `Table`, `BaseDocTemplate`, `pdfmetrics`, `TTFont`); the code and tests of this change.

  ## D-083: ReportLab, the Bundled Font DejaVu Sans Condensed and the Minimum Version for the PDF Printing (2026-10-17)

  * Date: 2026-10-17
  * Status: decided by the team lead (a new dependency and a bundled binary file; the owner asked for an established, maintained package: "ReportLab, BSD licence, if verified"). Not yet reviewed by the security reviewer.
  * Context: D-082 prints lists as PDF. D-015 and D-026 govern minimum versions and the choice of packages: an established, maintained package; the oldest version that is technically possible and still receives security fixes; no package with known vulnerabilities; the licence is recorded in `docs/THIRD_PARTY_LICENSES.md`.
  * Decisions:
    1. **Package: ReportLab** (`reportlab`, the open source toolkit of ReportLab Inc.; BSD 3-clause licence). Alternatives considered: `fpdf2` (licence LGPL-3.0-only, PyPI metadata of 2026-10-17: a licence that needs more explanation next to the GPL-2.0 `mysqlclient` and the BSD project) and WeasyPrint (BSD classifier, 70.0; it renders HTML and CSS, and its own documentation, "Security", read 2026-10-17, warns that untrusted input can cause long renderings, infinite loops and file or network access and asks for process limits: a larger attack surface than drawing a table); ReportLab is a pure Python wheel (`py3-none-any`, checked in the installed `WHEEL` file) that needs Pillow and `charset-normalizer` (the latter was already installed for `requests`). **Declared minimum `reportlab>=5.0.1`** (pyproject.toml) and the pin `reportlab==5.0.1` in `requirements/minimum-constraints.txt` (`scripts/check_minimum_constraints.py` agrees). Reason for choosing the newest release as the minimum (the rule of D-026, "Minimum patch levels are the current releases at the time of the decision"): 5.0.1 (2026-08-20) is the current release of the line that the vendor maintains; the project has no support policy that names an older maintained line; all four known advisories (CVE-2019-17626 and CVE-2019-19450, code execution through `<span color=...>` and the paragraph parser; CVE-2020-28463, server-side request forgery through `<img>` in paragraphs; CVE-2023-33733, code execution through `rl_safe_eval`) are fixed in 3.6.13 or earlier, so 5.0.1 has no known vulnerability (OSV database, read on 2026-10-17); it supports Python 3.9 to 3.14 (PyPI classifiers 3.10 to 3.14), which covers the declared Python 3.12 and the used 3.14. `pillow>=9.0.0` is required by ReportLab and installed with it (the newest compatible release at installation, D-015); LEG-Platform draws no image and uses no Pillow function.
    2. **Use only the safe subset.** ReportLab's paragraphs read an XML-like markup and its history shows that this parser is the attack surface (the four advisories above). The rule (`printing/pdf.py`): nothing a person typed or the database holds is ever given to a paragraph as markup; every text goes through `markup()` (escaped, control characters removed, length cut); no `<img>`, no `Image`, no link and no use of `rl_safe_eval` or of colour strings from data are made; the markup that the code itself builds is only `<br/>`. A test pins the behaviour with hostile texts.
    3. **Font: DejaVu Sans Condensed (Regular and Bold) is bundled** in `leg_platform/management/printing/fonts/` with its licence. Reason: ReportLab's built-in fonts (Helvetica and the like) are the 14 PDF base fonts with WinAnsi encoding: no č, ć, ő, ń, ł, ś, and no Cyrillic or Greek letter that a customer name may contain in Switzerland; ReportLab also ships Bitstream Vera (it renders ä ö ü é è ç ß č ć đ š ž ł but **not** ő ű ń ś ź ż, checked with the glyph table on 2026-10-17); DejaVu Sans Condensed (the Vera family extended) has all of them (checked: ä ö ü Ä Ö Ü é è à ç ß ñ ć č đ š ž ł ő ű ń ś ź ż ğ ı İ ş Ğ € – · → ✓ ● and Greek and Cyrillic letters, in both faces); the condensed width lets 33-character metering point numbers fit a column of an A4 page. The Source Sans 3 file of the pages (`static/vendor/fonts/SourceSans3.ttf`) is a **variable** font; ReportLab draws only its default instance and has no bold, so it is not used. Source and hashes of the files are in `docs/THIRD_PARTY_LICENSES.md` ("Bundled Assets"): DejaVu fonts 2.37 from the official release archive of https://github.com/dejavu-fonts/dejavu-fonts, unmodified; licence: Bitstream Vera Fonts Copyright with the public domain changes of DejaVu (`LICENSE-DejaVu.txt` next to the files; bundling with a larger software package is allowed, "no copy … may be sold by itself"). A character that is in no face is drawn as the notdef box of the font (no crash); that covers the Latin, Greek and Cyrillic alphabets but not Arabic, Hebrew or Asian scripts (open point).
    3a. The fonts are read by ReportLab from the package directory with `TTFont`; no static file, never served (the project does not use `collectstatic` for them).
    4. **Installation notes.** No system package and no font installation are needed: `pip install` brings ReportLab (pure Python) and Pillow (a binary wheel; the installed 13.0.0 is `cp314-win_amd64`; wheels for the other platforms were not checked one by one). The fonts are part of the source tree; an installation that copies only Python files must copy `leg_platform/management/printing/fonts/` too (the same applies to templates and static files). `README.md` and the installation documents need no new step.
    5. **Test and verification without a PDF reader dependency.** pypdf is not a dependency and is not added: the tests assert the document model (`areas`: texts only), the magic number, the headers and the page count (the page objects of a ReportLab file are not compressed), and the escaping with ReportLab's own `Paragraph.getPlainText()`. The rendered pages were read back with PDFium (pypdfium2, Apache-2.0/BSD) in a temporary virtual environment outside the project, for the review only.
  * Verified facts (2026-10-17): https://pypi.org/pypi/reportlab/json (5.0.1, 2026-08-20, `requires_python <4,>=3.9`, classifiers up to 3.14, `Requires-Dist: pillow>=9.0.0, charset-normalizer`, no yanked release in the last 14); OSV `https://api.osv.dev/v1/query` for `reportlab` (PyPI): four advisories, the highest fixed version 3.6.13; installed `reportlab-5.0.1.dist-info/licenses/LICENSE` (3-clause BSD, ReportLab Inc.), Pillow 13.0.0 `License-Expression: MIT-CMU`; the glyph tables of `Vera.ttf`, `SourceSans3.ttf` and both DejaVu faces read with ReportLab's `TTFont`; a render of 1500 rows in 0.97 s.
  * Open points: (1) the minimum version could be lowered to an older line that the vendor still maintains if the owner knows one (the vendor names no support policy); (2) scripts that DejaVu does not cover (Arabic, Hebrew, Asian) print as boxes; (3) Pillow carries many advisories of its own over time; it is installed as the newest release (D-015) and is not used by the project, which the dependency check of a release should keep in view.
  * Sources: https://pypi.org/pypi/reportlab/json, https://api.osv.dev/v1/query (read 2026-10-17), https://github.com/dejavu-fonts/dejavu-fonts/releases/tag/version_2_37, the installed packages' metadata and licence files, ReportLab 5.0.1 source as installed (`pdfbase/ttfonts.py`, `platypus/paragraph.py`), D-013, D-015, D-026.

  ## D-084: Review Round of D-082 (Security and Design Findings) (2026-10-17)

  * Date: 2026-10-17
  * Status: decided by the team lead on the reports of the security reviewer and of the designer about HEAD 7b3e5a2; implemented by the developer. Not yet re-reviewed.
  * Context: D-077 (names, rights, access rule), D-078 (list framework), D-081 (merge of locations), D-082 (overview and printing), D-083 (ReportLab, fonts).
  * Decisions (security):
    1. **M-3, merge of locations** (`community/locations.py`, `management/location_views.py`, `location_forms.py`). The merge is done only if the user confirmed it **and** holds the delete right of locations (`confirmed = confirm_merge == "1" and has_perm(DELETE)`); without the right the dialog shows the ordinary "address in use" error, it does not answer 403. The confirmation is **bound to the target that was shown**: the merge dialog carries a hidden `merge_target`, and `update_address(..., merge, merge_target_id)` compares it with the target found under the lock; another id raises `MergeNeeded` again (the dialog is shown anew). An **archived target is refused** (`MergeRefused`, form error). The rows (the location, the target) are locked in ascending id order. For **every metering point moved** a history entry is written (new event `METERING_POINT_LOCATION_CHANGED`, "Location changed", with the old and the new address in the detail text, as for other changes of the point).
    2. **L-3.** `leg_switch.switch_leg` locks the metering points in ascending pk order (no deadlock with a second switch or a merge).
    3. **M-4, gate by the view rights of the other areas.** An account sees the data of an area only with that area's view right: `summaries.duplicate_summary(existing, user)` and `CheckNumberView` return direction, status and the link only with `view_meteringpoint`, the address only with `view_location`, the LEG name only with `view_leg` (otherwise only "the number exists"; `lookup.js` and `_duplicate.html` show no link line when the address is empty). In the lists the **search branches, filters, sorts and columns** that expose another area are available only with that right (`see_legs`, `see_locations`, `see_points`, `see_persons` in `apply_search`, `details`, `list_columns` and `occupancy_options`): the location list needs `view_personrecord` for the occupied and free filters, the occupancy sort and the person counts, and `view_leg` for the LEG name; the metering point list needs `view_location` for the location column and `view_leg` for the LEG column, filter and sort (a sort or filter by a field the user may not see is ignored like an unknown value); the location suggestions never search LEG names. The same gating is applied in the print mirror (`printing/areas.py`), so the PDF holds nothing the page does not show. The metering point list's phone card shows only the triangle, not "● keine LEG" (LISTEN-STANDARD 11, design finding 19).
    4. **M-2 (part), logging of prints.** New security event `LIST_PRINTED` ("List printed"; `accounts` migration 0028; long retention class like the other events about personal data; message in `security_log.MESSAGES`): for every PDF the account, the list key, the scope, the number of entries and of detail pages are recorded; **no names, no search text, no ids**. It is written after the PDF was rendered successfully.
    5. **L-1.** `REMARK_MAX_LENGTH = 5000` (model fields of the remarks of location, metering point and LEG, and of the staff note of a person record; `community` migration 0003) and `max_length` on the form fields. `per=all` is capped at `lists.MAX_ALL_ROWS = 5000`: above it the list falls back to the default page size and shows a notice (`Page.notice`, "refusal style", `_list_foot.html`). The printing keeps its own bounds (D-082).
    6. **L-2.** Every place that read a number from the query string with `int()` now checks `isascii() and isdigit()` (and a length) first: `?circuit=²` was a 500 before; `_house_key` of the LEG and person lists uses `isascii()`.
    7. **L-4.** The digests of locked accounts are computed only for the rows of the current page (`person_list.locked_account_ids(now, only=...)`, `PersonList.locked_for(persons)`); the full scan stays only where the status filter "active/blocked" or the sort "account" needs it.
    8. **L-6.** The end and delete forms of an assignment carry the assignment id (hidden field, `hidden_values` in `dialog.html`) and compare it with the one in the URL; a mismatch is refused.
    9. **L-8.** The note in the PDF is cut at the stored maximum (`pdf.MAX_FACT_CHARS = 5000`); long values (above 400 characters) are a label and a paragraph that may split across pages. Django's `DATA_UPLOAD_MAX_NUMBER_FIELDS` (1000) is a setting of the whole project and cannot be raised per view (Django 6.1 documentation, "Settings"), so **the selection of entries is limited**: the dialog shows checkboxes only up to `areas.MAX_SELECTABLE = 300` entries; with more, the option "selected entries" is disabled with a hint (narrow the list by a filter), and the server accepts at most 300 posted ids and ignores the rest. A print selection can never exceed the field limit (no 400).
    10. **L-9.** `pyproject.toml` has `[tool.setuptools.package-data]` for `leg_platform.management.printing` (`fonts/*.ttf`, `fonts/*.txt`); a built wheel was checked to contain the DejaVu files and the licence text. Note: templates and static files are **not** part of the wheel (as before); the installation is from the source tree (D-083, decision 4).
  * Decisions (design, numbers of the designer's report):
    11. Focus ring (1, 20): a 3 px accent outline on all buttons, the icon buttons, the sort button, the caret, the info button and the column grips of the area. Sort button 44 px high (21).
    12. Tokens (2, 18): `--ok-text` (#1d6b40 light, #5dbb86 dark; at least 4.5:1 on the page, the card and the chip tint) for "Ausreichend", "angemeldet am", the feed-in chip and the like; the dots keep `--ok`. `--warn-icon` (#b45309 light, #f5a623 dark, the colours of the reference) for the fill of the warning triangle only; `--warn` stays for text.
    13. Phone location detail (3): goto icons beside the LEG, the person and the metering point in the cards. Location detail (4): the table of metering points is a fixed table with the column grips and a 56 px link column to the metering point; the member table of the LEG detail has the same link column.
    14. Phone header (5): the buttons of the heads wrap instead of overflowing. Overview left-aligned (`mgmt-wide`), settings left-aligned with a maximum of 900 px (6). Resize grip on the last data column of persons, locations and LEGs (7).
    15. Settings (9): validation on leaving a field (`settings-validate.js`, `settings_check` view, `data-validate-url`; the same server texts, shown under the field; the form is validated on the server again on every save). Person detail (11): the first card is "Angaben" (own msgid with context "person card", also in the PDF). Empty history tables of the location and the metering point show one row "Noch keine Einträge." (12). The goto links have an accessible name with the subject (`aria-label` = a plain text name, escaped by the template; 13). A skip link "Zum Inhalt springen" is the first element of the frame (all pages after login) and the target is `<main id="main-content" tabindex="-1">` (14). The active menu item has `var(--warm)` as background (17). The location field of the metering point dialog is wider than the LEG field on a desktop, its hint set smaller and the pair stacks on a phone, so that the placeholder is read to the end (16).
    16. **Narrow desktops, 761 to 1279 px (10).** The tables of the lists are `table-layout: fixed; width: 100%` with the proportional widths; `table-fit.js` scales the widths to the container (floor 60 % of the preset and 90 px; columns marked `data-keep`, the number columns, are never narrower than their text); if they still do not fit, **the card scrolls horizontally, not the page** (`is-overflowing`). Without JavaScript the card scrolls (CSS only). The information bubbles open to the left below 1280 px. Checked at 1440, 1280, 1024, 768 and 390 px in the browser: no horizontal scroll of any page of the area.
  * Not fixed or only documented (open points for the owner and the team lead):
    1. **M-1, release blocker (owner): the legal basis of the retention flag** (D-016) is not settled; the person-assign dialog names no consequence of it because the specification text has none; the text of the dialog is left as it is.
    2. **IBAN right (M-2, owner decision pending):** the IBAN is printed and shown with `view_personrecord` like the other personal data; whether it needs its own right is not decided, so it is not split.
    3. **L-5, L-7, L-10** are documented only (no code change ordered).
    4. **Detail pages are not gated by the view rights of other areas**: the location and LEG names on the page of a metering point, the member tables and the person and LEG name rows of the details show data of the neighbouring area to everyone who may open the page. The lists, the duplicate hint and the print are gated (decision 3). Owner question: gate the details too?
    5. **Design finding 8 (owner question):** with several lists affected, "Probleme filtern" opens the first affected list in menu order (D-082); a page with all the affected lists would be the alternative.
    6. Design finding 15 was skipped on order.
    7. The selection limit (300 entries per dialog) and `MAX_ALL_ROWS` (5000) are decided by the developer; the owner may set other limits.
  * Verified facts: the new tests in `management/tests/test_review_round.py` (duplicate notice and list gating by view rights, merge page bound to the target, remark maximum, `per=all` cap, `circuit=²`, lockout lookup per page, print log event without content, selection over the limit, 5000-character note, fonts in the package data, skip link, tokens, goto names, empty history hints), the merge rules (archived target, delete right, history entries, target binding) in `community/tests/test_locations.py` and the updated tests of the earlier blocks; the pages in the browser (Edge through Playwright, own server and local database, 1440, 1280, 1024, 768 and 390 px; skip link order, focus rings, sort height, grips, goto links, validation on blur, triangle and green colours, placeholder width).
  * Risks: more role-dependent output in the lists (a test per gate is the safeguard); the width script `table-fit.js` depends on JavaScript for the exact fitting (the CSS alone keeps the page from scrolling); the merge now changes several rows in one transaction.
  * Sources: the reports of the security reviewer and of the designer; Django 6.1 documentation (Settings `DATA_UPLOAD_MAX_NUMBER_FIELDS`, `select_for_update`, models validators, migrations); `LISTEN-STANDARD.md` section 11; the code and tests of this change.
